# JJ Simon: Fair Pricing Theory, risk model and multi-account prop-firm operation

A replication dossier. The purpose is constructive: to record what JJ Simon
says he does, in his own words, precisely enough to rebuild and test it, and
to work out what makes it work (see `docs/MECHANISM.md`) and how to grow into
it from a small bankroll (`docs/BOOTSTRAP.md`). Everything below is from
public material: his own videos and landing pages (marked **JJ**), podcast
appearances, and third-party codifications and backtests (marked **3rd**).
Each section carries blocks headed "From the primary corpus" built from
3,390 machine-verified transcript quotes (cited by video id, approximate
time and transcript line). Where his statements changed over time, both
versions are shown with dates so the current rule can be chosen. Nothing
here is affiliated with or endorsed by JJ Simon. Section 10 tracks the open
questions and the places where statements conflict, as a to-do list for
the replication rather than a scorecard.

Evidence files: `docs/research/EVIDENCE.md` (merged, deduplicated facts
with URLs), `docs/research/*.md` (per-angle reports), `docs/research/sources.csv`.

### From the primary corpus: his own account of his path, age, degree, start date, and what he offers as proof

**1. The path in his own words**

*1.1 First contact, age 14-15, Topstep.* The earliest anchor is a $50 Topstep evaluation at 14: ("I first found out about trading when I was 14 years old. I saw that Topstep was giving you money to trade with if you spent $50 in one of their evaluations." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:08, L32) [eval_math]; the same number in September 2026, with the cadence: ("technically first found out about trading when I was 14, and I did it until I was 19. But by did it, I mean off and on, randomly trying one top step eval once a year for $50 and losing it." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~7:07, L232). He sized as if the eval were everything he had: ("basically I would buy one EVO, $50 one from Topstep and I would treat it like my entire net worth was that Eval" — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~5:52, L241) [eval_math]. The route in was a YouTube trader ("Basically, I think I was 14 or 15 when I saw TJR on YouTube." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~5:42, L235); the serious look came in 11th grade (1CEAgRUNZ4c L238), and he dates his first platform at about four years before April 2026: ("They've essentially changed my life. It was obviously the first platform that I ever encountered like 4 years ago or something." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~19:17, L672). His parents did not fund a second attempt ("My parents wouldn't give me any money because I lost the first evaluation." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:32, L47), so he worked: ("I became a math tutor at Kumon Math. I worked there for about $15 an hour for just a few weeks, so I was able to get enough money to buy Topstep evaluations." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:37, L50); ("So, I decided that I was going to attempt two more evaluations with Topstep in my senior year of high school, but I had to get a job first because I literally had no money." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:26, L43) [eval_math]. The September 2026 telling puts the tutoring at 14 ("I was a math tutor at Kum when I was 14 years old." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~0:02, L34) at $16 an hour (1CEAgRUNZ4c L70), adds a welding job at $25 an hour (1CEAgRUNZ4c L143), and anchors the calendar: ("End of 9th grade year. That is when COVID hit for me." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~1:45, L95), so 9th grade was 2019-20. AP credit supplied half the degree ("180 credits to graduate. By the way, I had 90 from high school just taking those advanced college classes in high school." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~9:49, L381).

*1.2 College: University of Washington, quantitative finance, two years.* The school is named in the description and through tuition (1CEAgRUNZ4c L196): ("I graduated with a quantitative finance degree from the University of Washington at 19, made $25K playing poker, turned down a corporate risk management internship at Deloitte, and now I trade prop firms full-time from NYC making $100K+ per month in profit." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~0:00, L21) [results_claims]. He switched into the major: ("I figured if I was able to switch my major to quantitative finance, I'd be able to learn a lot more about the financial markets." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~1:15, L74); ("This is coming around to be January of 2024. I was accepted into the math major which I was super happy to hear." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~9:35, L373), admission by GPA, top 6% (1CEAgRUNZ4c L461). Content he connects to trading: ("throughout my 2 years in college, I kept playing poker, of course, but I had a lot of classes talking to me about finance, specifically pricing options, pricing futures, which is what I trade now" — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~2:44, L130); ("I graduated with a degree in quantitative finance, and so that is what I've been applying to trading on prop firms, risk management, strategy generation, that sort of stuff." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~8:29, L312). Duration and age: ("So 2 years finished the college which was really glad um that parents didn't have to pay for more of that." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~15:15, L574); ("Then I went to college, got a degree in quantitative finance, graduated when I was 19, and now I'm 21 years old." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~0:03, L35); ("I did an internship in the industry, and I've been working full-time in funding companies since graduation, which was around May 2025." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~1:02, L72).

*1.3 Poker, summer 2024 to early 2025.* ("So this is 2024 2 years ago summer got really really into poker." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~12:24, L473); online multi-tabling at $20 buy-ins on 8-16 tables (1CEAgRUNZ4c L530); the bankroll ("I started with probably $25 to my name playing with one to five dollar buy-ins at in college, and I turned that into $25,000 in probably about 6 months of going to the local card room for some higher stakes." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~1:48, L95) [results_claims]; its date ("I had about $25,000 that I made from poker over that summer, the first half, like September, October, November 2024." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~14:13, L537) [results_claims]. The decision to trade prop firms rather than take a 9-to-5 is dated January 2025 in two medium-confidence, garbled lines (1CEAgRUNZ4c L557, L584), the second attributing it to news about prop firms being unregulated.

*1.4 Start date, internship, going full-time.* The start is February 2025 in every dated telling: ("started prop firm trading full-time in February of 2025. So, we're at exactly 16 months now. I did start with $0 kind of." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~0:59, L57) [results_claims]; ("I started in February 2025 with funding signatures. March 5th was my first payment into a 150k account. So you can see the maximum payout below of 5k on Topstep." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~5:51, L231) [results_claims]. The internship was Deloitte risk management, summer 2025: ("I also did have an internship approved for risk management at Deote for that summer of ending my second year in college." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~15:05, L568); ("I turned 19 in May, and I left for my internship the first day of June. My internship was in risk management, so it's related to what I studied in college, and it's also related to how I trade prop firms right now." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~3:19, L152). He says the strategy was built then: ("I was trying to apply the things I was learning there, plus what I'd already learned in college about the financial markets, to create a very, very strong prop firm specific trading strategy. And that's what I was able to do." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~3:35, L162), trading during work ("It didn't pay too well, so honestly, during the online meetings, I was just trading in the background." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~3:31, L159) [psychology_process], with the poker money as capital (1CEAgRUNZ4c L643), around the clock from April and in New York from June (1CEAgRUNZ4c L697), and the internship-month result ("I was at the internship I made $125,000 in payouts from prop firms. I probably spent like 30k." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~18:13, L679) [results_claims]. The return offer was declined: ("got the return offer for the internship. Then I told my parents, hey, I accepted this return offer. They didn't know about prop training back then. I told him that I did that and then I declined the return offer" — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~19:12, L714); at 19 in New York with no safety net (CZYbdX2ofiI L184). The income goes to an investment account so as not to return to a 9-to-5 (1CEAgRUNZ4c L773); the stated goal is ("a $5 million net worth and then open a hedge fund." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~51:17, L1640).

*1.5 What he presents as proof, and his stance on verification.* The strongest item is the tax form: ("uh 1099 tax form. You can't fake this stuff. Topstep paid me 250k last year and then RiseWorks paid me 450k." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:27, L80) [results_claims]; Rise is the payment rail several firms use and he shows that account as proof (74CRg-mID5c L45 [results_claims]). His framing of the figures: ("I've done over 1.8 million dollars worth of prop firm payouts in the last 18 months, and that is not account balances, that is literally payouts hitting my bank account." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:21, L99) [results_claims]; ("I've earned over $1.6 million in payouts, and I'm going to show you the proof because everyone in this space talks and almost no one shows anything." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~0:01, L29) [results_claims]; and, from October 2026, a tightened rule after accusations: ("I'm only going to show payouts that hit my bank account or hit the rise count. Uh no longer showing you when I request because for some reason people were saying that I was faking that." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~8:13, L275) [results_claims]. What he says the payouts do not prove: ("The million and a half dollars I earned with funding companies is not proof that I know where the market is going. It proves that I can survive the environment of fundraising companies." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~14:22, L590) [results_claims]. The only third-party check in the corpus is the FX Replay host: ("Obviously, you know, it's not fully verified on our end. I think he has done a decent job so far of showing like live logins. He showed like a bunch of different bank accounts" — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~0:32, L85) [results_claims]; the Titans of Tomorrow billing of "over 30 trades per day" and "less than 18 months" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L73 [results_claims]) is the host's. His own disclaimer sits beside the claims: ("Trading involves real risk, and most people who try it end up losing money." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~8:58, L412).

*1.6 Affiliations.* An affiliate code: ("Again, you can use my code JJ to get the best discount on all of these firms." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~7:41, L261); ("If you use code JJ with all the firms, that should give you a better discount. Uh maybe like 80 or $90." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~0:42, L54) [eval_math]. Topstep is the firm he credits personally (5RzMu2B2E_0 L672). Appearances in the corpus: FX Replay (SNO1wqJTq5A, 2026-04-29), Backtesting with Vai (c61c4CxTpYI, 2026-06-09), Titans of Tomorrow (aCOgfvL6lK8, 2026-06-22) and its clip, whose description gives the cut point ("Watch the full episode with JJ simon here: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aCOgfvL6lK8&t=1384s" — other, -lxNWJGWtbg ~0:00, L12), NEXOR (JcW8Wjnw8ck, 2026-08-27) and Chart Fanatics (KHEQ5g55dQ4, 2026-10-04).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The start date never moves: February 2025, first payout March 5, 2025. The experience count is the upload date minus February 2025 in June (16 months: C-_0QhKZmA4 2026-06-06; gjWiGBiVis0 06-15; xTxrtFKlQfc 06-21) and 18 months from late August (4BXpI-hYqe0 08-27, 0Ua-ffltO0I 09-10, PN1UKQMPb5M 10-04, which also says, at the $2M mark, ("It's been 19 months now." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~16:07, L510) [results_claims], the latest count); the 16 months still spoken in PMGhHCjYG5c (08-20), S8fSNbPFuLA (08-06), MtBZeAkYH8Q (08-13) and the Chart Fanatics recording is stale, since February 2025 to August 2026 is 18 months. Full-time is dated to February 2025 in C-_0QhKZmA4 but to graduation around May 2025 in PMGhHCjYG5c (08-20), and his own narrative has him in college until May 2025 and in a summer internship until the return offer was declined, so the latest and most detailed account (1CEAgRUNZ4c, 09-27) places full-time trading from late summer 2025. The first-eval age is 14 in every spoken telling (May, Aug, Sept 2026) and 15 only in a description. The age statements drift by a year (section 4). Proof moved from screen recordings ("Proof screen recorded in the first 15 seconds of that." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~9:45, L352) [results_claims] and the 1099 (03-28) to bank-and-Rise-only (10-04), the latest rule.

**4. Contradictions**

1. Age. Turning 19 in May before a June 1 internship (CZYbdX2ofiI L152) and graduating around May 2025 at 19 (PMGhHCjYG5c L72; 1CEAgRUNZ4c L35) put his birth in 2006 and his age in September 2026 at 20; the spoken 21 (1CEAgRUNZ4c L35, Sept 2026) and the 20-year-old description (3L8xdh3oPm4 L20, March 2026) put it in 2005. ("I made a million dollars at 19 years old. Here's my trading journey." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:02, L28) [results_claims] fits 2006; ("When I was 14, I lost my first $50 on the Topstep evaluation. Then, when I was 20 , I lost over $60,000 in a single day" — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~17:57, L757) [results_claims], a day he elsewhere places at a $200k net worth (dJdBnSBJlgQ L370 [results_claims]), i.e. mid-2025, fits 2005. The dossier should say 20 or 21.
2. First platform "like 4 years ago" from April 2026 (5RzMu2B2E_0 L672) is 2022, his 11th-grade spring break, not the first eval at 14 (2019-20).
3. The Kumon job is placed in senior year to fund two more evals (CZYbdX2ofiI L43 [eval_math], L50, $15/hr) and at age 14 (1CEAgRUNZ4c L34, L70, $16/hr).
4. Seed capital: $25K in three videos (1CEAgRUNZ4c, CZYbdX2ofiI, nvOaN6mRu-o) against "about $20,000" in the Titans episode (aCOgfvL6lK8 L1359 [results_claims]).
5. When the strategy was built: during the summer 2025 internship (CZYbdX2ofiI L162); ("Personally, it took me two to three years to figure out all of these values and also a degree in quantitative finance." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~1:18, L71), which reaches back before he had any bankroll; "a while back when AI wasn't that intelligent" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L1522); ("The strategy has pretty much been the same for 16 months." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~53:46, L1716) [results_claims] against a monthly change of entry model with market conditions (MVP7X-3v8xk L30) and, medium confidence, ("I changed the strategy a little as market regimes change" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:12, L81).
6. Against the dossier: Section 10 item 14 treats 16 months versus 18 months as a contradiction; both count from February 2025 at different upload dates. Section 1 "Experience" quotes 16 months as a fixed credential; it is a June 2026 value.
7. Against the dossier: the credential row gives no dates; the corpus has him switching his major into quantitative finance, admitted to the math major in January 2024, finishing in two years at 19, with the Deloitte internship and the February 2025 start.

### From the primary corpus: the mentorship business as he describes it and what he withholds

**1. The business in his own words**

*1.1 Format.* In April 2026 he moved to one-to-one only: ("And I will be offering one-on-one mentorship from now on. No course, just mentorship." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~7:51, L303); ("Uh I teach a lot more about that in my one-on-one. Obviously, I can't give away everything on YouTube. Hopefully that makes sense and you forgive me for that, but I bet you can figure it out on your own if you are truly dedicated." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~11:40, L414). Entry is by application with a capital floor: ("if you have at least $1,000 to invest and you want me to walk you through this step by step and then help you scale, apply for mentorship at the link below. I'll give you statistically optimal risk management as well as my plus EV mean reversion trading strategy." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~10:18, L342); ("there is a link in the description to apply for a mentorship. It is going to be closing somewhat soon just cuz I can't teach everybody in the world." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~16:49, L584); ("There is an application link in the description. It won't stay open forever just because I don't want this approach to become too public." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~10:53, L431); ("It's by request, so I'm keeping it small on purpose." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~14:22, L612); ("It's a request, and I'm keeping it small on purpose." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~5:54, L281). One August video says the opposite: ("That's why I run a mentorship program. There's no application to join and I keep the group small." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~5:39, L255). Size: ("I keep a very small group of 200 250 people max actively trading prop firms exactly the way I do." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~5:45, L226), against a larger cumulative figure: ("And I am not uninformed. I've taught probably over 500 people how to begin trading." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~7:47, L312). Free tier: ("I have a free Discord actually in the description of this video. I talk a lot more about my trades day to day there in in the general chat. Just paste my trades, say I took it for this reason." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~7:36, L295); ("Then, you can join our free Discord, which is linked in the description. I can give you feedback on some of your trades." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~1:08, L68); and a description-text lead magnet ("grab my free playbook that breaks down the risk management system I use across 10+ firms." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~0:00, L25). Delivery includes live sessions ("Wednesday I couldn't live for you guys because I was live trading for my students." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:37:28, L3052), one-to-one weeks ("The reason I didn't get as many this week is because I wasn't trading much. I was working with Jeremy." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~0:29, L46) and the Miami episode (MIWsdldzJgQ, 2026-07-27).

*1.2 What is sold: a per-account sizing function.* Every pitch names the same deliverable: ("I have a mentorship where I go over the statistically optimal risk and profit target for every trade you ever take based on your account balance, what firm you're on, and what rules you're given." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~6:13, L297); ("And in my mentoring, risk management is the main part of what I review. I review the statistically optimal risk in terms of take-profit, stop-loss, and practically everything else for your funded account, based on the firm you trade with, your account balance, whether it's an evaluation or funded balance, the loss limit, and everything related." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~8:31, L361); ("We review the statistically optimal take-profit, stop-loss, and contract size for your account, based on the platform you use, your balance, and your available loss margin." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~14:22, L612); ("if you want me to work directly with you, give you the exact risk parameters, take profit, loss limit in dollars, and the contract size I would use on your specific accounts, generating expected value, then there is a mentorship in the description." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~40:56, L1384); ("risk management is the most important part of trading in funding firms. That's mainly what I teach in my mentoring." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~3:13, L154). The March 2026 list of what the video omits: ("I also go over like the exact risk management approach you should take, how many accounts to buy, which firms to use, how much to risk per trade in terms of dollars, points, all that sort of stuff, how to risk on different firms, copy trading, scaling, all that stuff." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~20:42, L716); and in May ("In my mentorship, I teach a lot more specifically about entry models, exit models, risk management, and when to be trading." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~12:03, L384). Trade review: ("One thing I do directly with people is review every operation they perform to ensure they follow the math, not their instinct." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~5:54, L281); ("If you want me to review your trades instead of having to learn the hard way, the link is in the description. I keep it small and I really review your trades and compare them to mine." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~14:48, L607). The discretionary layer is explicitly outside the public rules: ("I teach you a lot more about these discretionary trades. Like you wouldn't have known to take these trades from my strategy in YouTube, uh the previous one. But strategy works, you just add a little bit of discretion to make it even more profitable." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~7:58, L307); ("If you want the real mechanics, the exact way I test a configuration, size it, and exit it, that's what the mentorship program is for." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~8:44, L402). Bookkeeping he prescribes: ("you want to track SIM funded capital. So you want to see if you hit a 3K win on a funded account, you're tracking 3K, right? Because that 3K, that's not spend, that's not profit, that's not payouts. It's a future payout." — JJ, r5AhmGaFujU ~1:13, L68).

*1.3 What he will not publish.* ("You've already seen what 10 trades a day with a 1.5 R ratio produce for me. You've seen what it does for all my students, and I'm never going to publish my exact risk management in a YouTube video, simply because it's the statistically most optimal sizing." — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~11:42, L460); ("If I was to leak it and put it up on the internet for free to all 30,000 people that subscribe to my YouTube channel, u then I promise you prop firms would not exist in a month from now." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~5:34, L220); ("Personally, it took me two to three years to figure out all of these values and also a degree in quantitative finance." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~1:18, L71). Against this, the series promise: ("I just wanted to provide full transparency on how I'm executing the strategy in New York AM session and how I'm approaching prop firms in order to generate a million-dollars-in-payouts, hopefully, before the end of 2026." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~16:22, L562). For replication this means the public rules are the entry/exit skeleton at 1.5R and about 10 trades a day; the per-account-state sizing is withheld by design, and `fpt.risk` must reconstruct it from the firm rules.

### From the primary corpus: the student results he cites and the prop-firm business model he teaches

**1. The business model in his own words**

Where firms' money comes from: ("The only time a prop firm ever makes money is when you pay them an eval fee. They are not copy trading, they are not selling data, nothing like that." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:37, L110); ("They just make money off eval fees, and they have to pay out money to people who win, but those payouts are less than the eval fees, which keeps them consistently profitable." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:43, L114); the risk he says is unpriced: ("almost no one considers the risk that the firm will not pay. That's called counterparty risk. It is the biggest risk in the firms' model and it is an exposure without compensation." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~10:10, L423). Return targets: ("You're not going to be able to spend a thousand dollars and get back twenty five thousand dollars. It's just completely unrealistic and very, very statistically unlikely. So, that's not something you should shoot for. You should aim to at least double your money." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~2:56, L131); ("If I could spend a hundred k and withdraw a hundred thirty k, sure it's a lot of spending, but I'm still making thirty thousand dollars a month, right?" — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~3:25, L148); ("Every time you spend 10K, if you were given back $11,000, how many times would you take that offer? Literally as many as you could because you're going to print $1,000 out of thin air every single time you do." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~11:28, L426); ("Hopefully the video helped you understand why prop firms should be treated like a business, why it's totally fine to lose way more than you win." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~13:16, L488). Scaling arithmetic: ("to scale, you want to reach 50K a month, you want to reach 100K a month, perfect. You're going to have to spend 30 to 40K a month." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~25:00, L855); ("So, you need 160 evaluations per month to spend 40,000. So my goal is not to earn 100,000 a month. My goal is, how am I going to spend 40,000 a month?" — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~26:37, L908); ("You need volume, you need an edge, and you need as much exposure to that edge as possible. You're not going to earn six figures a month by doing one trade a day. You'll get 20 deals a month." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~17:46, L616). The ceiling: ("You're not really able to get more than 100k just because of how the firms work. They are trying to sort of limit the winners just by sending them to live accounts, restricting the amount of evals they can buy" — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~10:03, L359); ("Like based on those 10 sites, you should be able to hit 100k, but you couldn't stay static at 100k for a super long time. You could probably do it for a few months." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~22:15, L867). The idealised ladder versus his own: ("And then you can do 5K to 17K your first month, 17K to 51K your second month, 51K to 151K your third month. Obviously, this was not mine personally. U I was not able to go that fast. It took me until my fifth month to hit 125K in payout." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~30:54, L907); ("3.5x in 2 months. 3.5x in 2 months. 3.5x in 2 months. So 6 months you turn 5K into 150K" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~31:43, L930); the earlier doubling version ("if you can make $1,000 a month consistently, you should be able to make $2,000 a month consistently, and then keep compounding that to 4, 8, 16, etc." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~4:54, L211). Phases taught: ("I will review phase zero, its background, from 0 to 10,000, from 10,000 to 50,000, from 50,000 to 100,000 and, of course, its results." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~0:26, L38); ("He joined the mentorship in June 2025. In the first two weeks, he learned about businesses, not the market." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~1:26, L76); ("That means adopting a rules-based approach to operate correctly. One focused primarily on risk management. I wanted to spend a little and withdraw a lot." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~1:31, L79); ("When you go from $10,000 a month to $50,000 a month. First, you focus on consolidating the strategy, which is what the lesson here is about. You need to scale between companies consistently." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~3:08, L140). Live accounts are dismissed: ("you just need five million dollars to make 100k per month with this exact return. 2% per month, cuz 2% of this is this." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~10:59, L392); ("If you're not making at least 30k a month in your own live account, there's no point in trading it. Funding firms are what generates the most generational wealth right now." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~26:58, L918). The promise made to students: ("Optimize your risk. Continue, reverse, optimize your risk for funded accounts and I promise you can earn $10,000 a month, and then I'll help you scale that." — JJ, MIWsdldzJgQ ~2:36, L117). His plan for one student, which is also an operating rule: ("So, main things for you to do is copy two evals together on the 50K's, layer in a lot more aggressively in the AM, and those two things and also take the overnight trades for sure because it's no effort. You can get through 10 accounts with an edge of the higher timeframe reversion." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~23:17, L906).

None of these is itemised beyond what is quoted; the $780,000 figure is stated jointly by JJ and the student in one video, the "$0 to $250,000 in 90 days" line is description text never spoken in the captions, and the Connor figure in the corpus ($210,000 in 45 days) differs from the sales-page figure the dossier cites ($0 to $40,000 in 2.5 months).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The student count claims rise through 2026: "some students" at $100k (07-30); "10 of my students" over $30k (08-16); "five, six figures a month" for "the traders I teach" (09-10); then the most specific and latest, two at six figures and 10-15 at $30k (09-13). The spend guidance is stable: 30-40k a month for 50-100k (07-30), 2x minimum and 1.3x acceptable (06-17), 3.5x per two months as the idealised ladder (09-22) against his own five months to $125k. The $100k ceiling appears in June (C-_0QhKZmA4) and again in September (VY-bO35-tjE, "a few months" at 100k on 10 sites).

Student income counts: "10 of my students are already earning over $30,000 a month" (0uAQUHEB_L8 L33 [results_claims], 08-16), "some students to reach 100,000 a month" (AxP-cg50TdM L1139, 07-30), "consistent five, six figures a month" (0Ua-ffltO0I L304, 09-10), "two at six figures a month. Um 10 15 or so at 30k a month" (HlWSP7ajgpQ L591, 09-13).
2. "I promise you can earn $10,000 a month" (MIWsdldzJgQ L117) against "most people who try it end up losing money" (4IGbxmKJ4BU L412) and ("Uh, no income claims on YouTube, but I've been able to make $100,000 a month just by finding the best way to fit in that rule set." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~14:19, L422) [results_claims].
3. "at least double your money" and 1.3x ("spend a hundred k and withdraw a hundred thirty k") as acceptable (XhsbfEdJBAc L131, L148) against the "3.5x in 2 months" ladder (dJdBnSBJlgQ L930); he reconciles them only by saying the ladder was not his own pace (L907).
4. "copy two evals together" (VY-bO35-tjE L906) against the five-at-a-time copying described in the accounts group (5RzMu2B2E_0 L808 [execution_copier]); the two-eval figure is advice to a $50k/month student, the five is his own practice.
5. Against the dossier (Business, funnel detail): the student results listed there (Connor $40,000, Kyle, Dillon, Mitch) do not appear in the corpus; the corpus has the $780,000 student, Henry, Quinn, Jeremy, the "$0 to $250,000 in 90 days" description line and "Connor Made $210,000 in 45 days".

### From the primary corpus: statements in the "other" topic that bear on replication

**1. Instrument, inputs and what he does not use**

NQ only: ("yep and this is all futures NASDAQ only is what I trade" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~22:08, L749); ("So, I'm going to short with 0.5 contracts. And all these are NQ, by the way." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~17:42, L495), which means he sizes in half-NQ (five-MNQ) increments in his own backtest; the reason ("Personally, no, just because Nasdaq is the one that has at least the best commissions and and the spread for my trading approach" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~27:13, L989). A host moved it to MYM and says the math changed (c61c4CxTpYI L16) and another says, medium confidence, that he trades it on MNQ ("he he does this strategy on M and Q" — host, c61c4CxTpYI ~14:07, L347). No chart patterns: ("I don't believe in it too much. I think most of it is artificial when market makers are balancing their inventory." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~5:36, L247); no institutional or order-flow inputs: ("I personally have not investigated using institutional signals. Um I think there's definitely some alpha behind it because institutions most of the time are correct since they're making money. Uh but personally no, I have not explored it." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~6:24, L275); ("I tried a lot of the things that you are probably familiar with like order flow, level two data, and specific profit target stop losses all on live accounts. I was able to have marginal success" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~25:47, L941); ("Institutions usually looking to load up or exit positions in mass and that can create unfair moves as well." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~21:31, L797); not ICT vocabulary: ("I'm personally not too well knowing of the online lingo, but I'm just not too familiar with it in general. I don't like I don't know what those terms mean, but" — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~4:01, L193). The host's summary, which the fact's note records him affirming at line 175: ("I'm guessing the most important thing for you is not higher time frame, it's not trend, it's not points of interest. What is most important is reaction of M1 related to markets in time throughout the day." — host, -lxNWJGWtbg ~3:19, L169, medium). Volume: he reads it off candle size, medium confidence ("Like these three definitely had more contracts exchanged than all of these like 30 candles." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~7:28, L277), while a backtester reads him as not using it ("he doesn't look at volume or anything really. It's just this kind of price action analysis." — host, c61c4CxTpYI ~11:44, L296); VWAP is a host's observation, not his rule ("he often will have like the VWAP on his chart as well," — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~10:31, L407, medium). The whole method in two sentences: ("Guys, I'm telling you, it's literally that easy. Everything you do is a continuation, you do a reversal, okay?" — JJ, MIWsdldzJgQ ~2:27, L112); ("It's just me and reversion session opens or the bias." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~17:13, L575). The 9:29 candle claim: ("This video is just to prove the 9:29 a.m. candle is the most fair price of Nasdaq futures." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~4:30, L158). Regime dependence (block 1, item 5): a different entry model when drifting to highs, dumping, correcting or stagnating (MVP7X-3v8xk L30). Live-account expectancy: ("I I have a strategy that it works on live, but it's not going to be insane returns on live. It's going to be very small positive expected value. That's because it has an asymmetric risk reward." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~4:29, L223); ("the most edge you can have probably is like 1 to 5%." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~3:52, L160) [risk_eval_vs_funded]. On trade frequency, medium confidence: ("Not really. There is an optimal way to do it, but it is not for me." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~6:19, L285).

The 38-point line matters for Section 2 of the dossier, which says no statement by JJ of a fixed 38 has been found: here 38 points is his own number for the size of the unfair move he expects a continuation to make (750 dollars on one NQ contract is 37.5 points), so the joetroyer band is his target distance, not an invented gate.

Then I went back in time and used bar play to start testing the strat." — host, JcW8Wjnw8ck ~0:39, L40) and notes no directional filter ("Cuz first of all, this guy doesn't even have a bias. Like, who needs bias, really, huh?" — host, JcW8Wjnw8ck ~3:17, L112) against the host's own two-a-week baseline (JcW8Wjnw8ck L127). Backtesting with Vai logged 40 trades on MYM under Apex trailing drawdown (c61c4CxTpYI L16, L62, the latter medium) and reads the method as marking a region and waiting ("You just kind of mark out a region and just wait for price to kind of come back. He's not analyzing volume or anything really." — host, c61c4CxTpYI ~33:27, L762). The Titans host's own account-cycling model ("trade everything 1 week apart on a 4-week cycle and a 1-week off. Uh, and when you reach a payout, turn that account offline until the next cycle." — host, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~42:39, L1509) and his claim that a losing trader "made a 12K payout" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L1516) are the host's and must not be attributed to JJ.

1. Trades per day: 10 at 1.5R in the taught version (2BtwoTCxcbc L460) against ("I have performed 20 trades per day for the past 16 months." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~1:53, L106) [results_claims] and the host's "over 30" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L73 [results_claims]). The 10 is the per-session figure he tells students; 20 is his own multi-session total.
2. Contract: NQ in his words (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L749; MVP7X-3v8xk L495) against a host's "M and Q" (c61c4CxTpYI L347, medium); the 0.5-contract backtest shows he thinks in NQ with MNQ granularity.
3. Volume: candle-size volume read (Lep139B5EbU L277, medium) against two hosts who say he ignores volume (c61c4CxTpYI L296, L762).
4. Live capital: "five million dollars" (vJuwbKibN1E L392) and, a few lines later, ("Would I rather have 500 million of my own money, or would I rather have 30k of my own money to make the exact same return?" — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~11:08, L397, medium), a slip or mis-caption.
5. Against the dossier (Section 2): the statement that no fixed band has been found in his words, with 38 treated as the indicator author's choice, is contradicted by "my 38 point unfair move" (BLvsYJ4sqn8 L927), though as a target distance rather than an entry gate.
6. Against ASSUMPTIONS.md row 24 (18:00 and 20:00 sessions, one continuation then three or four reversions): his evening rules add a gap filter and a 20-point minimum excursion that the code does not have.

## 2. The Fair Pricing Theory model

**Premise (JJ).** When the market has no new information to price in,
price is likely to revert toward a reference level he calls fair value /
fair price. For NQ the reference is the price at the 09:30 ET cash open;
the 365-day backtester paraphrases him as "the price of the candle right
before the NASDAQ opens, the pre-open price, before all that institutional
volume hits" ([365-day backtest transcript](https://sozai.app/transcript/jj-simons-strategy-backtest-365-days/), **3rd**; see contradiction 3 in Section 10 for the open-vs-pre-open-candle difference).
A second anchor is the 14:00 ET price for the afternoon, in his words only when that open sits inside the morning's fair zone ("At 2:00 p.m., it opened pretty much in the fair price. So, then I would assume any move away from this 2:00 p.m. price would be unfair." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~2:58, L112); otherwise the afternoon still reverts toward 9:30 ("if 2 p.m. opens down, I will try to trade it all the way back down to 9:30." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~42:53, L1383)
([fxreplay codification](https://fxreplay.com/strategies/jj-simons-fair-value-theory-nq-strategy)).
He has said the open is fair price in roughly 95% of cases; his mentorship
has separate "understanding fair price" and "identifying fair price"
modules plus VWAP and volume-analysis modules for the rest (3rd-party
course listings). The alternates are on record in his own videos: the pre-open consolidation when the open runs straight through it (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:19:50, L2513), the candle before a pre-open volume spike ("instead of having the open price is fair. I'm looking more towards that uh 24 after candle" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:30:38, L2843), the 8:29 candle body on scheduled-news days (8lbkj0PF1uM ~3:37, L141) and the most recent consolidation once price leaves the open on volume ("I change fair price throughout the day based on most recent consolidation." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~2:34, L94).

In the verified caption, ("The fair price will be the market opening 95% of the time" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~2:45, L137), restated for non-news days in October ("On non-news days, the open is going to be fair most of the time, like 95% of the time." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:13:12, L2310); the sozai.app rendering "the fair price is 95% of the time going to be the market open" ([$1.6M video](https://sozai.app/transcript/strategy-behind-1-6m-prop-firm-payouts/)) is a third-party transcript of the same line;
on news releases: "The spike off the number is the unfair move, fade it
back to fair price" ([X](https://x.com/itsjjsimon)). The FX Replay PDF adds
that "the best setups require displacement + break of structure on the
same candle", that mean reversions alone ran PF ~1.46 with 49-59% win rate
depending on filter, and that continuations had the higher win rate and
profit factor. Other codifiers anchor differently: krisskross18's
TradingView script uses the 09:29 candle open; the 365-day backtester
"the candle right before the NASDAQ opens"; joetroyer offers six sources.

**His own words on the anchor and its drift (Titans of Tomorrow episode aCOgfvL6lK8, 2026-06-22; the Sept 2026 clip -lxNWJGWtbg repeats the same passage).**
"First fair price that I will assume is just 9:29 Eastern before the market
opens. And then from there, if there's consolidation and then more
breakouts, I'll just treat the most recent consolidation as a fair price."
On why the open is exploitable: "Whenever the session opens obviously
increase in volatility ... it creates an unfair move. So just because
volume came into the market and it moved a specific direction that
shouldn't change the fair underlying price of the stocks." On news:
"news most of the time is going to be priced in pretty fairly so that when
news comes out there's a huge candle, I'll just trade a continuation of
that candle for my first trade of the day. And then after that I'll take
usually like three to four trades trying to revert that move ... back to
the pre-news price." On cycles: "Not from session to session ... But within
a session, I do believe there are unfair moves and if you are able to
revert those unfair moves, then you have a positive expectancy."
`rolling_fair_value=True` implements the consolidation re-anchor.

**His full day (same episode, June 2026).** 08:30 news: "the continuation of news and then usually three reversions or so" (aCOgfvL6lK8 ~18:19, L689). 09:30 New York: "one continuation, three or four reversions."
Then "a longer trade ... going into lunch hour like 11:00 a.m. ... I'm
trying to play it out from 11:00 all the way until 2:00." 14:00: "one
continuation, three to four reversions." 18:00: "same thing." 20:00 Asian
session: "one more time." That is the arithmetic behind "20-30 trades a
day": five sessions of four or five trades in that interview. His two dedicated evening-session videos instead give one continuation and one reversion each for 18:00 and 20:00 ("So, 6 p.m. one continuation, one reversion. Asian session, same thing. One continuation, one reversion." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~4:15, L163; "This allows me to take four extra trades per day." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:33, L39), and his logged totals are about 20 a day with 10 in the morning ("I'm trying to complete probably about 20 trades during a whole trading day. Uh, well, I usually do 10 in the morning." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~19:34, L677). `--all-sessions` enables the
08:30, 18:00 and 20:00 windows.

**Window (JJ).** 09:30 to 11:00 ET ("I'm going to trade it from 9:30 until 11." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~37:17, L1212), a soft cut-off he extends while accounts remain ("I usually don't go past 11 a.m. EST unless I haven't finished trading a majority of my accounts." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:38:58, L3098); the afternoon opens at 14:00 and its length is not stated by him (the FX Replay host says so and uses 15:00, SNO1wqJTq5A ~6:01, L262; his "First 90 minutes of every single session" would give 15:30, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~2:10, L138, medium confidence); the 18:00 and 20:00 sessions are on his session list from March 2026 (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~7:59, L296), got a dedicated video in May 2026 (GMDUiamqgig) and another in September 2026 (BLvsYJ4sqn8), which set the Asia window at 90 minutes ("I'll trade for 90 minutes. So, until 9:30." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~6:10, L222) and the anchor test between the two opens (BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~13:51, L459); the 08:30 news window runs to the 09:30 open (8lbkj0PF1uM ~4:42, L174).

**Two phases.**

| Phase | JJ's own description | fxreplay / AndrewFXTD codification |
|---|---|---|
| Continuation | his own figures: 0-10 minutes (March 2026, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~2:34, L117), 10-15 minutes (June, UVKVSWKFlvo ~8:29, L310), 15 minutes (June, gjWiGBiVis0 ~13:14, L460), 5 minutes (July, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:35, L95; October, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:28:06, L2766); latest = 5: trade with the opening push away from fair price | "first 10-15 minutes"; fxreplay's optimisation also skips the first 3 minutes |
| Reversion | the remaining ~85 minutes to 11:00: trade back to fair price | same, but fxreplay's filtered test keeps only reversions in the first ~30 minutes and avoids 10:00-11:00 |

**Premium / discount band.** joetroyer's TradingView indicator draws a
+/-38-point band around the fair line and fades price back to the line
after a displacement candle closes back toward it ("sell above, buy below",
[indicator page](https://www.tradingview.com/script/j4BFY7JW-Fair-Price-Theory-NQ-Reversion-Continuation/)).
He does use 38 points as the size of the unfair move: "took the shorts, got out break even because it didn't make my 38 point unfair move" (JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~29:02, L927), which is the 750-dollar continuation target on one NQ contract (37.5 points) rather than a published entry gate; treat the band as his target distance and the touch gate as the indicator author's choice (`require_band_touch=False` by default).

**A caveat in his own words.** On TikTok he has said the simplest
continuation model outperformed the simplest mean-reversion model over the
last five years, even though his flagship method is reversion to fair
price. Read with Section 8: the fxreplay tests found continuations the
stronger half (higher win rate and profit factor); reversions ran PF ~1.46
and only held up in the first ~30 minutes, weak after 10:00, which is
consistent with his TikTok remark.

<!-- target: Section 2 -->
### From the primary corpus: what fair price is

**The claim.** The method prices NQ as a basket of companies and sells or buys any move away from that price: ("it's saying that we are trying to price the futures of Nasdaq. So, we're pricing actual companies, and then any moves away from a fair price, we want to trade back to fair price with a reversion." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~0:59, L61). Only two external events matter: ("I'm only looking at external factors that impact the price. So pretty much just news and session opens." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~3:24, L176). Of those, only news moves the fair price; a session open moves the traded price and is therefore the thing to fade: ("Fair prices only ever changed by news and by session opens. Now, session open's not going to actually change the fair price, but it'll change the real price" — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~1:16, L71); ("the only thing that, in my opinion, changes the fair price is the news." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~18:29, L703). Before every trade the cause of the move is classified: ("did news cause this move or was it like a session open? What caused it? Unfair, fair, news." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~1:29, L78).

**Why the open is exploitable, in his words.** ("the New York session open acts as the first major intraday reference point for fair auction. Early moves away from that price are often driven by opening flow, liquidity imbalances, stops, hedging, momentum, participation, not necessarily by a true, long-lasting repricing of the index." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~8:39, L318); ("when the market opens at 9:30, there is an increase in volume, all of the overnight orders are executed, institutions are acting unfairly, and price is manipulated." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:44, L45); ("are we really changing the fair price of these companies just because the session opened? Well, not really," — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~0:44, L53). The first seconds of the open follow the overnight order flow, which is why the continuation is taken first: ("it is pretty likely that the opening trend for at least the first 10 to 15 seconds is going to have a lot of selling orders because obviously the overnight trend was mostly bearish here." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~5:00, L183). He sizes the edge small: ("the opening price is usually a pretty good estimate of the fair price because it 's a bias. And remember, if you have a bias in a funding firm, that's essentially a 1% advantage on average, something like that." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~8:42, L318); ("Fair value is a very strong bias that wins above break-even rates, which is why it works so well on prop firm environments." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~7:26, L282); even at fair price, ("it's a bit more likely than chance that it will go in the direction of a higher timeframe reversal, right?" — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~13:53, L529). Weekend gaps are not a separate rule: ("we are valuing companies and the fair value of those companies on the Nasdaq doesn't care if there was a void, because the fair price of them changed over the weekend." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~20:23, L704).

**Reference 1: the 9:29 candle body, which he equates with the 9:30 open.** The strongest single statement is the May 2026 video built around it: ("I can tell you for certain the candle is the 9:29 a.m. candle every single day. That is in EST, and it is a very, very good representation of fair price of the market." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:34, L40); the drawing rule is ("mark out the body of the 9:29 a.m. candle, just like this. Use a rectangle, and then this is going to be the fair price for the day." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~1:10, L58) and ("no need to include the wicks." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~6:04, L205). The same body rule appears in April (("I'm going to mark out the pre-open candle 9:29 just the body of that candle right here." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~2:34, L117)) and June (("First, fair price that I will assume is just 9:29 Eastern before the market opens. And then from there, if there's consolidation and then more breakouts, I'll just read the most recent consolidation as a fair price." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~17:48, L672)). In other videos the same reference is called the opening price: ("I believe the 9:30 a.m. is the fair price. Any move away from that is unfair. So, I would trade a reversion back to the opening price." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~10:07, L366); ("The fair price will be the market opening 95% of the time" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~2:45, L137); ("coming into the session, this is going to be my fair price, just the opening price of the 9:30 a.m. session." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~3:52, L190); and, with the news-day qualifier, ("On non-news days, the open is going to be fair most of the time, like 95% of the time." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:13:12, L2310). He says the two readings are interchangeable (medium confidence; the caption is garbled): ("Opening candle at 9:30 right here. The opening price seems quite fair. Well, you can also dial half of 929. It's more or less the same thing." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~22:59, L762). The general form is ("fair price is basically the price before the session open, because when the session opens, there's an influx of volume from institutions that are willing to trade unfairly." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~0:22, L41). Two backtesters read it the same way: the FX Replay host says ("somebody said they thought it was the 9:29 candle. It's basically market open." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~7:57, L324) and the Vai host uses the whole candle rather than the body (("He typically just does the body, but I'm just going to do the whole candle" — host, c61c4CxTpYI ~2:00, L87)).

**Reference 2: the pre-open consolidation, when the open runs straight through it.** The 9:29 print is not sacred: ("Just understand that 9:29 is not always going to be the fair price because there can be unfair moves pre-market open." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~9:00, L305). Test: if the open is in the middle of the overnight range it is fair (("it looks like it's opening in the middle of the overnight range. So, it is probably opening at a fair price. I'll just mark out the open." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~9:12, L270)); if the opening move goes straight to a pre-open consolidation and beyond, that consolidation is fair and the open is not (("this is the chunk of consolidation right before the market open. Now, I know market opened down here. But when it opened, it went straight up to this consolidation and then even farther beyond. So, open not likely to be fair." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:19:50, L2513)). A pre-open volume spike moves the reference to the candle before the spike: ("Usually at at 25 after there is sometimes a volume spike which creates an unfair move. So instead of having the open price is fair. I'm looking more towards that uh 24 after candle" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:30:38, L2843); ("fair price could be this consolidation right here before the initial volume spike which technically did happen 6 minutes pre-open." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~19:52, L622); ("it was only going to trade back towards this as the fair price because it's the consolidation before that pre-market volume spike." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~2:37, L124). Also JJ, MIWsdldzJgQ ~4:00, L163 (overnight consolidation as fair; the open went straight to it) and PN1UKQMPb5M ~5:55, L206.

**Reference 3: the pre-news price on scheduled-news days.** ("the fair price of news is going to be the price before the news came out. This is because this news is planned. If the news is planned, they already have a forecast for it." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~1:44, L84); the drawing rule is the 8:29 candle body (("mark out the candle before news. So, 8:30 a.m. was news. And I'll mark out here the body of the candle before the news. So, this would be 8:29 a.m. EST." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~3:37, L141)), because ("Most of the time it's pretty much exactly on the forecast." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~1:54, L89). On such days the 9:30 open is explicitly the wrong anchor: ("if you mark this out and say this is my fair price cuz it's 930, unfortunately, you're going to be wrong. And the reason for this is because when the market opened, the volume came in and it's going to move to a fair price, which is the pre-news price." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:03:48, L2023). The choice between the two is made by the first minutes after the open: ("So I would no longer consider morning to be fair after 9:34. I would only look to trade back towards the pre-news price." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~14:09, L496); ("worst case scenario, the open is fair. Like, the lower fair price would be the open because it's only been going down. So the fair price is either the open or the pre-news price, right?" — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~13:27, L513); ("I stopped looking at the open because if the open were fair, this green candle would have printed as a huge red candle and we would have been back to the open by now." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~14:57, L567). The same logic covers CPI and FOMC: ("CPI obviously today CPI is right here. That is the fair price." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~14:34, L491); ("the pre-FOMC price is extremely fair just because it's already priced in what the rate is going to be if it's changing or not." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~5:56, L227); and the reversions on a news morning target the 8:30 print (("looking for reversions to this red line, which is the open of the 8:30 a.m. news event" — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~13:02, L444)). The September summary: ("We're going to assume a specific fair price in the market, which would be a session open or a pre-news event." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~0:51, L59).

**VWAP.** Acceptable as an average estimate, rejected for news: ("You could use VWAP. It is a pretty good estimate on average." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~7:35, L287); ("the only problem is VWAP doesn't really accurately account for news. So, that's why I personally don't use it." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~7:47, L294). No other fact in the corpus mentions VWAP.

**Numbers.**

**Evolution, by upload date.** March 2026 (3L8xdh3oPm4): the 9:30 price is fair and the pre-open candle is marked. April (Lep139B5EbU): 9:29 body. May (7UXiI2arAlQ, 8lbkj0PF1uM): the 9:29 candle presented as the single key; the expected-news rule (8:29 body) and the 9:29-not-always-fair caveat. June (UVKVSWKFlvo, aCOgfvL6lK8, CzxSgYujDxs): "the price before the session open"; 9:29 and 9:30 declared the same. July (KN7j6NXXAio, 7KJOh2NF-lQ): the 95% figure; the 9:34 decision on news days. September (DEkzU8gqX-o): "a session open or a pre-news event"; VWAP remark. October (KHEQ5g55dQ4): 95% restated for non-news days, pre-news beats the open on news days, 9:24 candle on a 9:25 spike. No later video withdraws an earlier definition; later ones add qualifiers. Current: the October statements with the May drawing convention.

**Contradictions.** (1) News priced correctly or not: in one interview he says ("I'm betting that news most of the time is going to be priced in pretty fairly." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~15:40, L600) and a minute later ("because I believe on average the news was forecasted and priced incorrectly. So the increased volatility solely from the fact the news actually was released should not impact the fair price of the futures." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~16:09, L616); the May video sides with the first (8lbkj0PF1uM L89). (2) Dossier contradiction 3 (open versus pre-open candle) is not a contradiction in his usage: he draws the 9:29 body and calls it the opening price in the same breath (CzxSgYujDxs L762; 7UXiI2arAlQ L40 against 3L8xdh3oPm4 L366). (3) The dossier says the exact alternates to the open are not public; the corpus gives them: pre-open consolidation, the candle before a pre-open spike, the pre-news candle, and the most recent consolidation (next block). (4) The dossier's version of the 95% line comes from a third-party transcript and has the market open at the end of the sentence; the verified caption reads ("The fair price will be the market opening 95% of the time" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~2:45, L137). (5) The dossier dates the anchor-drift quote to September 2026; the clip -lxNWJGWtbg (2026-09-18) is cut from aCOgfvL6lK8 (2026-06-22), so the statement is June 2026.

<!-- target: Section 2 -->
### From the primary corpus: when fair price moves and when it stops being a target

**Reference 4: the most recent consolidation, once price has left the anchor on volume.** ("the most recent consolidation is usually what I would term to be the fair price." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~2:25, L124); ("I change fair price throughout the day based on most recent consolidation." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~2:34, L94). The trigger is consolidation, then a break of structure, then continuation away from the old anchor: ("I believe fair price to now be this area right here because we consolidated then broke structure and continued away." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~2:39, L114); ("I would delete the fair price at 9:30. Now, I'm going to assume that the fair price is this most recent consolidation, because when the volume came in, it moved away from the session opening" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~11:00, L434); ("I would adjust the fair price from the open to right here before this candle spawned in at 9:51. Because when the candle spawned in, a bunch of volume just took it up farther." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~6:32, L245). The logical test he states repeatedly: ("If the open was fair, this move up here would have been a move down. It would have came straight back down to the open, but it didn't. So I would now treat this as fair." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~5:31, L205). The new zone is drawn as top, bottom and middle (("So just marked the hop in the bottom of the consolidation. There's the middle of it." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~5:12, L195)). Three qualifiers: the move away must be large enough (("It never made enough of a move away for me to adjust fair price to here. So, I'm taking a pretty long trade back up to market open." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~24:04, L747)); the consolidation must carry volume (("all this consolidation here was unfair basically because there is no volume." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~3:57, L160); ("consolidation helps to indicate a fair price if it is during a session, because that is when the volume is most obvious" — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~23:14, L795)); and the direction of the peak-volume break decides which side is fair (("when the peak volume of these candles arrived, it dropped. If it had gone back up, then obviously I would have bought when it broke back down to here, but it broke down. So, I would treat this most recent consolidation as a fair price and simply buy there." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~22:37, L750)). One duration datum: after a news spike he waited for ("There's the 30 minutes of consolidation. This is me marking out a fair price right here." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~2:29, L126). Losing reversions are themselves a signal to move the anchor: ("I try to adapt fair price if my trades are losing. So, I won't keep trading back towards the market open if it's trending heavily in one direction." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~32:36, L1069). He concedes this layer is not mechanical: ("It is a little bit discretionary. Unfortunately, I can't make it 100% mechanical exactly how to do it." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~4:36, L176); ("I just rely on a little bit of discretion to identify fair value." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~7:47, L294).

**The first-move test for a new session open (6 p.m. versus 8 p.m.).** The rule is the same as reference 4 but applied at the Asian open: ("is 8:00 p.m. fair or is 6 p.m. fair? If you said 6 p.m., that is correct because it opened. Huge move down. The first move was down. Uh, so 6 p.m. is fair. I am not going to revert to Asian. I'm going to revert to six." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~13:51, L459); (medium) ("I switched fair price from six to Asian because Asian opened down. If Asian opens up, then I would leave fair price at six." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~7:12, L254); ("8 p.m. open is a red candle, but it didn't do anything. There's no movement short. So, I would still be considering 6 p.m. is fair." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~26:01, L834); ("when Asian opened, boom, it went away from 6 PM. So 6 PM down here is no longer fair. Asian open is fair. Just trade a reversion." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~2:22, L105); ("since the first move was bullish, I no longer see 6:00 p.m. as a target." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~8:51, L348). The same test applied at 9:30 against the overnight anchor: ("Fair price likely is the open because it opened up away from the Asian price so I can delete that." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~2:24, L109). In words: an open that moves away from the prior anchor becomes the new anchor; an open whose first move is back toward the prior anchor confirms the prior anchor; an open that does nothing leaves the prior anchor in place.

**Two or three candidates.** When the test is inconclusive he takes the midpoint and trades between them: ("I just put a line in the middle of all of them and then treated it as the fair price for most of the session." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~4:37, L177); ("So, it's either here or here. So, I put it in the middle of them. Uh because my whole goal here is to have a bias." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~20:09, L560); ("worst case, you can just trade to and from them with a 1.5 risk to reward." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~12:28, L441); ("Just trading from and towards the 6:00 p.m. and Asian session because one of them is right." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~17:11, L647); when 6 p.m. and 8 p.m. coincide, ("this area right here between these two lines is the most fair price ever." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~6:26, L221). When unsure he picks the direction of the next likely fair value: ("Where's the next most likely fair value? Well, the long direction because the reversion would be reverting these shorts." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~19:44, L657).

**Unexpected news resets the anchor.** ("Since the news was unexpected, it will change the fair market price." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~6:56, L325); ("instead of trading back to the pre-news price, because the move is fair, I would treat the most recent consolidation after the news to be the new fair price" — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~10:05, L338); ("unexpected news, this is a new fair price and I would just expect a slight positive drift if the news was positive." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~2:55, L120). A large miss on scheduled news is treated as unexpected: ("Wednesday, pretty far off, so um in this case it's sort of unexpected in a way. Then, I'll just treat this consolidation and the 9:29 a.m. candle as the fair price." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~6:42, L235); a small beat shifts the target to the post-news consolidation (("instead of going down to the pre-news price, I was just looking at this value here because the news was a little better than expected for the Nasdaq." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~18:21, L689)). Unscheduled shocks (tweets) are recognised from the tape: ("the volume increased out of nowhere at a random time and then it just started consolidating after. So, this is a new fair price." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~29:51, L817). Retracted news is ignored: ("Fake news is very weird when you're doing fair pricing theory. Um, basically just pretend it didn't happen." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~39:17, L1067). The anchor also drifts with the sign of the day's inputs: ("from here over time fair price changes right and I feel like it's going to change in the positive direction because news was green and open was green" — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~42:54, L1163).

**When the reference stops being a target.** After 11:00 the 9:30 price is demoted from target to bias: ("9:30 is not a target after 11:00 a.m." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~41:28, L1340); ("It is just a high time frame bias for reversion because in the long run markets are reverting." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~41:32, L1342). Midday has no volume to revert on: ("Between 11 to 2 p.m. there is basically no volume. It's kind of a dead time in the market. So, it is possible, slightly possible for price to be trading at an unfair price between 11 and 2 p.m." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~42:39, L1376); ("during the hours of 11:00 a.m. to 1:00 p.m., the volume is dead and it's unable to return to its fair price." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~2:34, L100). Price too close to the anchor is not traded: ("it's too close to take reversals at 6:00 p.m. Because remember, it's 6:00 p.m. It's a fair price." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~1:07, L70); a whole evening was skipped because ("This one literally did not move more than 20 points away from anything." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~10:40, L369). Trading away from the anchor is the defined error: ("the fair price is the price prior to the opening right here. And if you go short here, that means you're trading away from it." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~10:35, L442). The 6 p.m. open is fair ("probably 98% of the time" — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~18:59, L633) but not always, because ("there's not enough volume transacted between 5 and 6 for that hour when the market's in its downtime for it to open 207 points higher than where it is." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~19:20, L644). The distance to the anchor is the expected payoff and drives account selection: ("if you think the fair price is here, you know you'll get 80 points. You know you'll get 50 points. You know you'll get 100 points, and then you know exactly how many points you're going to get." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~5:45, L221).

**Numbers.**

**Evolution, by upload date.** April 2026 (RnwOqmEF6Rk): "most recent consolidation" named as the fair price, with the 30-minute example. May (GMDUiamqgig): 6 p.m. defined as fair, Asian open confirms or replaces it. June (MVP7X-3v8xk, aCOgfvL6lK8, CzxSgYujDxs): intraday re-anchoring stated as a rule; fake news; midpoint of candidates. July (KN7j6NXXAio, EyR3i2GxwyA): "delete the fair price at 9:30" after a volume break; three-candidate trading at 1.5 RR. August and September (g68HHbrIBBg, BLvsYJ4sqn8, l6iq0ljhxIo, DEkzU8gqX-o): the first-move test between 6 p.m. and 8 p.m. fully worked; 98% for 6 p.m.; discretion admitted. October (KHEQ5g55dQ4, PN1UKQMPb5M): losing reversions trigger re-anchoring; "enough of a move away"; 9:30 not a target after 11:00. Nothing is withdrawn; the October statements are current.

**Contradictions.** (1) 6 p.m. "always" fair versus 98% versus the 207-point counter-example: in the September session video he retracts "always" mid-sentence (BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~11:27, L385) and three days later gives 98% (l6iq0ljhxIo L633, L644). (2) The dossier presents `rolling_fair_value` as an optional reconstruction; in the corpus the re-anchor is his standing rule from April onward (RnwOqmEF6Rk L124; MVP7X-3v8xk L94; KN7j6NXXAio L434). (3) The dossier's Section 6 reports the rule that the 9:30 fair price is invalid after 11:00 as second-hand from the Chart Fanatics episode; it is first-hand in that episode (("9:30 is not a target after 11:00 a.m." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~41:28, L1340)). (4) The 11:00-14:00 "one final trade" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L694, next block) against ("I'd recommend not trading during these hours" — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~2:34, L100) is consistent only if that single trade is entered at about 11:00 and left to run.

<!-- target: Section 2 -->
### From the primary corpus: the daytime sessions, their windows and trade counts

**The list and how it changed.** The March 2026 strategy video gives six opens: ("that would be any EST, the 6:00 p.m. when the market reopens, 8:00 p.m. Asian session, 3:00 a.m. London session, uh 8:30 a.m. when there's news, 9:30 a.m. Eastern, and then 2:00 p.m. Eastern for the New York PM session." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~7:59, L296). The design intent was a strategy for every session with multiple entries per session (XhsbfEdJBAc ~5:15, L211). London is on the list because the method works there, not because he trades it: ("It works for the Asian session, the London session, the 6:00 p.m. opening, all sessions." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~15:39, L590) against ("I'm not going to talk about London because I don't operate there." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~3:56, L155) and, in October (medium), ("If you're trading London session, then well, there you go. Not me, though." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~27:36, L852). Both backtest hosts say the same (host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~1:59, L132; host, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~40:10, L1300, medium, where the extractor's note records his assent that it works there). The sessions he actually trades are 8:30 news, 9:30, 2:00 p.m., 6:00 p.m. and 8:00 p.m. ET, plus an overnight limit-order routine (next block). His stated daily routine is AM session, lunch, PM session, dinner, evening session (CZYbdX2ofiI ~4:34, L199, medium; the caption repeats "AM" for the evening).

**The whole day as he described it in June 2026.** ("8:30 a.m. gives me the continuation of news and then usually three reversions or so on that. New York a.m., one continuation, three or four reversions." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~18:19, L689); ("Usually going into lunch hour like 11:00 a.m., I'll enter one final trade and it's a much longer trade just cuz I'm trying to play it out from 11:00 all the way until 2:00." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~18:28, L694); ("At 2:00 p.m., New York p.m. session opens. I'll start again one continuation, three to four reversions. 6:00 p.m. session, same thing. One continuation, three to four reversions. And then again, one more time Asian session at 8:00 p.m." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~18:35, L698). The September clip -lxNWJGWtbg (L150, L154, L159) is cut from the same episode. Daily totals elsewhere: ("I'm trying to complete probably about 20 trades during a whole trading day. Uh, well, I usually do 10 in the morning." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~19:34, L677); ("640 operations in 30 days, that's about 20, well, I'll say 21 a day." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~29:48, L1013) of which 11 came in the 90-minute New York morning (same line). The $1.6M video, which the dossier cites for a cadence of around 10 trades per day, says of the morning reversion phase ("There is no maximum number of transactions." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:45, L101); the 10 is a morning count, not a daily cap (KhooqEQK9bA ~8:45, L379: "more than 10 in a morning session"). The lowest figure is the backtest video's description, "2 strong trades every morning" (MVP7X-3v8xk ~0:00, L22).

**08:30 news.** Days: ("red folder US impact news. That's going to be 8:30 a.m. EST, usually Tuesday, Thursday, sometimes Friday, Monday, whatever." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~1:17, L70). First trade: ("I'll start at 8:30 a.m. Eastern just because there's usually news there. I'll just get my first trade in just a continuation of the news" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~15:40, L600), then about three reversions to the 8:29 body (L689 above; ("looking for reversions to this red line, which is the open of the 8:30 a.m. news event" — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~13:02, L444)). The window closes at the cash open because the open's overnight orders can wick a winning trade out ("it is very, very dangerous if the market is opening while you're in a trade." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~4:42, L174). Without 8:30 news the first trade of the day is the 9:30 continuation (UVKVSWKFlvo ~5:08, L199).

**09:30 New York AM.** The main session: ("This works with any session opening, but New York is the best" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:35, L95); ("That's when the majority of my trades go in and they're always the the higher risk reward higher risk trades because it's more volatile" — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~0:53, L62). Window: ("I'm going to trade it from 9:30 until 11." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~37:17, L1212); (also 7UXiI2arAlQ ~5:12, L179); logged live at ("11:06 a.m. Eastern. Just finished trading A.M. session" — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~9:54, L325). Pre-open entries exist: ("at 9:25 Eastern, there's usually a volume spike. So, if you can get the direction of that, then you can always get in a little bit early to positions and then hold them through the open" — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~1:20, L72); ("sometimes I enter these pre-market positions at 9:25 and 9:05 if there are volume spikes at those times." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~2:09, L107); also RnwOqmEF6Rk ~4:55, L206 (a spike 5 to 10 minutes before the open on some days). Continuation phase, five statements across seven months: March ("I only trade it in the first 5 to 10 minutes or I guess like 0 to 10 minutes." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~2:34, L117) with reversion from minute 10 to 90 (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~2:47, L124); June ("I only take continuations in like the first 10-15 minutes because when the unfair initial opening move stops, I definitely want to be shorting for reversion." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~8:29, L310) and the 15-minute candle as the last continuation candle (gjWiGBiVis0 ~13:14, L460); July ("I'll do a continuation in the first 5 minutes" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:35, L95) and ("After that, I will look for a mean reversion for 5 to 90 minutes. So, 5 minutes and after that, I look for the reversal." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:45, L101); October ("I usually limit my continuations to the first five minutes." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:28:06, L2766). The latest two agree on five minutes. The principle behind all of them is that continuations belong to the opening push only (UVKVSWKFlvo ~3:01, L129). Reversion phase: any time before 11:00 (("As long as it's before 11:00 a.m., I don't care." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~5:20, L238)). The 11:00 cut-off is soft and account-driven: ("I usually don't go past 11 a.m. EST unless I haven't finished trading a majority of my accounts." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:38:58, L3098); also 74CRg-mID5c ~23:51, L819 (past 11 only if the 10 New York trades are not done). After 11 only reversals with the higher-timeframe turn: ("after 11, I would only take positions for reversals. So, I only look for short positions after 11. Simply trading in the direction of the trend reversal." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~12:04, L511); at all-time highs he avoids it (MVP7X-3v8xk ~20:50, L578). Being back at the anchor at 11:00 ends the session (gjWiGBiVis0 ~12:54, L449). Day filters: Monday or Friday is the least volatile day for lack of morning news (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~26:49, L918); holidays are traded if ("There is an influx in volume just cuz you can see the 9:30 a.m. candle that's larger than most of these candles." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~8:13, L301). Chart: ("Everything here is 1 minute. The only thing I'll do on the 5 minutes is look for my high time frame bias." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~5:59, L255); the bias looks back up to six hours (aCOgfvL6lK8 ~13:30, L527).

**11:00 to 14:00.** One long trade only (aCOgfvL6lK8 L694 above); otherwise dead volume (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L1376; 7UXiI2arAlQ L100) and the 9:30 price is a bias, not a target (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L1340).

**14:00 New York PM.** Playbook: ("Just a continuation after the opening candle. A very large green candle, as always, and then a reversal after the structure breakout back to the 2:00 p.m. opening price." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~12:05, L431). Anchor: the 2:00 p.m. open is fair if it opens inside the morning's fair zone (("At 2:00 p.m., it opened pretty much in the fair price. So, then I would assume any move away from this 2:00 p.m. price would be unfair." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~2:58, L112)); otherwise the afternoon reverts toward 9:30: ("when the 2 p.m. session opens, which is another session that I trade, if 2 p.m. opens down, I will try to trade it all the way back down to 9:30." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~42:53, L1383); ("obviously you can't revert 500 points in 2:00 p.m. session, so use your discretion. Just know the direction's most likely back towards 9:30." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~13:49, L485); also 7UXiI2arAlQ ~8:23, L274 (one final short toward 9:29 from the 2:00 session). The 2:00 candle also confirms a standing bias before a continuation (Lep139B5EbU ~7:43, L285). Count in one logged session: continuations first (the extractor's claim counts two; the caption gives no number), then ("Then no more continuations for me personally. Reversion one, reversion two, reversion three. So one, two, three wins, two losses for that session." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~29:57, L922). Whether it is traded depends on accounts left: ("I don't really trade in the PM session because I can trade all my accounts during the morning. Sometimes I operate the opening at 6:00 p.m. and the Asian opening." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~7:10, L316); ("I don't trade 2 p.m. if I finish all my accounts, but the bias is just short." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~6:37, L240); ("I had to trade 2 p.m. just because I didn't get through anything in A.M." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~29:35, L911); and on volume (EyR3i2GxwyA ~4:28, L177: "basically like zero volume"). The window length is never stated by him; the FX Replay host says so and picks 3:00 (("He didn't he didn't specifically say how long he trades. From my testing, I think it's probably best to cut off around 3:00 if you're trading that afternoon window." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~6:01, L262)); the only JJ statement is the medium-confidence ("First 90 minutes of every single session. New York, Asia, New York TM." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~2:10, L138), which would give 15:30. In low volume (New York PM, Asia) he moves to the 5-minute chart with identical rules (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~27:00, L924).

**Evolution.** March 2026: six opens including London; continuation 0-10 minutes. June: the five-session day with one-plus-three-or-four each; continuation 10-15 minutes; "I don't operate" London. July: continuation 5 minutes, no maximum reversions, 21 trades a day, the 2 p.m. playbook (q8PXXvY9lS0). August: 20 a day, 10 in the morning; 11:00 soft cut-off tied to account count. September: "I don't really trade in the PM session". October: 5-minute continuation, 9:30-11:00, 2 p.m. traded only when the morning did not clear the accounts. Current: the October statements.

**Contradictions.** (1) The afternoon: a defined session in May, July and October against ("I don't really trade in the PM session" — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~7:10, L316) in September; reconciled by his own condition, accounts remaining. (2) London: on the March list and "works", never traded. (3) Continuation window: his own figures span 0-10, 10-15, 15 and 5 minutes; the dossier's Section 10 item 1 attributes 10-15 to third parties only, but it is also his (UVKVSWKFlvo L310). (4) The dossier's afternoon session of 14:00-15:00 in earlier videos has no JJ source in the corpus; the 15:00 end is a backtester's choice. (5) The dossier's Section 10 item 4 reads the $1.6M video's 10 as a daily figure; in that video and later ones it is the morning count and the day is about 20. (6) The dossier gives the 08:30 news session one continuation and three or four reversions; the caption says ("usually three reversions or so on that" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~18:19, L689).
