<!-- target: Section 2 -->
### From the primary corpus: the evening sessions, the overnight book and evaluation versus funded use

**Naming.** In June 2026 the 6 p.m. reopen was itself called the Asian session (("Now, Asian session of Monday, 6:00 p.m. open was here." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~2:19, L106)); from May's dedicated video onward Asia means 8 p.m. (("Asian session opens at 8:00 p.m." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~1:27, L67); PN1UKQMPb5M ~11:45, L380). Read "Asian" in the June weekly recaps as 6 p.m.

**18:00 reopen.** Anchor: ("Fair price is the 6:00 p.m. open whenever it reopens for the day. Just because the futures were closed for an hour, which means there's an hour for them to be priced at a good price." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:14, L29); ("6 p.m. is going to open fair. There's an hour from 5 to 6 when the futures market is down." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~0:53, L59). Count: ("I'm going to look for one continuation of 6 p.m. Usually, I don't think ever more." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~1:02, L64); ("each session I would trade one continuation and one reversal." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:27, L36). Direction follows the opening candle: ("I'm going to do whatever the opening candle does, and it is green. Break and close above structure, so pretty good continuation trade as usual for Asian session, 38 points take profit." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~4:10, L168). Until the Asian open only reversions to 6 p.m.: ("between 6 p.m. and Asia, I'm just looking for reversions to 6 p.m." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~0:57, L60); ("assuming it's 6:00 p.m. It opens at a fair price; I'll only be looking for reversals around 6:00 p.m." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~0:58, L62). Sundays are traded the same (("I mean, I do the same thing on Sundays." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~12:18, L411)) and open fairer (GMDUiamqgig L245). Targets are halved: ("Obviously you can take profits of 19 points instead of 38, simply because there is much less volume. If you do a 38, make sure you go to the break-even point, especially when it breaks structure against your position." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~9:56, L426). It is optional: ("I don't really want to trade 6 p.m. open. So some days I'm going to skip 6 p.m." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~11:31, L373). The session is positioned as the add-on for funded scaling: ("It is a good additional session to add after mastering New York AM Session if you are ready to scale trading on funded accounts. (All times are in EST)" — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:00, L18).

**20:00 Asia.** ("Asian session opens at 8:00 p.m. Asian session opens fairly most of the time unless it's very far away from the 6:00 p.m. If then I'll trade back to the 6:00 p.m." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~1:27, L67). Count: ("So, 6 p.m. one continuation, one reversion. Asian session, same thing. One continuation, one reversion." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~4:15, L163), i.e. ("This allows me to take four extra trades per day." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:33, L39). The preferred shape: ("8:00 p.m. you look for a continuation. The continuation ideally is in the direction of 6 to make it a stronger setup because it's also a reversion." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~30:03, L958). Window: ("Delete the 6 p.m. and still look for reversions to Asian. I'll trade for 90 minutes. So, until 9:30." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~6:10, L222); afterwards ("If you trade after 9:30, make sure you're trading with the trend. So, breakup structures instead of just displacements." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~27:13, L871). Targets: ("19 points is probably ideal, simply because there is basically zero volume in this session, as you can see. It only moves about 10 points at most." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~14:46, L619); ("The Asian opening is our lowest timeframe bias, where you can have a structural breakout here and send another assessment by 19 points and take another profit. The one at six is more of our long operation" — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~30:05, L1025). A continuation at the extreme is still allowed: ("Asian open, continuation long seems fine. You are buying at the absolute top of the past 6 hours, but it's still fine for just a continuation trade." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~20:26, L679). The FX Replay host adds a volume filter he attributes to JJ: ("if you're going to trade Asia, he says he really only likes to trade it if there's like a surge in volume and volatility." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~10:50, L417); not in his own words in this corpus. Which open is the anchor is decided by the first-move test (previous block).

**21:30 to 09:30 overnight limits.** ("you can see my overnights start at 9:30 p.m. That's just when I go to sleep." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~13:28, L456); ("I have an eval short at every single piece of structure to the left all the way back down to 9:30." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~19:43, L771); ("you can just get at least 10 evals placed. It won't hit all of them every time, but you could easily get at least five orders triggered overnight all in the direction of your higher time frame bias" — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~20:04, L784); ("it takes 3 minutes and it could get you through 10 of your 50 accounts" — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~21:04, L822); ("Allows me to take like 20 trades with no effort." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~16:18, L642); ("I would prefer to always have one account running overnight because there's obviously huge move overnight." — JJ, r5AhmGaFujU ~13:08, L421). The overnight book can replace Asia (("I could even skip the Asia session and just send them all overnight like I did last night" — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~11:51, L383)), ends at the cash open (("And I guess technically I finish at 9:30, so there would be one more entry here at 9:29." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~2:21, L108)), and its results vary by firm (("Overnight trades were all short. So, I had all of these ones execute mostly winning on E8. Then the trading pit about 50/50 and then FTMO, one win, a few more losses than wins" — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~4:36, L167)).

**Friday last hour.** ("there's a very large increase in volume in one direction every Friday in the last hour. You should check if you can reverse it because it's an unfair move." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~31:15, L1059); ("on Friday, I take a reversal at the end of the day on Friday, generally because institutions are exiting positions ahead of the weekend" — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~17:33, L730).

**Evaluation versus funded, by session.** The corpus does not give different windows per account type; it gives different uses. New York AM carries the large-target, higher-risk trades and most accounts (Lep139B5EbU L62; KhooqEQK9bA L316). The 2 p.m., 6 p.m. and Asia opens exist to use accounts not yet traded (l6iq0ljhxIo L240; PN1UKQMPb5M L911; KhooqEQK9bA L316) and 6 p.m./8 p.m. are the add-on ("if you are ready to scale trading on funded accounts" — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~0:00, L18). Evening targets are evaluation-sized, 19 points (KhooqEQK9bA L426, L619; 74CRg-mID5c L1025), while a funded account rides a reversion to a consolidation for ("$4,500 of profit" — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~2:39, L114) or a long overnight for 100 points (("So I sent a long position and was looking for 100 points in this account." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~4:01, L162)). An afternoon open that breaks structure the right way goes to the funded account, otherwise one evaluation trade (("If it had broken the structure upwards, it would have gone long in the funded account, but it broke downwards." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~7:48, L285)). Overnight limits are placed on evaluations (VY-bO35-tjE L771) and, when the overnight misses, evaluations are layered in the morning instead (VY-bO35-tjE ~18:30, L725, medium). A thin overnight forces aggression next morning: ("you'd have to be very aggressive in this next New York session if you have a lot of accounts, because you were only able to make one trade." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~7:41, L284). The distance to the anchor sets which account gets the trade: ("I have exactly 65 points in my favor towards that fair price." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~1:18, L73).

**Numbers.**

| Asia window | 90 minutes, 8:00-9:30 p.m.; trend-only after | Asia | (JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 L222, L871) |

| Extra trades from the evening | 4 per day | evening | (JJ, GMDUiamqgig L39) |

**Evolution.** March 2026 (3L8xdh3oPm4): 6 p.m. and 8 p.m. already on the session list. May (GMDUiamqgig): the dedicated 6 p.m./8 p.m. video, one continuation and one reversal each, presented as a funded-account add-on. June (xTxrtFKlQfc, aCOgfvL6lK8): the 6 p.m. open called "Asian session" in the recaps; the interview gives one-plus-three-or-four for every session. August (74CRg-mID5c, pwO9IAu7FUQ): 6 p.m. as the "long operation" and Asia as the 19-point evaluation bias. September (BLvsYJ4sqn8, l6iq0ljhxIo, HlWSP7ajgpQ, VY-bO35-tjE, KhooqEQK9bA): the session video repeats one-plus-one, sets the 90-minute Asia window, and the overnight limit routine is described in detail; 19-point targets. October (PN1UKQMPb5M, r5AhmGaFujU): 6 p.m. skipped some days; Asia replaced by the overnight book on some nights; "always have one account running overnight". Current: the September videos for the sessions, the October ones for how they fit the account book.

**Contradictions.** (1) Per-session trade counts: one continuation plus one reversion for 6 p.m. and 8 p.m. in both dedicated videos (May and September) against one plus three or four for every session in the June interview; the dedicated videos are the more specific and the later. (2) The dossier says the 18:00 and 20:00 sessions appear from May 2026; they are on the March list (3L8xdh3oPm4 L296). (3) The dossier says no public codification covers them yet; the two session videos are themselves the codification, down to the window (90 minutes to 21:30) and the anchor test. (4) 6 p.m. "always opens fairly" is retracted in the same sentence (BLvsYJ4sqn8 L385) and quantified as 98% three days later (l6iq0ljhxIo L633). (5) The 2:00 p.m. figure in pwO9IAu7FUQ (~7:48, L285) is spoken in a week he narrates in Pacific time (the extractor's note on ~1:32, L80 of the same video records a 6:29 a.m. PST pre-open); whether it means 2:00 p.m. ET or PT is not resolvable from the captions.

**Replication parameters.**

<!-- target: Section 2 -->
### From the primary corpus: the news trades (08:30, 10:00, 14:00 FOMC, overnight)

**The premise.** News is the one thing that moves fair price: ("News changes fair price, but opening session does not. So, you do have to be careful if some sort of news comes out and changes fair price." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~21:26, L740); he keeps Forex Factory on one monitor for it (GMDUiamqgig ~5:28, L191). Two kinds: ("There's two types of news. There is expected and then there is unexpected. So the expected news, this is 8:30 a.m. Red folder news EST. Unexpected is stuff like tweets, speeches, things like that." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~12:41, L460). Expected news is priced in because the print is close to the forecast: ("If the news is expected, meaning it's the 8:30 scheduled red folder news, usually happens twice a week, CPI, PPI, whatever. This is already priced in." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~13:11, L475), 90% of the time (~13:28, L484) and later 99% (~28:55, L956); ("They already have a forecast for the news. And if it doesn't deviate too much from the forecast, it will simply reverse. Even if it deviates, in the long run it still reverses. So this is the best possible trade you can make with my strategy." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~12:20, L436). So on a news day the anchor is the candle before the release: ("We believe I believe that the fair price is the candle before news. And just because news came out, news is already priced in on average." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~29:04, L992); ("I always mark out the candle before news. So this will be the 29 candle." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~29:51, L1018); ("This is the price before the news because it was the opening of the 8:30 a.m. candle, so I think it's fair." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~7:52, L290); "the top of the pre-news candle" (l6iq0ljhxIo ~11:37, L399). With two releases the earlier one sets it: ("Now, the the fair price is going to be the pre-8:15 news because a move away from 8:15 is unfair, which is this move right here, and then the the 8:30 there's no there's no fair price here because it was already determined by the 8:15 candle." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~5:23, L209). The pre-news price outranks the 09:30 open when price returns to it at the open (74CRg-mID5c ~9:08, L332). Unexpected news moves the anchor to the consolidation after it and is not faded: ("Since the news was unexpected, it changes the fair market price. Then there is more consolidation, so this level down here would be the fair price, not the one up here, because this is unexpected. The unexpected changes the fair price. This is expected, so it doesn't change the fair price" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~21:56, L827); the same for Trump tweets (CzxSgYujDxs ~16:50, L566) and for a news crash he calls "an unfair move that you shouldn't reverse" (4IGbxmKJ4BU ~6:31, L307); the tells are a volume spike "at a fairly random time of day" (4IGbxmKJ4BU ~6:42, L315) and candle size, "a 106 point candle at 10 10:12 a.m. which never happens. So clearly a tweet." (MVP7X-3v8xk ~43:15, L1172). What he reads is occurrence and scheduling, then actual versus forecast, US only (8lbkj0PF1uM ~3:07, L126; ~11:02, L367): ("It was forecast to be 0.7%. The actual release of this news was 0.7%. So, they already knew what the outcome was going to be. So, why would the fair price of Nasdaq futures change? Well, they shouldn't. Therefore, this entire move down is unfair" — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~3:49, L147). The rule in one line: ("do the continuation if it is extremely unexpected, and reversion if the news came out as expected." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~9:10, L310).

**The 08:30 procedure.** The day starts there for the release: ("I'll start at 8:30 a.m. Eastern just because there's usually news there." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~0:09, L60); "news" in his videos means the 08:30 red folder, with 09:45 and 10:00 releases only to be avoided while in a trade (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~28:40, L979). Step one, in the March to June videos, is a continuation of the news candle treated like a session open: ("News came out at 8:30. News is basically like another session open. You can take continuation long since news was green. Pretty much same trade as the open." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~4:03, L163); ("And then after that I'll take usually like three to four trades trying to revert that move. So if news came out it was very very bullish, then I would trade multiple times probably three or four evaluations trying to short back to the pre-news price" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~15:58, L610). By the July recording and September it is an evaluation tactic he mostly skips: ("sort of an aggressive tactic to pass evaluations quickly. Um but I sort of stay away from that mostly. Now all I'm going to do is try to revert towards the pre-news price and again stop after three losses." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:10:50, L2238); ("Continuation of news is fine. I usually only do I don't really do continuation of news anymore actually, unless it's a huge candle." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~11:37, L399). The size threshold is discretionary both ways: a 92-point candle was too much for pre-market volume (EyR3i2GxwyA ~10:59, L392), while on FOMC ("I did 50 point stop, 76 point take profit just cuz the candle was huge. Maybe I could have even sized down lower than that, but I just saw 157 point red candle and I got in short as soon as it closed." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~5:42, L219). Step two is the reversion to the pre-news price, the trade he rates highest: ("Remember my strategy, 8:30 a.m. we have news, which is priced in, trade a reversion back to the pre-news price." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~5:57, L222); ("If I were trading on a real account, without testing, I would only trade news reversals. AM session reversals are perfect, but I would bet much more on these news reversals and less on New York ones, because the news ones are very profitable." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~12:33, L443); "reversals are my strongest trade" (KhooqEQK9bA ~15:25, L645); his first news reversal lost and it is now his most profitable trade (MtBZeAkYH8Q ~0:45, L57); ("So pre-news are the strongest reversions in general. That's how I won 20K." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~18:31, L618). The March 2026 checklist: ("So I'll go low time frame continuation, high time frame reversion, close above break of structure, and points in your favor." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~29:51, L1018), where points in favour means not entering at the fair price (~30:25, L1037). The trigger is a break of structure or the first displacement back toward the pre-news price, entered early on a sub-minute chart: ("I only dialed 3825 for all of them, but you could even get in from this one. He's a bit aggressive. I like to wait for a bit of confirmation, because sometimes it can shoot up like crazy just because of how big this candle is." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~22:11, L836); ("just the first displacement candle that I saw, targeting 100 points exactly for a $30,000 win with 15 contracts back to the pre-news price." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~10:28, L375); an A+ break of structure back to the pre-news line (HlWSP7ajgpQ ~10:58, L377) and a re-entry on the next break after a lost first attempt (gjWiGBiVis0 ~15:09, L522); a wick rejection for an early entry because news reversions are stronger (PN1UKQMPb5M ~12:45, L410); ("You would have to do it on sort of like the 5 or 15-second chart. for news reversions and the opening reversions." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~14:57, L505); ("Usually, I don't enter positions fully on news candles just because it is not the strongest setup at all." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~12:02, L411). Sizing: the evaluation pair is 38/25 (KN7j6NXXAio L836), the replay pair 750/500 ("Sell one. Okay, exactly 750 to fair price. Perfect. 500 here and then just play that one out." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~12:32, L358; medium), funded accounts are sized up ("I always size up on the news inversions cuz they are the strongest ones. News is literally priced in every single time, so there's no reason to not do this one." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~10:50, L387; medium), and the pair is fitted per account (DEkzU8gqX-o ~11:00, L403). Nothing is taken against the reversion once it starts: ("I don't like opening longs because I'm my goal now is to revert it to pre-news. So, I only want to take continuations to pre-news." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~41:35, L1128). Failures are shown too: ("Continuation was good, but the reversion unfortunately was not. I sent one off this break of structure and then one off this break of structure. Neither of them reverted, which was tragic." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~29:00, L894); the first attempt on the second news day of the 80-trade sample lost (KN7j6NXXAio ~25:26, L953). ("This this works on every news. You can go back and look. Um back test it." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~30:49, L1050; medium) is the only universal claim.

**Which releases and other clock times.** Red folder at 08:30, CPI and PPI about twice a week (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L475, L1750); during CPI itself he waits: ("I don't usually take trades during the CPI. I'm only going to look for a reversal, since it's much stronger." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~7:52, L290). A 10:00 release is handled identically (gjWiGBiVis0 ~14:50, L512): ("we had news at 10. News right there at 10. That creates the unfair move away. So, I'm in the reversion as soon as we displaced long above here. Boom. Send in five contracts. $6,000 win on a funded account with FTMO." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~31:19, L963). FOMC at 14:00: ("FOMC is always priced in so well, like we literally know if the rate is changing or not, so um when it when it comes out and it's exactly as forecasted, then this entire move down is unfair." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~5:28, L211); ("I'm just shorting back to the pre-news price right there. And then you could even do it again. Two attempts at that one." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~15:33, L539); the continuation rule read onto it ("2 p.m. FOMC comes out. The wick rejects long and it closes short as displacement. So yes, that is a continuation trade. The candle is 20 points and since it's less than 25, we would do a 2538." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~14:44, L498). A 07:00 news candle makes a setup worse but he still entered on a funded account (HlWSP7ajgpQ ~11:49, L404). Orange folder: ("The orange folder is more like a larger timeframe reversal, if you will. I mean, if it makes a move. Usually, it doesn't" — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~12:27, L477), but faded when it does (PN1UKQMPb5M L410). Evening releases are assumed away in his 18:00 backtest: ("Obviously, if news is released between 6:00 and 8, that changes things. I'm just going to assume for the point of this whole video that there is no news cuz it's kind of hard to back test and know like where news is." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~4:54, L183). Unscheduled items end or redirect the session: a 9:20 headline made the open not fair (g68HHbrIBBg ~6:21, L258); ("Trump's tweets, the day is practically over after that. If you want to trade, trade with the trend, especially after a tweet." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~14:12, L596); a live speech stops trading for the session (PN1UKQMPb5M ~6:23, L220); ("So, 10 EST, the dude is speaking. So, probably not going to be taking any trades at 10:00 a.m. afterwards" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:36:37, L3026); a tweet of about 300 points hit a stop (Lep139B5EbU ~10:29, L375). A mid-session tweet re-anchors to its consolidation and he trades back to it after a structural break (74CRg-mID5c ~31:10, L1061); he keeps a second playbook for tweets that is not on YouTube: ("I have another news rich strategy where I would be taking longs down here back up to the consolidation after the Trump tweet." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~10:42, L353); "tweets are traded a lot differently" (UVKVSWKFlvo ~8:55, L324); intraday fair-price changes are a mentorship topic (Lep139B5EbU ~5:07, L200). Earnings are drift, not reversion (CzxSgYujDxs ~17:28, L586; medium).

**News drift.** ("news drift is a commonly studied phenomenon that goes with unexpected news. Pretty much just got this from Google. What it means is financial asset prices continue moving in the direction of a surprise piece of news." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~2:40, L112); it runs over the next few hours (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~14:51, L526) or overnight (RnwOqmEF6Rk ~2:49, L137). It is the theory behind his biggest day (RnwOqmEF6Rk ~1:51, L105) and he sizes its edge at 1 to 2%: ("So, the only way to capitalize on something that's like a very small 1 to 2% edge, like it's not a crazy edge. You can't build a strategy around news drift. Um but you can make money off of it. You have to risk a very large amount of money." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~3:07, L147). Entry is on the consolidation after a surprise, with session and bias confluence: ("if news is red, instead of entering a continuation here, you'll enter a continuation as soon as it starts consolidating." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~11:16, L374; medium); a long when news broke structure up and the Asian open, the 6 p.m. open and the higher-time-frame bias agreed (EyR3i2GxwyA ~14:54, L521); a ceasefire at 8:50 plus a green open and break of structure read as a long drift (xTxrtFKlQfc ~10:19, L373). Management is the opposite of the 1.5R trade: ("basically like incredibly large takeprofit stop loss. just let it run and see what happens. More often than not, it's going to win." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~31:24, L858); a big stop so a stop-out does not cost the expectation (~44:18, L1200); ("It's going to hit four and then I'll just exit because that's when the propers make you exit." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~31:06, L850); ("So I just took a small news drift trade right there for a nice little profit on the live account of 1,300." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~10:53, L391); on a funded account in profit a drift trade up 50 points was closed flat at the bell (xTxrtFKlQfc ~11:01, L396; `stops_psychology.json`).

**Break-even rule.** A scheduled release while in a trade is one of only two exceptions to his no-management rule: ("So, break even, I usually only go break even what if I'm in a trade and then and news is coming out. It's not the best to be in a trade when news is coming out, but go break even just so you don't get wicked out. And when a new session is opening." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~7:16, L284; `risk_sizing.json`); about 95% of trades are never moved (-lxNWJGWtbg ~8:57, L362; `risk_sizing.json`); applied in replay: ("And then news comes out. I would move stop to break even, but I'm in draw down, so I don't need to let it play out." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~6:18, L193). He also stopped trading when a speech began mid-reversion (PN1UKQMPb5M ~14:26, L460).

**Which accounts.** News reversions go to every funded account and the evaluations: ("News is always discounted in the price. I'm going to reverse the news with all my funded accounts." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~17:46, L668); the 10:00 reversion in the week-in-my-life video (uploaded 2026-10-04) went to an FTMO funded account at five contracts (PN1UKQMPb5M L963), then ("Three contracts, $3,400 win. What else we got? We got number 256. See if I can find it over here. I have a lot of eval. There it is. Same thing. Two contracts, $1,600 win. Number 745." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~31:39, L973) and ("So almost just won like $13,000 in 2 minutes. So already done trading for the day, which is great just in one move." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~32:18, L992); the $30,000 reversion was 15 contracts (EyR3i2GxwyA L375); on the Friday he sent many accounts mid-candle (PN1UKQMPb5M ~33:02, L1014; medium). The three-or-four reversion attempts are placed on evaluations (aCOgfvL6lK8 L610), the continuation is an evaluation tactic (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L2238), drift goes to the live account (q8PXXvY9lS0 L391) or a funded account in profit (xTxrtFKlQfc L396), and on funded accounts the instruction for news days is ("Literally, take any offset input you see towards the fair price." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~4:23, L196). He knows some firms "prohibit trading news" but names none (S8fSNbPFuLA ~9:02, L379). A student repeats the premise: ("one of our best strategies is news reversion. So, we assume that news is priced in." — other (Henry, student), r5AhmGaFujU ~2:56, L119). The FX Replay host restates the 08:30 reversion second-hand (SNO1wqJTq5A ~11:25, L436) and the Vai host avoided releases altogether (c61c4CxTpYI ~4:26, L139).

**Evolution and what is current.** March 2026 (3L8xdh3oPm4): 08:30 red folder, anchor the 08:29 candle, five-condition checklist, reversion "works on every news". April (Lep139B5EbU, RnwOqmEF6Rk): double-release rule; drift defined, 1-2% edge, not a strategy. May (8lbkj0PF1uM): dedicated news video, expected = reversion, extremely unexpected = continuation on consolidation, US only, check actual versus forecast. June (UVKVSWKFlvo, MVP7X-3v8xk, xTxrtFKlQfc, aCOgfvL6lK8, CzxSgYujDxs): continuation first then reversion, 750/500 in replay, break-even on news, drift held to 16:00, FOMC a favourite, tweets re-anchor. July (EyR3i2GxwyA, 7KJOh2NF-lQ, KN7j6NXXAio, KHEQ5g55dQ4 recording): 100-point funded targets, size up on news, "best possible trade", 38/25 on evals with confirmation, continuation called an evaluation tactic, stop after three losses. August to October (pwO9IAu7FUQ, g68HHbrIBBg, 74CRg-mID5c, KhooqEQK9bA, l6iq0ljhxIo, PN1UKQMPb5M): FOMC faded twice, all funded accounts, orange folder, tweets end the day, no continuation unless huge, 5/15-second entries, 10:00 reversion across FTMO, a second funded account and evals. Current (September-October 2026): reversion to the pre-news candle on every account, three to four attempts, three-loss stop, continuation only on a huge candle, nothing after a speech or tweet, drift (last shown in July) as a separate large-stop trade on live or in-profit funded accounts.

**Contradictions.** (1) Continuation of the news candle: first trade of the day (UVKVSWKFlvo L163, aCOgfvL6lK8 L610, June) versus dropped unless huge (l6iq0ljhxIo L399) and an evaluation tactic he avoids (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L2238); inside l6iq0ljhxIo he still labels a 20-point FOMC candle a continuation trade at 25/38 (L498) three minutes after L399. The dossier's Section 2 line on the 08:30 news (one continuation, then three or four reversions) and ASSUMPTIONS row 24 reproduce the June version. (2) Size on news: "3825 for all of them" (KN7j6NXXAio L836) versus "I always size up on the news inversions" (EyR3i2GxwyA L387; medium) versus 100 points on 15 contracts (L375); the evaluation and funded rules differ and the funded stop is never stated. (3) Entry timing: "Usually, I don't enter positions fully on news candles" (HlWSP7ajgpQ L411) versus "just the first displacement candle that I saw" (EyR3i2GxwyA L375) versus "take any offset input" (KN7j6NXXAio L196). (4) Forecast accuracy 90% versus 99% in one episode (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L484, L956); "works on every news" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L1050) versus the Wednesday double failure (PN1UKQMPb5M L894) and the lost first attempts (KN7j6NXXAio L953, gjWiGBiVis0 L522). (5) Drift: "You can't build a strategy around news drift" (RnwOqmEF6Rk L147) versus drift trades in June and July (MVP7X-3v8xk, xTxrtFKlQfc, q8PXXvY9lS0). (6) The anchor is "the 29 candle" (3L8xdh3oPm4), "the opening of the 8:30 a.m. candle" (74CRg-mID5c) and "the top of the pre-news candle" (l6iq0ljhxIo): the first two are one print; the third is the candle high. (7) Dossier Section 6 says news-day rules were not found and Section 10 lists 8lbkj0PF1uM as not yet extracted; both are superseded. (8) Dossier Section 9 says the 08:30 trade is unavailable on MyFundedFutures; the corpus shows him sending news reversions to "all my funded accounts" and names FTMO and evaluations (the $3,400 account's firm is not in the captions), but never says which firms are excluded.

## 3. Entry rules

| Rule | Detail | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Chart | NQ, 1-minute | **JJ** |
| Trigger | either a break of structure (a close beyond a wick that is lower/higher than the one candle before and the one after) or a displacement candle, both pointing at the target; the two together are the strongest case; he never separates MSB from BOS ("Um for the entry, I look for a displacement or a break of structure and a close just depending on the volume and what session I'm trading." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~3:16, L140; "When there's a wick down here that is lower than the two candles previous, the candle before and the candle after. If the wick is lower and then a candle comes in here and it breaks the structure, then I will take a short position." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~6:54, L283) | **JJ** |
| Displacement candle | body larger than the previous candle's body, close beyond the previous candle's wick, previous candle of the opposite colour ("what I use for a displacement candle means the body of the candle must be larger than the body of the previous candle. And it also must close below the wick." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~5:22, L236; "a displacement also has to displace a candle of the opposite color." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:11:26, L2256). The 20% counter-wick test is the fxreplay presenter's own mechanisation ("for my testing specifically, I've tried to focus mostly on like the decisiveness of the candle." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~4:06, L200) | **JJ**; 20% rule **3rd** |
| Setup grades | A+ = break of structure (March 2026 version: both biases aligned plus break and close); A = displacement, traded on every account; B = biases opposed, never traded; displacement entries go to evaluations, breaks of structure to funded accounts ("Basically, an A+ configuration is a structure break. A configuration A is a shift, and a configuration B is something you should never operate." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~3:48, L175; "Evaluations as well will be all of my displacement candle entries. Funded accounts I like to save for the breakup structure just because it's a much stronger entry." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~9:43, L369) | **JJ** |
| Direction, continuation | the colour of the session's opening 1-minute candle (and the opening 1-second candle), entered only when a candle closes through prior structure in that direction; taken only toward an open gap at 18:00/20:00 ("the entry criterion for continuation is practically just the color of the opening candle." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~5:28, L235; "1 second green, 1 minute green, clear break of structure, clear displacement." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~0:42, L51) | **JJ** |
| Direction, reversion | back toward fair value from premium (short) or discount (long) | **JJ** |
| Cadence | one continuation plus three to four reversions per session; "at least 10 transactions per day during the morning in New York" ($1.6M video, KN7j6NXXAio ~2:02, L111); "10 to 20 per day" (AxP-cg50TdM ~20:37, L710); one trade per account per day (AxP-cg50TdM ~27:35, L940); in March-April 2026 three trades a session with a fourth only for an A+ (Lep139B5EbU ~4:54, L193) | **JJ** |
| Management | reversions: static stop and target, no break-even; continuations: closed at break even if the unfair move does not materialise, exited on a closed structure break against the position or when the bias flips ("if it ever breaks structure against me, I will exit." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~17:59, L622; "most likely get out break even right here just because the initial unfair move doesn't seem to happen." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~7:03, L254) | **JJ** |
| Entry timing | on the 1-minute close ("Always waiting for candle closes on displacement and breakup structures." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~18:27, L636), with three exceptions: mid-candle when waiting would leave under ~30 points of room, limit orders at the break level or at structure overnight, and mid-candle layering of additional accounts (code: next bar open) | **JJ** |

In his own words (Titans of Tomorrow clip): phase one is time, "the minute
any session opens, I'm ready to take a continuation"; phase two starts
"when volume starts dying out, it will usually start to consolidate ... I
will trade when it breaks structure back towards the opening price", "as
long as it's before 11:00 a.m."; the signal is binary, "Either it broke
structure or it didn't. I try to take the discretion out of it just
because I'm trying to take so many trades"; execution: "as long as it
closes below the structure that it broke, I'm good with entering". He does
not use chart patterns ("most of it is artificial when market makers are
balancing their inventory") or institutional-footprint signals.

Additional detail from the `entries` research angle (44 facts): the MSB is a
**close** past the wick high/low of the recent price leg (AndrewFXTD's
codification), BOS is the same test with the trend; the fxreplay
presenter's mechanical rule is the wick test (fib 0 / 0.2 / 1) with size
left discretionary, which inverts JJ's own emphasis: his mandatory test is
body larger than the previous candle's body and a close beyond its wick,
with a huge wick disqualifying by judgment; entries are market orders after the displacement close; he says
"I'm just going to enter every A setup I see. Some B+ I'll enter. Just
cuz, you know, I'm trying to trade all my accounts." (3L8xdh3oPm4 ~22:46,
L784) and routes displacement entries to evaluations and breaks of
structure to funded accounts (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~9:43, L369);
fxreplay's filtered variant (skip the first 3 minutes for continuations,
reversions only in the first ~30 minutes, avoid 10:00-11:00 and
15:00-16:00) reported about 62% win rate and PF 2.46; joetroyer's
continuation variant waits for a retrace to the fair line instead of
entering on the displacement close; a May 2026 video (2026-05-18) covers the
18:00 and 20:00 sessions ([GMDUiamqgig](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GMDUiamqgig)).

Unpublished and therefore assumptions in the code (see `ASSUMPTIONS.md`):
the structure lookback (his swing is a wick lower/higher than the one
candle on each side, a 3-candle pivot, not the code's 3/3), the
minimum-room filter (15 points floor, 30 for a 38-point target), the
opening-candle body threshold (25 points, not range), layering across
accounts (he does not hold one position at a time), and whether open
trades are flattened at 11:00. Grade-A entries are taken on evaluations.

<!-- target: Section 3 -->
### From the primary corpus: the continuation and reversion phases

**What a continuation is.** The first trade of every session is a trade in the direction of the opening candle, read as the net of the overnight orders that execute at the open: ("the first trade I take is a continuation of the influx of volume, usually because it's just a sum of the overnight orders that are executed on the opening candle, and it creates a very good bias." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~0:56, L60). His own definitions of both words: ("by reversion, I just mean coming back to the opening price. And by continuation, I just mean like this this series of green candles or the series of gray candles for this continuation." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~18:10, L632). The direction rule is the colour of the opening 1-minute candle and nothing else: ("The only way you want to look for a continuation is short because the first candle was red, which means the overnight orders were mostly short. So, you're going to look for shorts." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~11:30, L412); ("the entry criterion for continuation is practically just the color of the opening candle. Because I'm taking an unfair trade, i.e., a move away from the fair price, I want to get in and out literally as quickly as possible." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~5:28, L235); ("even though high time frame bias is short, opening candle is green, so I only like to take continuations in the opening candle direction." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~4:49, L186). From August 2026 he also reads the opening 1-second candle: ("Um, 38 points as usual, green opening candle, uh, green 1 second candle, structure breakout, shift, everything you need for a long continuation seems to be in place." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~4:22, L187); ("1 second green, 1 minute green, clear break of structure, clear displacement. High time frame is short, but obviously you can go against that if the opening candle is so strong." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~0:42, L51); ("The opening candle wasn't too biased. There were pretty large wicks on both sides, but uh opening 1 second was red." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:27:29, L2747).

**The trigger inside the continuation.** The colour alone is not an entry; the candle (or the next one) must close through prior structure: ("So the first trade literally the color of the first candle as long as it breaks structure of the previous candles then I'm good then I'm good to enter and I will do a 38 point take profit and a 25point stop-loss for this trade." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~4:31, L210); ("There is no structural break, so there are no continuations until the first one we get here." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~16:57, L636); ("opening candle is green, but it's not closing above any structure. So, I am long biased, marking out the pre-open price as the fair price, but I'm not going to take continuations because it didn't close above any structure. Not going to short because the opening was green." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~8:34, L308). A wick that rejects the opening direction, or two large wicks, cancels the trade or defers it to the second candle: ("there's a a huge wick rejecting shorts and it means the open's fairly priced so I'm just not going to take anything for a continuation on this day." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~2:38, L117); ("I didn't go into the opening candle because the wicks were quite large on both sides. So I waited for the second candle to close. It closed below the first candle, which was great." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~10:31, L374). A tiny opening body means no bias and no continuation: ("the first candle was extremely unbiased because the body was literally four points. So, that alone just tells me that the market doesn't really have a strong bias going into the session" — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~2:58, L128). Follow-up continuation entries are allowed on each further break inside the window: ("And remember to follow up within the first 5 minutes whenever it breaks the structure in your favor." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~36:24, L1234).

**How long it lasts and what ends it.** Two conditions coexist. The time condition is five minutes: ("So, I'll take a continuation away from the fair price. After that, which is just the first 5 minutes. After that, I'm only looking for a reversal. And the reversal takes the next 85 minutes to complete a 90-minute session in total." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~2:28, L127); ("I usually don't take them after the first 5 minutes. This is in the sixth minute." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~30:43, L1046); ("it's obviously too late to take continuations now because the original unfair move never happened. And if it doesn't happen within the first 5 minutes, it's not happening." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~1:47, L87). The volume condition is the end of the "unfair move": ("when the market's going to open, there's going to be an initial unfair move which creates a continuation. As soon as the unfair move stops, then I'm looking for a reversion." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~15:07, L510); ("So, just keep shorting until the original unfair move is done. After the unfair move is done, look for reversion." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~23:56, L790); the stop of the move is read from opposite-colour candles ("Pretty much as soon as the unfair move up stops, then I'm looking to revert. And you can kind of see it stop there with the those two red candles." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~14:08, L451) or from consolidation ("when volume starts dying out, it will usually start to consolidate. From there, there's obviously that pack of consolidation. I will trade when it breaks structure back towards the opening price as the second phase." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~20:34, L765). In June 2026 the time limit was fifteen minutes: ("technically a continuation trade as well just because the opening candle was red and we're still within 15 minutes." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~6:22, L236); ("it is 16 minutes after the open I usually stop continuations after 15 minutes" — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~10:50, L390; `stops_psychology.json`). The fxreplay presenter's "first 10-15 minutes" ("he generally looks for continuations kind of away from the fair value the first 10-15 minutes, um, after the open" — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~6:48, L287) is the host's restatement, not his words.

**Continuation management (he does manage these).** If the unfair move does not materialise the trade is closed flat: ("most likely get out break even right here just because the initial unfair move doesn't seem to happen. So we get out break even and now assume 6 p.m. is fair." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~7:03, L254); a closed break of structure against the position is an exit ("if it's on an uptrend in my in my favor in a continuation and it's forming structure on its way up, if it ever breaks structure against me, I will exit." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~17:59, L622); a bias flip is an exit ("Had to exit the continuation trade obviously because the bias is short now." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~14:16, L455).

**Continuation sizing by opening candle.** Continuations always use the fixed pair because the room is unknown: ("in continuations I'll always do 38 to 25. That should be 25. Because you don't know how many points you have in your favor." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~19:33, L674). The pair widens to 76/50 when the opening candle exceeds 25 points: ("I would use the standard 3825 only because the opening candle was only 7 points. Remember, I would adjust the size to 5076 if the opening candle was over 25 points." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~18:55, L656); ("Friday, the opening candle is green, 40 points, more than 25, so I will use a stop of 50 points and a take profit of 76 points." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~16:29, L631). The measure is the body: ("Opening candle is red, so you would short. That is it. 3825 as usual because the candle body is 25 points, less than 25 points." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~3:50, L152). The rule applies to every session's own opening candle, including 6 p.m.: ("Opening candle of 36 points. So do 50 stop 76 take profit." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~21:59, L730). The June version was contract halving rather than a wider pair: ("first one, opening candle 33 points. I'm automatically going to do half my normal contracts and it's going to buy just because opening candle is green" — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~0:41, L44). He does not run continuations to 100 points: ("I usually don't go for 100 on continuations" — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~10:17, L364).

**Evening sessions: the gap decides.** At 6 p.m. and the 8 p.m. Asian open the continuation is taken only toward an open gap, and not at all when the open is already fair: ("Remember, Asian trades in the direction of the 6:00 p.m. open most of the time. 6:00 p.m. trades in the direction of the gap and then back to the 6:00 p.m. price. So, the 6:00 p.m. price is a very good indicator of the fair price for the evening." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~12:15, L401); ("For the continuation, I'm not going to do it away from the gap." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~1:16, L71); ("I wouldn't take any continuation trades for Asian because it's opening at what I think is a fair price." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~1:41, L74).

**What a reversion is.** ("All I'm looking to do is revert back towards what I claim to be the fair price, which is sort of what fair pricing theory is, just a return to the opening price." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~5:06, L228); ("Now, for reversion, I just trade back to the pre-open price, as always. Entry would just be off of a break of structure." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~2:29, L111). The trigger is any break of structure or displacement candle pointing back at fair price once price is far enough away: ("basically every time it moves more than 15 points from the right price in either direction, I'm going to send a reversal." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~11:20, L405); ("Just pretty much every single displacement you can find back towards this price." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~6:52, L256); ("Anytime you see a red displacement, revert to 6 p.m. here, revert to 6 p.m. here." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~28:22, L906). The direction is always toward the anchor, from either side; the target is the anchor itself or a fixed 38 points when the room allows: ("Usual target 38 points." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~5:52, L223). Longer reversions exist on smaller accounts: ("I trade reversions on my 50K accounts. If I see a move up, then I can trade a reversion to it for like 50, 75, 100 points. On a 150K, I probably wouldn't even take this sort of trade." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~13:38, L478); ("Um I tend not go for more than 100 as my longest trades, though." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~6:22, L236).

**Minimum room.** The floor rose over the year. April 2026: the room had to exceed the target ("my target was 75 points, and I was more than 75 points above what I believe to be the fair price, which is the pre-open candle." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~3:59, L163). August 2026: 15 points is the hard floor and 30 the preference for a 38 target ("It's just that every time you see a green candle and you have at least 15 points in your favor, you'll probably want to take a reversal." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~22:49, L785); ("you get 15. No, you get 14.75. Wow, that's borderline. I wouldn't do it" — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~27:16, L932); ("Since there are 15 points, you can take it. I mean, there are 14 and a half. Uh, let's say 50/50, you either take it or you don't. Next up, we'll take another short one right here. No, there are only 13 points, so you can't take this one." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~35:59, L1220); ("she was also a little too close to go for 38. I usually want 30 points." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~19:19, L669); with 28 points he halves the target ("since it's 28, that's less than 30, so I'd use my 19.1 here, but obviously both are fine because it's about the same." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~22:20, L769). September 2026: 30 points for the 25/38 trade ("I wouldn't take anything if it's not 30 points away. So, I need at least 30 points in my favor to take this eval trade for a 2538." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~3:44, L147); ("I need at least 30 points to do the 25 38. Uh, so if you have have an account that needs 24, this is a really strong trade to take." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~16:05, L541). The target may overshoot fair price only a little: ("I generally like to have 80% of my profit target in my favor toward the open, which is the direction of my bias, and only 20% or less on the other side, because once it changes to the other side, the bias would be short and you don't want to trade against the bias." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~11:04, L471); ("as soon as price gets below fair price, its bias is going to be back towards it. And I don't want to be trading away from the bias for too long." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:22:33, L2596).

**When reversion is off.** At fair price nothing is traded: ("it's just chopping at fair price right now. Obviously, no reversions because it's at a fair price." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~10:37, L359); a break that comes after price has crossed the anchor is ignored ("We do get structure broken, but it's past the fair price. So, I'd sort of be done there." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~10:36, L374); a one-candle reversion is uncatchable ("Unfortunately, this perfect reversal happens in a single candle. So you really can't get in. There is no structure to break here either." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~10:07, L363). The anchor itself moves after a successful move away: ("Then if it ever makes a displacement, break of structure away from the open, like it does here, then I would adjust it to the most recent price." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~7:56, L299). Reversion attempts per session are three to four: ("And then after that I'll take usually like three to four trades trying to revert that move." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~0:26, L70), and in May he warned against more ("But, I would recommend don't look for too many reversions throughout the day." — JJ, 8lbkj0PF1uM ~5:31, L199).

**Numbers**

| What | Value | Applies to | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Continuation window | first 5 minutes of a session | NY open (KN7j6NXXAio); also stated in the 6 and 8 p.m. session video (BLvsYJ4sqn8); session unstated in 74CRg-mID5c | KN7j6NXXAio ~2:28 L127; 74CRg-mID5c ~30:43 L1046; BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~1:47 L87 |
| Continuation window, June 2026 version | 15 minutes | NY open | gjWiGBiVis0 ~6:22 L236; xTxrtFKlQfc ~10:50 L390 |
| Reversion window | the next 85 minutes, 90-minute session | NY 09:30-11:00 | KN7j6NXXAio ~2:28 L127 |
| Default pair | 38-point target, 25-point stop (also quoted 38.25) | continuations always; reversions with 30+ points of room | 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~19:33 L674; 74CRg-mID5c ~2:34 L115; l6iq0ljhxIo ~3:50 L152 |
| Wide pair | 76-point target, 50-point stop | opening candle body over 25 points | 74CRg-mID5c ~18:55 L656; KN7j6NXXAio ~16:29 L631; CzxSgYujDxs ~21:59 L730 |
| June 2026 wide-open rule | half the normal contracts | opening candle 33 points | MVP7X-3v8xk ~0:41 L44 |
| Half target | 19.1 points | room 28 points (below 30) | 74CRg-mID5c ~22:20 L769 |
| Minimum room, hard floor | 15 points (14.75 rejected, 13 rejected) | reversions, Aug 2026 | 74CRg-mID5c ~11:20 L405, ~27:16 L932, ~35:59 L1220 |
| Minimum room for 38 target | 30 points | eval 25/38 trade, Sep 2026 | BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~3:44 L147; l6iq0ljhxIo ~16:05 L541 |
| Target past fair price | at most 20% of the target | reversions | KhooqEQK9bA ~11:04 L471 |
| Reversion targets on 50K accounts | 50, 75, 100 points | 50K funded | C-_0QhKZmA4 ~13:38 L478 |
| Longest trade | 100 points | reversions; not continuations | gjWiGBiVis0 ~6:22 L236, ~10:17 L364 |
| Reversion attempts per session | three to four | each session | -lxNWJGWtbg ~0:26 L70 |
| Opening body that means no bias | 4 points | NY open | xTxrtFKlQfc ~2:58 L128 |

**Evolution.** March 2026: continuation = direction of the first candle, then "trade reversion because the initial unfair move is sort of over" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L794). April (Lep139B5EbU): targets 75 with 50 stop ("Second trade exact same thing 50 point stop, 75 point take profit." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~6:33, L247), room must exceed the target. June (gjWiGBiVis0, xTxrtFKlQfc): 15-minute continuation window, contract halving on a large open. July (KN7j6NXXAio, 7KJOh2NF-lQ): 5 minutes, 38/25 always on continuations, 76/50 above 25 points. August (74CRg-mID5c): 15-point floor, 30 preferred, 19.1 half target. September (BLvsYJ4sqn8, l6iq0ljhxIo, KhooqEQK9bA): 30-point floor for 38, 80/20 overshoot rule, "until the unfair move is done", break-even exit when the move fails. October (KHEQ5g55dQ4, PN1UKQMPb5M): the 1-second opening candle, continuation exit on a structure break against it. The October statements are current.

**Contradictions.** (a) 5 minutes (Jul-Sep) against 15 minutes (June) in his own words; the dossier and Section 10 item 1 present the 10-15 figure as third-party only. (b) The continuation is taken against the higher-time-frame bias at a daytime open (the 2 p.m. session in g68HHbrIBBg) ("It goes against the short bias in high timeframes, but it was a structural break. Good enough." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~4:31, L192) but in October requires it to align ("pretty much it's just going to be in the direction of the opening candle assuming it aligns with a high time frame bias that I have" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~3:57, L193), and at 6 p.m. is skipped against a gap bias ("we have the 6:00 p.m. opening, a good green candle, but I'm not going to buy because the bias is short to fill this gap." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~20:12, L698). (c) "the reversion, which is always a bit better" (MVP7X-3v8xk ~22:02, L610) against the October ranking where expected-news reversions are strongest and the open "is a little bit worse" (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~29:18, L968). (d) The dossier's Management row (none: fixed target, no partials) is wrong for continuations (break-even, structure-break and bias-flip exits above). (e) The code takes the continuation on the side of fair value price happens to be on; he takes it on the opening candle colour plus a structure close, which can differ on the second or third bar.

**Replication parameters**

| Name | Value | Status | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `continuation_end` | session start + 5 minutes (09:35) | confirmed | KN7j6NXXAio ~2:28 L127; BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~1:47 L87 |

<!-- target: Section 3 -->
### From the primary corpus: structure breaks, displacement candles, grading and entry timing

**Structure and its break, in his words.** Structure is a single-candle wick extreme: a wick lower (or higher) than the candle before and the candle after. ("it's basically whenever there's a wick just like that, and it's lower than these two. So, it's structure. Just wait for a close below it." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~10:18, L299); ("And structure is when a wick is taller than the two next to it, which we just get right here." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~3:21, L138); ("When there's a wick down here that is lower than the two candles previous, the candle before and the candle after. If the wick is lower and then a candle comes in here and it breaks the structure, then I will take a short position." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~6:54, L283); ("if you have a wick here and this candle breaks above the wick because this one is taller than the two next to it, it is structure. When this shoots up beyond it and closes, then the structure is being broken." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~6:13, L241). A monotonic run has no structure: ("There is no structure here, believe it or not, because the left one is always lower than the right one. So, no structure." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~13:29, L468). The break is a close, never a wick: ("Just wait for the structure to break. Wick, but no break. I want to close below." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~4:36, L148); ("As long as it closes below the structure that it broke, I'm good with entering and then the stop loss take profit are static." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~22:40, L836). A wick through a level weakens it: ("the structure is invalidated when it leaves a wick but doesn't close" — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~13:02, L498). Several untouched wicks at one level make the strongest break: ("There's a bunch of structure here. 1 2 3 four wicks that have not broken this level. So, it's outstanding structure. And this candle, very strong breakup structure, obviously." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:00:24, L1919). The structure must have formed before it can be broken: ("There's no structure being formed here that is broken. Structure is forming here and it's broken here." — JJ, GMDUiamqgig ~4:39, L166). A break candle with a large wick is a judgment call: ("There's a close below, but there's a huge wick. Um, I would not short that one probably." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~10:27, L303). No fact in this group has him using the term MSB or distinguishing it from BOS; the hosts do: ("market structure break is really if price is in a downtrend and you see a close above a previous, uh, kind of wick high." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~2:29, L148); ("breaks in structures and market structure breaks we use pretty interchangeably" — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~2:57, L163); ("It can It can be a BOS, chalk, or MSB." — host, JcW8Wjnw8ck ~1:52, L73; "chalk" is a mis-hearing of CHoCH).

**Displacement candle.** The definition hardened over the year. March 2026: ("Displacement candle usually, at least in my opinion the way I trade it, is a candle that's larger than the previous one and it doesn't really have any wicks." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~11:45, L420). July: ("Displacement candle is classified as when it's bigger than the previous one pretty much. Just very simple definition." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~5:21, L206); ("the condition for it to be a displacement candle without a doubt is that the body of the candle is larger than that of the previous one. This one is 10.0 larger, so it's an offset." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~6:35, L275), with the wick close as a preference ("This one, the body is bigger, but in my opinion, it didn't close below this wick here, and that's something I would prefer it to have" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~7:14, L298). August: ("There's a slightly large wick at the bottom, but the body is larger than the previous one and it closed below the wick of the previous one. So, step by step, that's displacement." — JJ, g68HHbrIBBg ~1:13, L74). September and October, the current form, three tests: ("entry criteria for a displacement candle is the body of the candle is larger than the body of the previous candle, and it closes above the wick of the previous candle." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~6:47, L260); ("what I use for a displacement candle means the body of the candle must be larger than the body of the previous candle. And it also must close below the wick." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~5:22, L236); ("a displacement also has to displace a candle of the opposite color." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:11:26, L2256); ("It has to displace this candle because it has to be a green displacing a red in just the way that I trade it." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~8:26, L298). He describes the test as binary: ("This is just displacements. Like this is not me picking and choosing anything. And it's literally like yes or no. Did it displace? Is the candle larger? Yes. Did it close below the wick? Yes. Yes. And then yes." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~13:19, L453). Two or three same-colour candles may be combined, with a subjectivity warning: ("You can add a bit of judgment by combining two red candles to represent a shift over a green candle. Like here, these one, two, three candles displace this green candle and then you could simply short sell. It's somewhat subjective" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~6:48, L283); but small candles never add up ("Remember that these three are not a displacement. Even these four working together do not displace this green candle. So there was no entry here." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~16:53, L586). A huge wick still disqualifies in practice ("This is a good displacement candle. This one's not cuz it's a huge wick and it's not very large compared to this one." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~5:57, L226), a displacement that broke structure the other way is void ("I would not be taking this green candle even though it is a little bit looking like a displacement just because it did break structure short, which is the opposite of what I want to see if I want to go long." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:24:13, L2647), and one that fails to break nearby structure is weak ("Almost. The body and the wick technically did close above. So, this is technically a displacement. However, is a very weak one cuz there's structure here and structure here. So, it failed to break structure long." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~2:38, L113). The 20% counter-wick test is the fxreplay presenter's own mechanisation and he says so: ("a decisive close you could say, "Okay, I don't want that counter wick to be more than 20% of that range." That can indicate that that's a really strong close." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~3:57, L195); ("the size of the candle is a little more discretionary. That's something that's a little harder to make a mechanical rule set around. So, for my testing specifically, I've tried to focus mostly on like the decisiveness of the candle." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~4:06, L200). JJ's own rule is the opposite emphasis: size relative to the previous candle is mandatory, the wick is judged by whether the close clears the previous wick. A break of structure need not be a displacement: ("This is not displacement because it did not close below this wick, but it is a perfect structural break right there. A top-tier setup." — JJ, 7KJOh2NF-lQ ~12:07, L429), though usually it is ("these are much stronger because it's just an extra confluence you can add, since to break the structure, it almost always has to be a displacement candle" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~7:27, L306). He puts the share of reversions that print a displacement at 95%: ("if it's coming back down, like 95% of the time, there is going to be a displacement. Sometimes it reverts in one candle. So, I can't really catch that." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~37:35, L1221).

**Grading.** March 2026 defined A+ by confluence of bias and trigger: ("You need both biases to line up, high time frame reversion and low time frame continuation to make an A+." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~5:21, L209); ("And then displacement plus break of structure. Break of structure and a close is an A+ setup. It's a lot more likely to win when that happens." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~5:27, L212); ("Um I am trading pretty much every displacement I see just because there's a slight bit of edge that I want to capture every single day in the market. So, displacement is usually an A and then B is when your biases are like going the opposite way." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~5:34, L216); ("This is an A setup. I love the break and close below. It's an A+. But yeah, I mean, I'm going to I'm just going to enter every A setup I see. Some B+ I'll enter. Just cuz, you know, I'm trying to trade all my accounts." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~22:46, L784). Being at fair price downgrades ("It'd be like a an A to B+ setup just because you're already at the fair price" — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~18:46, L652). His win-rate estimate for an A+ at 1:1.5: ("Maybe not 100, maybe like 70 to 80, but that's really good cuz it's 1 to 1.5" — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~22:33, L777). July 2026 reduced the grade to the trigger: ("Basically, an A+ configuration is a structure break. A configuration A is a shift, and a configuration B is something you should never operate." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~3:48, L175); ("it's a structure break. It's simply a more solid trade overall because the structure breakout is more confirming, it has a higher success rate, and that's what I trade live, right? But, in funding accounts, I try to make many trades per day" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~4:02, L183). August: ("you might only have one A++ configuration per day. I can definitely find 10 A configurations or 10 lower quality configurations that still align with my bias and still generate a positive mathematical expectation" — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~1:37, L97). October routes grade to account type: ("The second one is a breakup structure. Now, for breakup structure, it is pretty similar to a displacement entry, but it's slightly more profitable. The reason why I take displacements, even though they're less profitable, is because I have so many profit accounts that I'm trying to trade daily." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~6:19, L265); ("Evaluations as well will be all of my displacement candle entries. Funded accounts I like to save for the breakup structure just because it's a much stronger entry." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~9:43, L369); on a live account ("I would only trade breakup structure. Second of all, okay, and third, most likely only trade expected news reversions." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~28:23, L940); and in September ("I'm fine with taking weaker trades for evaluation since they're worth much less than funded accounts." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~11:40, L495). Context grades: ("when it uptrends so hard, especially in 6 p.m. breaker structure is infinitely stronger." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~8:20, L289); and the best setup of all is a continuation that is also a reversion ("Asian continuation plus the 6 p.m. reversion allin one. That's the strongest setup that there is." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~27:43, L886).

**Entry timing: close, mid-candle, limit.** The stated rule is the 1-minute close: ("I will. Yes. Always waiting for candle closes on displacement and breakup structures." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~18:27, L636); ("Uh, I try not to enter midcand. I'll wait until the close." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~1:12:54, L2301); ("It was a little bit too aggressive. It was midcandle at the very bottom. I was like 100% sure it was dumping, but nope. Should have waited for the close, so don't do that." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~2:17, L98); in July he dropped limit orders for closes ("for the structure breakout, I prefer it to close below rather than with a wick. Lately, she's had too many highlights, so this is a small change I've made. No more limits. Now, I'm just waiting for it to be finalized." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~15:02, L579; medium confidence, translated captions). The exceptions are specific. Mid-candle when the room would otherwise vanish: ("The only time I would enter mid-candle is if I see 38 points on this candle and I need to enter here." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~10:55, L465); ("The only time I will be aggressive is when it is so close to fair price that you have to be aggressive like this. It's technically not a displacement." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~17:21, L581); ("If I wait for it to close below the wick, then shoot, I only have 15 points in my favor. If I enter here though, I'm getting 22 and I have a 26 point trade. So, in my opinion, it's a little bit better to do that." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~17:30, L586). Otherwise wait for the structure: ("There's no need to enter two points early in a biased trade when you could sacrifice two points in your favor and simply wait for it to break the structure and confirm the trend in your favor." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~17:16, L601); unless no structure is near ("the reason is that there's no structure all the way back here. So, if you wanted to have a big 65- point trade, uh then obviously you wouldn't want to wait until it breaks the whole structure up here because then you'd only get 35 points." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~17:56, L623); ("so I have to enter by displacement. And if I'm forced to enter due to displacement, first of all, I want to enter very early, and second, I want a huge stop loss." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~18:09, L624). Limit orders persist in the fair-price zone (April), for the overnight book (July), at the structure level (August), on choppy days (September) and at the break level itself (October): ("So, I just had a limit somewhere in here. I got tapped right here." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~2:40, L132); ("around 8:00 or 9:00 p.m. m. When I'm about to leave the chart, I place limit orders to sell here, limit orders to sell there, and limit orders to sell there. Basically, I only place limit sell orders throughout the entire decline." — JJ, MIWsdldzJgQ ~7:08, L265); ("Okay, second attempt at longs, I just put a limit order on the structure." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~4:50, L188); ("opening candle red wick rejected longs break of structure. So really strong setup. I just had a limit. I zoom in really close. Limit right there at that break of structure. So boom, got tagged in." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~23:40, L735); ("So when it's a really choppy day like this, it's obviously hard to get displacement or break of structure entries because like by the time it makes this green displacement, it's already back to this area. So I can sometimes use limit orders." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~11:30, L394). He does not wait for a retrace: ("I don't like to miss out on moves. I just make my stop loss bigger." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~8:29, L310; `stops_psychology.json`). When layering accounts he goes mid-candle deliberately: ("Mid candle. As soon as it pushes above the wick of the previous candle, I'm just sending another one." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~16:45, L659); ("I'm extremely aggressive. I like early entries so I can send more accounts." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~2:29, L104). Situational overrides: all-time highs take only a structure break, not a displacement ("I'm going to wait for a break of structure. Uh, because we're at alltime highs, I want to wait for a break of structure to have it like really be ready to short. I don't want to short off displacement." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~1:26, L64); after losses the next entry must be a break ("And after two losses, I usually expect a structural breakdown. It simply makes it a more solid operation" — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~32:01, L1089); ("Had I waited for the second structural failure after the first one failed, as it is usually a good idea to wait for the second, that would have been a winning trade." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~8:06, L375). Layering: ("Then boom, huge displacement. Send the first one. Break a structure. Boom, send the second one. Breaking the structure here. Send this one. Break the structure. Send this one." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~1:25, L72); ("Layer in. Send another one there for sure. Then don't layer in draw down. So like don't take this. You could take that one cuz the first one loses." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~28:01, L920); ("Then you could send another one off this displacement here. I probably wouldn't though cuz it's the exact same level as the first entry." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~9:59, L347).

**Evolution.** Displacement: "no wicks" (Mar) to "bigger than the previous one" (Jul) to body-and-wick-close (Aug) to body, wick close and opposite colour (Sep-Oct, current). Grading: bias confluence plus trigger (Mar) to trigger only (Jul) to trigger routed by account value (Oct, current). Timing: resting limit orders (Apr, RnwOqmEF6Rk L132; Jul, MIWsdldzJgQ L265) to "No more limits" for structure breaks (late Jul, KN7j6NXXAio L579) to the stated close rule with the mid-candle, no-structure and layering exceptions (Sep-Oct, current), while limit orders are still used at the structure level (Aug, pwO9IAu7FUQ L188), on choppy days (Sep, HlWSP7ajgpQ L394) and at the break level (Oct, PN1UKQMPb5M L735).

**Contradictions.** (a) "Always waiting for candle closes" (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L636) against his own mid-candle entries in the same episode and the layering videos. (b) B is "never" traded (KN7j6NXXAio L175) against "Some B+ I'll enter" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L784); the later statement is the stricter one. (c) The dossier's displacement row attributes the 20% counter-wick to him via fxreplay; the host says it is his own mechanical choice. (d) The dossier's trigger row (a Market Structure Break (MSB) or Break of Structure (BOS) confirmed by a strong displacement candle) merges two alternative triggers into one; he treats displacement and break of structure as alternatives, with both present as the strongest case. (e) ASSUMPTIONS item 8 (pivot 3/3) and item 6 (body >= 0.5 ATR) are not what he describes; the pivot is 1/1 and the size test is relative to the previous candle.
