<!-- target: Section 6 -->
### From the primary corpus: psychology is fake, one-unit thinking and the tilt days

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 "Psychology is fake" is a claim about expected value.* The June 25 video states it: ("Every time a guru tells you, or you tell yourself, "I need to control my emotions. I lost that trade because of these feelings." It's just cope." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~0:12, L37); ("It's actually a math problem. You didn't really have the math set up for your specific trade, and that's what really caused you to lose the trade." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~0:30, L48); ("People telling you to fix your trading psychology is pretty much a cover for you not having an edge, and same with you telling yourself that." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~0:41, L55); ("Like, if you don't have an edge that you're able to replicate over time, then the psychology is not really going to do anything good for you, and and it's not going to do anything bad for you." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~0:48, L59). The mechanism: a tilted trade is a random trade, and a random trade is zero-EV with more variance: ("If you're tilting and you're placing completely random trades, that is literally break even. You're obviously going to have higher risk, higher reward conversely, but it's still that same break even expected value from a tilted trade." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~4:38, L198); ("The only thing bad about tilting and and psychology problems is the increased risk. And it's it should be pretty easy to dial that in in terms of account management." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~5:21, L224); ("Now, most traditional psychology problems are pretty much sizing problems in terms of trading. So, using too many contracts, too little contracts." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~2:04, L105; risk_sizing file). What replaces psychology is a plan whose deviation cost is known: ("And as long as you never deviate from your trading plan, then you'll never have psychology issues." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~2:37, L125); ("if your psychology can make you deviate from your trading plan, then you never really had a trading plan. You had sort of a trading suggestion" — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~2:51, L133); ("You need it to be extremely step-by-step so that you know, if I ever deviate, I know the math behind deviating." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~3:02, L140); ("Like when I personally get tilted or angry, I'm still going to follow the exact same trading plan. I'm just going to be more mad if I don't win." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~3:24, L153); ("Confidence is really what builds up trading psychology. Confidence just means that you know your edge is going to work out over time." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~1:18, L77). July restates it: ("As long as you follow a step-by-step trading plan, then psychology isn't real as long as you do it step by step." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~11:29, L477).

*1.2 The tilt rule is an account-routing rule.* The plan pre-assigns which accounts absorb a tilt: ("implement a trading plan, not a trading suggestion, where you go step by step, "If I ever tilt, I'm going to risk these accounts."" — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~8:15, L329); ("So, if you ever feel yourself going on tilt, you're going to revenge trade, you're going to FOMO, then at least send it in on an account that's worth incredibly less." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~6:09, L253; accounts_execution file); ("If I ever go on tilt, then if I ever go on tilt, I will take that entire account and I will full port the entire thing. Um the reason for that is it's only a thousand dollars worth of risk because I paid a thousand dollars for it. Basically, I just had to buy four evals and pass one." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~5:45, L238; eval_risk_math file); ("if you're going to tilt, don't do it first of all, but at least tilt on a new account, so you're only risking 500 for the same exact drawdown." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~12:06, L427; eval_risk_math file). October gives the arithmetic: ("If you're on Tilt, do not trade that account. You're risking $2,000 of your account to win whatever. So, it's break even. But if you go on Tilt, you buy 20 new evaluations for $100 each. That's the same $2,000." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~35:43, L1164); ("So, if you ever go into Tilt, do it on the eval, not the funded accounts." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~36:30, L1188). Near a payout the account is at its most valuable: ("as soon as you're close to a payment, your expected value increases considerably. Understood? So you need to operate in the most optimal way at that point in your career or account lifecycle." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~10:00, L372). Revenge sizing is named for what it is: ("You are deciding, actively deciding, to increase your variance when your expected value remains the same." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~2:26, L127); ("Perhaps if you earn more than 10,000 a month, it would be ideal to optimize your variance simply because you can replace lost accounts by having enough capital to do so." — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~2:32, L131).

*1.3 Where he says psychology does matter.* Two months before the "fake" video, rule five: ("Rule five, and probably the most important one of them all, is psychology related. $3,000 in a trade needs to feel like $3." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:24, L157); ("When a dollar amount carries such emotional weight, you're obviously going to start making emotional decisions. That makes total sense. You hesitate on bad setups, you move your stop loss, you size down when you shouldn't." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:30, L161); ("The number in your head starts running the trade instead of the strategy." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:37, L166); ("Once the trade is placed, the dollar amount is irrelevant." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:48, L173); ("You have a proven strategy, you have multiple accounts running, daily loss limits set on each one. The system is sound. The only thing left that can sabotage you at this point is your psychology." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:53, L176); ("That detachment does take time to build, but if you're still flinching when you pull the trigger, your emotions are costing you money." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~4:03, L183). The unit framing: ("The way I think about it, I'm not risking $3,000 per trade, I'm just risking one unit per trade. A predefined calculated risk is based on my account [music] and my edge." — JJ, RGYbTD1j628 ~3:41, L168; risk_sizing file, medium). Elsewhere: ("emotions destroy your edge. So, in trading and in poker, you never want to be making a decision when it's an emotional decision. You always want it to be backed up by at least some sort of scientific proof or data." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~12:58, L437); ("that is all assuming you're a break-even trader, and it's also assuming you don't have any psychology problems because tilting is definitely a problem and it's going to destroy your expected value." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~9:26, L340); ("Obviously, you do need to have like incredibly strong statistics and they need to be backed up over a long period of time or else it's not going to be true. And you obviously can't tilt." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~11:39, L432); ("if you blow three accounts or if you blow two accounts, even if you blow two accounts out of 20, your payout rate drops like exponentially and that's going to absolutely kill your your profit, right?" — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~11:51, L439); ("If you feel that emotion is influencing you, there is most likely something wrong with the operation and you should not have it." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~7:42, L358); ("You need to take your losses better than you take your wins, just because the inherent value of losing or the the emotional value of losing is two times as much as the absolute value of winning." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~22:11, L765); ("The copy trading very very bad for variance, and then variance obviously leads to tilt like 100%, even for myself." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~51:13, L1798; accounts_execution file). The detachment is credited to poker: ("I just felt like if I followed the optimal decision from a mathematic POV, I don't really care if I lose because at least I know I did it right. And then that mindset eventually is what transferred into my trading mindset" — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~11:15, L432).

*1.4 The tilt days.* Before the method: ("Before this clicked, I was strategy hopping every week, tilting after losses, and not following a strict risk management plan." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~0:16, L31). The $50K day: ("I had one tilt day where I blew literally 50K of funded account balances. Um basically, what I did was I lost 6K by trading on 15 NQ instead of MNQ. And that caused me to lose $6,000 in literally like 20 seconds." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~4:34, L176); ("After that, I decided to martingale, risk 12K, risk 24K, that sort of thing. And then just don't do that because that's how I lost 50K in one day." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~4:45, L182). The $60,000 day: ("I opened a 50,000 account. I raised it to 110,000. I was ready for a payment. I could have sat back and withdrawn a payment of 60,000 when I had 200,000 in my name." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~10:36, L392); ("I lost 23.7k in a single trade and then continued sending more trades consecutively." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~10:50, L400); ("but I kept sending that junk until I finally lost $60,000 in one day when, sadly, my net worth was $200,000." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~10:57, L404); ("Obviously, this is a $60,000 loss from one of my Apex funded accounts." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~11:25, L354); ("so yeah 60k down drain in one day. That was when my net worth was probably 200k." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~11:59, L370; results file; the same $60,000 and $200,000 as the August telling); ("I tried to force it, tried to end the day in profit because that's my goal right now." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~12:22, L381). Against both: ("I feel like I only tilted once in my entire life trading and then never have since." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~33:55, L1215); ("I know that I'm going to trade on even if I wanted to tilt, I would trade a different account because the account is worth a lot less." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~34:22, L1230). The admitted deviations after that are FOMO entries, not tilt: ("I saw these two red candles, and I kind of FOMO'd into a short position. Obviously not the best, I should have waited for the open." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~5:15, L217); ("the reason obviously I entered early, didn't want to miss out, not the best entry, so don't don't copy that." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~6:42, L265).

**3. How the statements evolved**

- 2026-03-08 to 2026-03-28 (aBVn6IUAjnM, 3L8xdh3oPm4): emotions destroy the edge; a loss weighs twice a win; do not trade when not at 100%.
- 2026-04-18 (RGYbTD1j628): rule five, psychology "probably the most important", $3,000 must feel like $3, one unit per trade.
- 2026-05-10 to 2026-05-28 (wsKx7L9EJys, vJuwbKibN1E): his own pre-method tilting; tilting destroys expected value.
- 2026-06-06 (C-_0QhKZmA4): the $50K tilt day; tilt on a new account if at all.
- 2026-06-17 to 2026-06-22 (XhsbfEdJBAc, aCOgfvL6lK8): "you obviously can't tilt"; tilted once ever; a tilt would go to a cheaper account.
- 2026-06-25 (2hwd27aqD30): psychology is "mostly cope"; a tilted trade is break-even with higher variance; the fix is a step-by-step plan and account routing.
- 2026-07-11 (J8qXxtFFpGg): "psychology isn't real as long as you do it step by step".
- 2026-08-27 to 2026-08-30 (4BXpI-hYqe0, 4IGbxmKJ4BU): the $60,000 Apex day told as the lesson that built the plan; the emotion filter on a trade.
- 2026-09-06 to 2026-09-22 (KhooqEQK9bA, dJdBnSBJlgQ): revenge trading as added variance; the $60,000 loss shown again.
- 2026-10-04 (KHEQ5g55dQ4; latest): tilt on evaluations, never on a funded account near payout; $2,000 funded equals 20 evaluations. The position at the end of the corpus: emotions are real, their only cost is variance, and the plan routes that variance to the cheapest accounts.

**4. Contradictions**

- "Rule five, and probably the most important one of them all, is psychology related" (April) against "psychology is is mostly cope" (June) and "psychology isn't real" (July). His own bridge is that psychology problems are sizing problems with break-even expectancy; the April rule is about not flinching on size, so both can be read as one claim. The dossier's "Psychology" row presents the two as a course-catalogue oddity; the corpus shows they are the same argument.
- "I only tilted once in my entire life trading" (June 22) against the $50K tilt day (June 6), the $60,000 Apex day (August, September) and "tilting after losses" before the method (May). If the $50K and $60,000 days are one event told twice, the figures differ (6K then 12K then 24K on 15 NQ, against 23.7k in one trade on a 50,000 account raised to 110,000); the corpus does not say.
- A tilted trade has "break even expected value" (June) against "tilting is definitely a problem and it's going to destroy your expected value" (May) and "emotions destroy your edge" (March). Later statements keep the June framing: the damage is to accounts and payout rate, not to the trade's expectancy.
- "If you feel that emotion is influencing you, there is most likely something wrong with the operation and you should not have it" (August, a filter on the trade) against "when I personally get tilted or angry, I'm still going to follow the exact same trading plan" (June, no filter). The August video is a mental-models list addressed to viewers; the June one is his own practice.

<!-- target: Section 6 -->
### From the primary corpus: the -$30,000 day and the statistics behind the stops

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 The "-$30,000 day" (CzxSgYujDxs, 2026-06-28) was not a trading loss.* The episode's own facts, all in the results, firms_payouts and accounts_execution files: ("Unfortunately, I had a minor incident with one of the companies where about $50,000 was taken from my account." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:11, L36; results file); ("Tradeify was going to get a maximum payout of $25,000, I'll update it. A maximum payment of $25,000, but they sent me to a live account even though I didn't meet any of the requirements for a live account." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:45, L54; firms_payouts file); ("So I had three accounts with $10,000 in profit completely wiped out. I don't even have a real account for them. I don't receive any payment." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~1:19, L72; results file). The rule he changed in response is an account-count rule: ("It worked well for me in the past, but from now on I'm only going to have three accounts at a time to avoid that problem." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~1:36, L81; accounts_execution file). The same episode contains the 11:00 rule costing him 9,000 (L529) and a separate $30,000 winning day: ("I only did it for one day in my life, but I earned $30,000. I'm going to show some things on the screen regarding my earnings for today. 30,000." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~18:43, L626; results file). The title's $30,000 matches three accounts at $10,000; the spoken intro says about $50,000; the corpus does not resolve the gap. The payout side of the same week was ("I received three from Tradeify. The total here should be $12,500." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:28, L45; results file) and the week's total ("This week I received payments totaling $25,700." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:02, L31; results file).

*1.2 Sample size comes before any stop.* ("Every costly mistake I made was from observing 10 or 30 trades, and then thinking I was doing something wrong based on that sample size." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~0:21, L41); ("Most people discover a new strategy and test it for maybe four trades. If they lose all four, they automatically move on to the next one." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~0:33, L49); ("You should judge yourself at least by the last 100 trades, if not more, not just the most recent one." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~2:51, L150); ("you should audit your last 50 trades and look at: Why am I losing these trades? What is my worst-performing setup? Why is it? And then delete it completely." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~8:19, L384); ("a really good strategy and a really bad one feel the same when executed day after day, simply because of the large sample size needed to determine if there is statistical relevance." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~3:31, L169); ("only thing that distinguishes strategies is the number of trades you execute." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~3:40, L175); ("Almost everyone quits because they can't distinguish between bad luck and a bad strategy." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~7:10, L316); ("The registration is the only thing that can contradict you. Your memory will only store winning trades, but a record will not." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~5:48, L261; medium; "registration" is the captions' record or journal); ("The number of trades I made didn't make me any better at predicting the market. It made me better to keep track of whether my performance was accurate and expected within the deviations of the strategy." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~7:02, L311).

*1.3 Streaks are expected and sized for.* ("A losing streak is completely normal within statistical significance, even to the point of potentially blowing accounts." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~2:03, L110); ("even a real advantage has losing streaks. There is incredible variance in trading, especially when your sample size is huge. So, when you're trading for 18 months, you might have a terrible losing streak" — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~16:06, L579); ("once you have a large number of trades that you review over several days, well, it becomes much more likely to have an atypical losing streak" — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~3:15, L167); ("So, each trade is memoryless, which means if I was to lose nine in a row with a 40% win rate, my next chance for a trade is still the same 40%." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~55:53, L1955; medium). Sizing from the streak: ("Essentially, the number of times you can make a mistake in a row gives you a good margin of trades before reaching your loss limit. And that should help you define the size of your positions." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~7:00, L302; eval_risk_math file); ("This is a simulation that gives me a stop loss of $500 and assumes a loss limit of $4,000. It takes eight consecutive losses to blow up the account. And when you simulate this 6,000 times in 100 trades, you have virtually a 50/50 chance of blowing an account." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~7:51, L335; backtests_news file); ("It was about taking a small functional advantage and sizing it perfectly so that a losing streak wouldn't wipe me out and I could experience the exponential growth that a prop firm would allow." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~18:24, L775); ("I believe that survival is far more important than winning, and that's how I ended up with $1.6 million in payments from funding firms." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~4:26, L218); ("Just make sure that one bad streak is not going to lose everything basically." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~25:49, L799). Frequency as the variance cure: ("if I take 20 trades per day it's the exact same as someone who takes one trade a day for a month. And if I'm able to trade 20 times as much with the same edge, I'm going to make 20 times as much in terms of my profit." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~10:29, L425); ("you just need the largest physically possible sample size to realize the advantage." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~2:37, L140).

*1.4 The thresholds that would make him stop and check.* ("Also, if this number ever becomes below 0.5%. then I would probably start to consider that maybe something's wrong." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~9:39, L294); ("1% chance that is still statistically likely enough for me to be like, "All right, maybe something's up, but I'm going to keep doing it." When this cuts in half to 0.5%, then I'm like, "All right, something something's something's gone wrong. I'm going to I'm going to check make sure everything's as it should be." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~9:50, L299); ("your rate could fall into this bottom 5% luck and then if it ever becomes bottom 5% luck, then something's probably wrong." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~11:31, L345), where the rate is the payout rate: ("Meaning 5% of the time you'll have a 41% or higher payout rate." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~10:14, L310; eval_risk_math file); ("So, if such a streak is too frequent, something is wrong." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~5:35, L267; medium); a truncated August version of the same idea: ("If you ever perform worse than 0.5% of traders, that is" — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~6:45, L291; medium, caption cut off). The three-loss session stop is given the same footing, a probability that has run out rather than a feeling: ("It's just a market that's most likely not going to revert according to my statistics." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~34:24, L1124). What can and cannot remove the edge: ("So the market specifically is not going to remove expected value from the strategy. The only thing is would be losing streaks. So variance basically and then profit rule changes." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~18:21, L665; "profit rule" is the captions' prop-firm rule). The pre-trade questions replace prediction: ("Do I have the correct position size here? Do I have a proven advantage? Will this work in my favor in the long run?" — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~2:10, L121); ("Did I take the correct approach? Are the numbers in my favor? How will expected value affect my funded account? How will this impact my approval rate?" — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~3:01, L146). The psychology journal is written before the entry so it cannot be rationalised afterwards: ("Now, on the other hand, if you're noting it down after you take your position, then I guarantee you you're going to write down bad psychology on most of your losses and then most of your wins, no matter what you were feeling like, you're going to write down good psychology." — JJ, 2hwd27aqD30 ~4:17, L185; entries file).

- 2026-06-25 (2hwd27aqD30): note psychology before the entry, not after.
- 2026-06-28 (CzxSgYujDxs): the Tradeify wipe-out; three accounts at a time there; a $30,000 winning day in the same week.
- 2026-07-11 (J8qXxtFFpGg): the streak-to-size rule and the $500 / $4,000 simulation; judge the process by expected value and pass rate.
- 2026-08-06 (S8fSNbPFuLA, medium): a 0.5% tail as the line between variance and a broken strategy (caption truncated).
- 2026-08-13 to 2026-08-30 (MtBZeAkYH8Q, PMGhHCjYG5c, 4BXpI-hYqe0, 4IGbxmKJ4BU): sample-size rules (10 or 30 mislead, 50 to audit, 100 to judge); survival over winning; an 18-month horizon contains a terrible streak.
- 2026-09-15 (sWJa8vRfPb8): only streaks and firm rule changes can remove the edge.
- 2026-09-29 (nvOaN6mRu-o; latest on thresholds): 1% keep going, 0.5% check, bottom 5% luck is a warning. Nothing later changes these; they are current.

**4. Contradictions**

- The episode title (I Lost $30,000 in Prop Firm Profits in One Day, per the transcript index) against the spoken "about $50,000 was taken" in the same episode, and against the dossier's Section 7 listing of a "-$30k day" among weekly trading results: the money was sim profit removed when Tradeify moved him to live accounts, and the same episode reports a $30,000 winning day and $25,700 of payouts.
- "stop after three losses" as a rule against the memoryless-trade statement: if each trade is still a 40% (or 57%) proposition, three losses carry no information about the fourth. His stated bridge is that the three losses are evidence about the day ("a market that's most likely not going to revert" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~34:24, L1124), not about the next trade; he does not quantify it.
- 0.5% (nvOaN6mRu-o, S8fSNbPFuLA) against "bottom 5% luck" (nvOaN6mRu-o L345): two thresholds in one video. The 0.5% is on the probability of the observed losing streak given his assumed pass rate; the 5% is on the realised payout rate. Both are review triggers, not trading stops.
- "Please stop tracking your win rate" (0Ua-ffltO0I, September) against "judge yourself at least by the last 100 trades" and "audit your last 50 trades" (August). He never says which one a reader should do.

The run from $1.5M (June 6-15) to $2M (reached in the week of 2026-09-21, 19 months after the February 2025 start; his running totals in between are ~$1.65M on 07-13, $1.7M on 07-27, $1.828M on 08-23, $1.85M on 09-06, $1.9M on 09-13 and 09-17, $1,931,000 on 09-20 and 09-22, $1,968,000 on the Tuesday of the $2M week; he also states $400,000 of payouts in the 90 days to 09-17) is about $500k in
three and a half months; the weekly episodes above itemise parts of it
($87k, $105.7k, -$30k day (sim profit on three Tradeify accounts removed when the firm moved him to live accounts, not a trading loss; the same week paid $25,700), $12.5k, $37.5k, $50k+ weeks) but no complete
ledger was found. Dates are the YouTube upload dates from the transcript
index (`docs/research/transcript-index.md`); Ep. 8, 10 and Weeks 1-12 of the
later series were not captured.

<!-- target: Section 7 -->
### From the primary corpus: the start, the first year and the 2025 month-by-month series

**1. The origin in his own words**

*1.1 Seed capital.* Poker produced the bankroll: ("I started with probably $25 to my name playing with one to five dollar buy-ins at in college, and I turned that into $25,000 in probably about 6 months of going to the local card room for some higher stakes." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~1:48, L95); ("I had about $25,000 that I made from poker over that summer, the first half, like September, October, November 2024." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~14:13, L537). The $100-to-$1M video dates the poker run from March 2024 ("uh let's start obviously with March 2024, exactly two years ago from when I'm filming this. The first time I started to play small stakes poker, I was doing five to ten dollar buy-ins." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~0:32, L38) to about February 2025 ("Next, we'll do 2,500 to 25,000. This was August 2024 to about February of 2025." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~13:46, L463). Before that, five years of failed evaluations: ("So, I figured why not buy the evaluation with all the $50 I had. Well, unfortunately, I lost the $50 in the evaluation." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:14, L36); ("So, in my 5 years of a trading introduction, just learning off of YouTube, all I was experiencing was failure. I wasn't able to pass the evaluations." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:51, L59); ("When I was 14, I lost my first $50 on the Topstep evaluation." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~17:57, L757). Immediately before the start he was broke: ("December, January, I was broke. That was like $200 and then $17,000." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~1:29, L72).

*1.2 First funded account and first payout: four tellings, four numbers.* The dated one: ("Uh you can see my first ever funded account was February 3rd, 2025. Uh I made $1,000 on it. For some reason I have the Renko candles." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~21:37, L715). The first payout is $1,000 in one telling ("Oh, I just got paid $1,000 here. I'm going to drop five grand on it. So, I dropped 5K of my poker money on two prop firms in the very end of January, like start of February, and that returned me $17,000." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~17:39, L659), $2,000 in another ("take my first small trades, hit 2,000, which was my first payout I believe" — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~2:48, L117), one or two thousand on a 50K account in a third ("I got the first payoff for like two grand or one grand. And then I started with 150k accounts just because I was 100% sure it was going to work on average." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~34:34, L1011), and a Topstep 150K payout dated March 5 in the fourth ("I started in February 2025 with funding signatures. March 5th was my first payment into a 150k account. So you can see the maximum payout below of 5k on Topstep." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~5:51, L231). The start month wobbles once: ("unfortunately, I was 14k off a million in my first year. Uh I started in March. Um March, I think February or March actually." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:58, L97); every later video says February 2025 ("But my first ever month actually doing it correctly was February of 2025. My first ever month, I started with $5,000." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~1:06, L61); ("started prop firm trading full-time in February of 2025. So, we're at exactly 16 months now." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~0:59, L57); ("trading profs full-time basically since February of last year when I first started them the correct way." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~21:09, L783).

*1.3 The first month: $5,000 in, $17,000 out.* The most repeated figure in the corpus, told the same way in seven videos from June to October 2026: ("my first month, like actually doing it the right way, when I knew it was going to work, I spent 5K out of my 25K, and I made 17K in payouts." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~17:49, L665); ("I started with $5,000 and earned $17,000 in my first month of trading. So I've been getting a return on investment of about 3.5 times." — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~9:15, L370); ("I made 17k back, like payouts, and I spent 5,000 to achieve that." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~12:50, L465); ("started with 5K. First month, $17,000 worth of payouts." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~1:29, L72); ("I started with $5,000 of my $25,000 capital and turned it into $17,000." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~10:03, L437); ("with $5,000 and I turned 5,000 into 17,000 trading the 50k accounts." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:00:38, L2115); ("I started with about $5,000. First month I made $17,000." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~0:02, L73). The sizing logic behind the $5,000: ("I started with 5,000. I felt like 5,000 and I'm going to trade the 50k accounts. It's about $100 each so I can afford 50 and then my risk of ruin with like a 90% 90 that 90% chance of failing meaning no payout to 0.9 to power 50 is like less than 1%" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:00:24, L2107); it ran on two firms ("The first one is I turned five into 17 in about a month trading on two prop firms." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:01:35, L2147). Two statements do not fit. The same interview puts the starting stake at $20,000 ("Um the first one, I was thankful to start with about $20,000 just because that's what I had made in poker and I felt comfortable using that on trading capital." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~38:12, L1359), and when reading a dashboard in September he says February produced nothing ("this is from 2025, last year. And February was my first month that I started trading. I made nothing then and nothing then. I had no money." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~12:43, L461), whereas the August dashboard reading shows 15k for February ("I guess you can see my start in February, 15k gain. March, 37, maybe 40, and then this 46. And of course, this was taken at the beginning of May. So I have 17k in the first half." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~6:30, L253). The likeliest reconciliation is that the two dashboards are single-firm views (Topstep's first payout was March 5) while the $17,000 is the two-firm total; he does not say so.

*1.4 March to June 2025.* First $100,000 bankroll on March 7: ("I was able to finally hit my first $100,000 of net worth {slash} trading bankroll, as you can see here on March 7th. I did get onto the 150k accounts like within a month. It was probably too quick" — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~5:42, L214). The first large win, which the extractor's claim (not the quote) places on the same day: ("Uh this is my first large win, so probably $8,000 win on this day. And then from there, got my largest payout ever, which was $45,000 from E8." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~22:13, L734). A funded account grown from $50,000 to $110,000 and then lost in a day: ("After that, I was pretty confident in what I was doing and even grew a funded account from $50,000 to $110,000." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~10:03, L437); ("I was going to keep repeating the same thing over and over until I got that record payout, until I ended up losing the $60,000 in my funded account in a single day." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~10:10, L442); ("Then, when I was 20 , I lost over $60,000 in a single day, which was more than a quarter of my net worth at the time." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~17:57, L757); ("so yeah 60k down drain in one day. That was when my net worth was probably 200k." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~11:59, L370). The same video's opening rounds that loss down ("I started with a loss of $50 at age 14, then $500, then $5,000, and then over $50,000 just last year" — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~0:33, L52). The extractor's claim for the dJdBnSBJlgQ line attributes the loss to Apex; the quote does not name the firm. The summer internship set the go/no-go threshold: ("I knew by the time my internship was over, I had to have made at least $50,000, or else I wasn't going to be able to do prop firm trading full-time." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~3:13, L148); ("coming out of the internship, I had made more from trading than I did in the internship, and at that point, I knew there's no way I was going to return to them." — JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~3:59, L177); ("I was at the internship I made $125,000 in payouts from prop firms. I probably spent like 30k." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~18:13, L679); ("I quit and rejected the return offer when I reached my first $100,000 a month in trading. At that point I was pretty sure it would work, but when I was at 30,000 a month, I thought I didn't know how long it would last, maybe my advantage would disappear." — JJ, MIWsdldzJgQ ~12:22, L436).

*1.5 July 2025 onward.* ("Here, in July 2025, my first $100,000 month." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~13:53, L504); ("July was my first six-figure month. $125,000. Obviously this 45K payout from EA definitely helps with that." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~14:17, L500); ("100k to 250k was July to August. I had my biggest month ever in trading. So, in July here, $125,000 in payouts. Uh I didn't actually do any other sort of work at this time. This was just off prop firms." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~24:05, L794). The July Topstep account: ("started with 9,000, 16.6, 8,000. So, I started with three big wins. And then a few days to record my payments. And then, obviously, I took the payment after 5 days." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~15:00, L542); one account's first two weeks of July ("we have the first 2 weeks of July. $56,000. So, this is on one account. And I'm not sure how many accounts I had at this point. This was a a crazy amount of accounts." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~24:18, L801). Autumn 2025 by bank-account month: ("You have November 145k, 100k October, 130k September, 100k October, and then July 125k. So, consistently getting those 100k months ever since July, and I still haven't gone below it." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~27:51, L915). The E8 account that paid the $45,000: ("In October, 47.5k, but it doesn't really matter, right? Because it was simulated money until they paid me $45,000." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~3:42, L158; the extractor's claim dates this October to 2024, which predates his start, and the photo date below puts it in autumn 2025); ("I lost seven days in a row. So I lost $21,000 in a row. But I still earned 48,000 in the month and received a payment of 45,000 for it." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~15:11, L531); ("The company here is E8 Markets. It's quite blurry. The photo was taken on November 1st from my camera or just for this video." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~11:04, L395). Lucid began September 5 ("I started September 5th, kind of like when Lucid released. I don't think anyone else was on it before me." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~6:34, L243). Net worth $250,000 to $1,000,000 ("And then lastly, $250,000 to a million dollars in September 2025 to March 2026. Now, when I'm recording this, and I'm a full-time prop firm trader." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~27:40, L909). The first year closed about $14,000 short of $1M of payouts ("14k off a million in my first year" — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:58, L97), and 1099s for calendar 2025 are the proof offered: ("uh 1099 tax form. You can't fake this stuff. Topstep paid me 250k last year and then RiseWorks paid me 450k." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:27, L80). Early 2026: ("uh February, I did a day in the life series, 20 days of trading, 100k in payouts, just standard day in the life content of which prop firms I'm trading, where I'm getting my payouts" — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~28:18, L929); ("I've documented two full months of 100k payouts on my Instagram" — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~2:16, L107).

Other first-year numbers: starting stake "$5,000" (seven videos) vs "about $20,000" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L1359); first-month spend "5K" vs internship-month spend "like 30k" for $125,000 (1CEAgRUNZ4c L679); first $100,000 bankroll 2025-03-07 (C-_0QhKZmA4 L214); first large win "$8,000" (aBVn6IUAjnM L734); largest payout "$45,000 from E8" (aBVn6IUAjnM L734; aCOgfvL6lK8 L1579); largest loss "losing the $60,000 in my funded account in a single day" at net worth ~$200k (PMGhHCjYG5c L442, L757; dJdBnSBJlgQ L370); seven losing days, -$21,000, inside a +$48,000 month (AxP-cg50TdM L531); Feb 2026 "20 days of trading, 100k in payouts" (aBVn6IUAjnM L929).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The first-year story is told from March 2026 (aBVn6IUAjnM) to October 2026 (KHEQ5g55dQ4, PN1UKQMPb5M) and the anchors do not move: February 2025 start, $5,000 of evaluations, $17,000 of payouts, July 2025 as the first $100,000 month at $125,000, the $45,000 E8 payout as the largest ever, and six-figure months from July 2025 on. What moves is the middle: March is 25k, 37-40k, 38k or 40k; June is 40k, 50k, 60k or "smaller one"; May is 83k or 84k; the Chart Fanatics sequence (17, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100) is a smoothed retelling that matches none of the dashboard readings. The latest dashboard readings (4BXpI-hYqe0, 2026-08-27, and DEkzU8gqX-o, 2026-09-08) and the latest narration (1CEAgRUNZ4c, 2026-09-27) are the figures to use; the two dashboard readings agree with each other on March to May (37-40 / 38; 46; 83 / 84). The claim that every month since July 2025 was above six figures is stated in March (aBVn6IUAjnM L915), June ("No one can really do 100k plus a month in prop firm payouts for 12 months straight. So, I've been able to do it for 13 months straight" — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~1:06, L65), August ("Fortunately, I've been above six figures every month since July 2025 onwards." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~15:32, L560) and September ("I do over $100,000 a month every single month since then in payouts." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~1:58, L86), but the Chart Fanatics recording of 2026-08-25 withdraws it: ("So, I'm not able to stay above six figures every month. It's mostly like 100, then 75, then 60, then 100." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~49:41, L1591). That recording (2026-08-25) predates the restatements of 08-27 (4BXpI-hYqe0 L560) and 09-22 (dJdBnSBJlgQ L86), so the blanket claim is also his latest word; the withdrawal is the only place he gives a month-by-month shape for 2026, and the two cannot both be true.

**4. Contradictions**

1. Starting stake: "$5,000" (seven videos) vs "about $20,000" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L1359). The $25,000 poker bankroll is consistent; what he deployed in month one is not.
2. First payout: "$1,000" (1CEAgRUNZ4c L659; aBVn6IUAjnM L715 for the first funded account) vs "2,000" (C-_0QhKZmA4 L117) vs "two grand or one grand" (dJdBnSBJlgQ L1011) vs "March 5th was my first payment into a 150k account" (4BXpI-hYqe0 L231). The dossier's row of two early Topstep payouts of $4,500 each has no counterpart in the corpus.
3. February 2025: "$17,000" (dJdBnSBJlgQ L72) vs "15k gain" (4BXpI-hYqe0 L253) vs "I made nothing then" (DEkzU8gqX-o L461). Month five: dJdBnSBJlgQ L78 calls the fifth month (June by his own Feb=1 count) the first six-figure month, while every other telling makes July the first.
4. The $45,000 E8 payout's month: tied to July 2025 ("this 45K payout from EA definitely helps", C-_0QhKZmA4 L500) and to October 2025 ("In October, 47.5k" and "they paid me $45,000", 4BXpI-hYqe0 L158; photo "taken on November 1st", AxP-cg50TdM L395).
5. "My biggest payout has been 45,000 and then every other payout from that then on is literally 5,000 or less." (JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~44:43, L1579) vs "I received a $46,000 payout from my live account." (JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:22, L34, Lucid, May 2026), "26k payout in August." (JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~20:35, L614), "18K from the live" (JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~0:28, L45, medium). Live-account payouts break the "5,000 or less" statement.
6. Single-day loss: "over $50,000 just last year" (PMGhHCjYG5c L52) vs "the $60,000" (L442, L757; dJdBnSBJlgQ L370).
7. Age: "I made a million dollars at 19 years old." (JJ, CZYbdX2ofiI ~0:02, L28) vs "when I was 20 , I lost over $60,000" (PMGhHCjYG5c L757) at net worth ~$200k (mid-2025, dJdBnSBJlgQ L370) and "14k off a million in my first year" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L97): by his own dates the first million came at 20.
8. Six figures every month since July 2025 (four videos) vs "100, then 75, then 60, then 100" (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L1591).
9. Dossier Section 7 row 1 dates the start as "implied"; the corpus dates it explicitly (first funded account 2025-02-03, first Topstep 150K payout 2025-03-05).

### From the primary corpus: the cumulative ladder from $1.2M to $2M, the weekly series, and what is shown as proof

**1. Definitions and framing in his own words**

*1.1 What the headline counts.* Payouts received, after the firm's split, not account balances: ("I've done over 1.8 million dollars worth of prop firm payouts in the last 18 months, and that is not account balances, that is literally payouts hitting my bank account." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:21, L99); ("I spent $550,000 on profit valuations, but it resulted in $1,931,000 worth of profit payouts after the profit split." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~0:02, L31). From September 2026 he counts only settled money: ("I'm only going to show payouts that hit my bank account or hit the rise count. Uh no longer showing you when I request because for some reason people were saying that I was faking that." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~8:13, L275). The weekly series is defined as the second million: ("In the first two weeks of June, I did $87,000 worth of prop firm payouts. This video series is me turning that into $1 million." — JJ, gjWiGBiVis0 ~0:02, L30); ("This is episode two of a series where I'm documenting my road from $1.5 up to $2.5 million worth of prop firm payouts." — JJ, xTxrtFKlQfc ~0:11, L35); the "Road to $1M" titles (xTxrtFKlQfc L1, medium) and the "$1.5 to $2.5 million" framing (pwO9IAu7FUQ L31) are the same series.

*1.2 What he presents as proof.* Tax forms ("uh 1099 tax form. You can't fake this stuff. Topstep paid me 250k last year and then RiseWorks paid me 450k." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:27, L80); firm dashboards ("Here's Topstep 300K and and then we'll go to Lucid. Here is 140K. Here's Tradeify with 180K. All these pages are refreshed, by the way. Funded next. Here is 177,000." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~2:22, L97); the Rise payment account ("I received $59,000 in payments in the last 2 weeks. Here is my account on Rise. You can see all the payments after the 7th, which is when we recorded our last video." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~0:27, L45); a Wise account and a bank account ("I received $83,000 in payouts to my Wise account this month." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:06, L26; "Lastly, Lucid Trading pays directly to my bank account. And I received a $46,000 payout from my live account." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:22, L34); Instagram ("Here's August 2026. That's also posted on my Instagram. Every payment I receive there is recorded. Anyway, so far, 87,000 there and then 797. So, more than 100,000 this month. 30,000 are still pending." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~17:32, L628); a screen recording ("Proof screen recorded in the first 15 seconds of that." — JJ, UVKVSWKFlvo ~9:45, L352); and the stated intent ("I've earned over $1.6 million in payouts, and I'm going to show you the proof because everyone in this space talks and almost no one shows anything." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~0:01, L29). Some proof is weak by his own description: ("this number is $47,000 in profit this month" — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~10:58, L392) is prefaced by "It's quite blurry" in the same line. No firm certificate appears in this corpus. The only third-party check is a host's: ("Obviously, you know, it's not fully verified on our end. I think he has done a decent job so far of showing like live logins. He showed like a bunch of different bank accounts" — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~0:32, L85). He also disclaims while claiming: ("Uh, no income claims on YouTube, but I've been able to make $100,000 a month just by finding the best way to fit in that rule set." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~14:19, L422).

*1.3 Biggest day and biggest week.* ("Here's how I made $56,893.28 in one day, and this is my biggest trading day ever." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~0:02, L45), made of one trade across accounts ("This was a 30k trade cuz it was 6k on five accounts." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~3:18, L153), with the caveat ("But days like this are not very common, so I just want to make sure everyone can see" — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~1:16, L86). The week: ("Here's how I made $52,392.04 this week trading Nasdaq futures." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~0:02, L34), restated two dollars higher at the end ("This was uh again how I made $52,394.04 this week trading Nasdaq futures." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~11:09, L397), itemised as Topstep Express accounts ("Here we have on Topstep Express account number 49 9500, number 71 8600. Another one on this week, by the way. Number 78 this week did 9700." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~0:07, L37) and Tradovate accounts ("Tradovate this week I did 8250 on this account. This account 6325, and then this account a bit worse 2700, but still." — JJ, Lep139B5EbU ~0:22, L45); the listed items sum to about $45,000, not $52,392. Both April figures are trading P&L across accounts, not payouts. The largest payout week in the corpus is the $2M week: ("because I started the week at 100 1.931 million, I didn't think I was going to get 70ks this week, but apparently I did." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~18:00, L566). A second one-day figure: ("I only did it for one day in my life, but I earned $30,000." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~18:43, L626).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The ladder is monotone when read by recording order, with three regressions explained by batch-recorded educational videos: $1.2M (03-28), $1.3M (04-12 to 06-01, with "12 months" attached), $1.5M (06-06 to 07-11, "16 months"), ~$1.65M (07-13), $1.6M (07-25), $1.7M (07-27), $1.5M (07-30) and $1.5M (08-06), $1.6M (08-13 to 08-20 and spoken on 08-30), $1.828M (08-23), $1.8M (08-27 to 09-15), $1.85M (09-06), ~$1.856M (09-08), $1.9M (09-13, 09-17), $1,931,000 (09-20, 09-22, and the start of the $2M week), $1,968,000 (Tuesday of that week), $2,000,000 (the FTMO deposit, upload 10-04). The $2M was therefore reached in the week of 2026-09-21 ("payouts today is the 21st" — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~3:58, L148; "September we are on the 24th" — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~15:08, L481), 19 months after February 2025, not on the upload date. The "12 months" qualifier drifts: $1.2M in 12 months (03-28), $1.3M in 12 months (04-12 to 07-05), then "$1.5 million with Prop Firms in the last 12 months" (xNeBEqv06k0 L23, 07-17), which conflicts with "$1.5 million in profit from payouts, 1.3 of them coming in the last 12 months" (C-_0QhKZmA4 L25, 06-06; EyR3i2GxwyA L31, 07-05) and with "In the last two years" (J8qXxtFFpGg L29, 07-11). The 90-day figure of 2026-09-17 ($400,000) implies ~$133k per month for mid-June to mid-September, which agrees with the ladder ($1.5M on 06-15 to $1.9M on 09-13). Monthly: June 2026 ~$131k from the three itemised weeks ($87,000 + $18,000 + $25,700); July weak by the itemised weeks ($11,500, $25,700, $12,500) yet $1.5M to $1.7M on the ladder; August "more than 100,000" (4BXpI-hYqe0 L628); September "over 150" (PN1UKQMPb5M L941).

**4. Contradictions**

1. Same-day figures disagree: 4IGbxmKJ4BU says "$1.6 million" aloud (L29) and "$1.8 million" in its description (L20, medium); -lxNWJGWtbg says "$2M" in the title (L1) and "$1.8 million" in the description (L18, both other, medium); KHEQ5g55dQ4's host says "$1.8 million" in the intro (host L87) and "1.5 million right now" in the body (host L1470), while JJ says "1.75 million in profit" (L1636; the only place he uses 1.75, and "profit" where elsewhere the figure is gross payouts).
2. "$1.5 million with Prop Firms in the last 12 months" (xNeBEqv06k0 L23) vs "$1.5 million in profit from payouts, 1.3 of them coming in the last 12 months" (C-_0QhKZmA4 L25) vs "In the last two years" (J8qXxtFFpGg L29).
3. Ep. 3 (CzxSgYujDxs) opens with "$50,000 was taken from my account" (L36) and later itemises "three accounts with $10,000 in profit" (L72).
4. Ep. 3 and Ep. 5 report the identical weekly total "$25,700" (CzxSgYujDxs L31; q8PXXvY9lS0 L509).
5. The week-of-$52,392 itemisation (Topstep 9500 + 8600 + 9700, Tradovate 8250 + 6325 + 2700, an E8 figure captioned "252325") does not reconcile to the total (Lep139B5EbU L37, L45, L41 low).
6. The dossier's "early October" for $2M is the upload date; the transcript dates it to the week of 2026-09-21.
7. The dossier's Ep. 2 row gives "$105,700 in 3 Weeks" (the title, xTxrtFKlQfc L1); the spoken weekly figure is "$18,000" (L40) and $87,000 + $18,000 = $105,000, so the title's $105,700 is the three-week sum, not a weekly figure.

### From the primary corpus: per-firm payout totals over time and the gap to the headline

**1. The per-firm statements in his own words, by date**

*2026-03-28 (3L8xdh3oPm4).* ("Eight Futures, really good firm to use, 172k in payouts there. Alpha Futures, I'm on the live account now. I haven't sent live in like 2 months, but was at like 75k or something." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~0:56, L63); ("Lucid, I'm on live as well. Uh 75k payouts on Lucid in like a month or 2 months. The 2025 1099 pair (Topstep 250 + RiseWorks 450 = 700) against "14k off a million in my first year" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L97) leaves ~286 outside those two payers if "first year" is read as calendar 2025 (by his February start it runs to February 2026, so the true gap is smaller), consistent with firms that pay to a bank directly (Lucid: "pays directly to my bank account", 7UXiI2arAlQ L34; FTMO via "JP Morgan Chase", PN1UKQMPb5M L256).

**3. How the statements evolved**

Each firm's number rises or holds except for three small retreats he notices himself or does not: MyFundedFutures 92 to 87 ("It was 92K. I'm not sure why they changed it." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~2:39, L105), Lucid 145 (08-27) to 140 (09-22), Apex 70 (08-27) to 60 (09-22) although he was banned in or before March 2026 (3L8xdh3oPm4 L69, medium). Tradeify is frozen at 180 from 07-25 to 09-22 while Tradeify payouts continue in the weekly videos ($12,500 in Ep. 3, CzxSgYujDxs L45; "$3,400 after profit split" from the crypto product in Ep. 4, EyR3i2GxwyA L44, medium; a "fairly large payment" expected from live accounts in Ep. 7, MIWsdldzJgQ L453), so the 180 is a dashboard figure that excludes something, probably the live accounts. Topstep's 320 (06-06) is above the 292-300 that every dashboard reading gives; he explains it as two dashboards ("probably like 280k in the new dashboard" — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~0:22, L36). E8 is the fastest-growing line in 2026 (172 in March, 288 in September). FundedNext grows from 67 to 177 in six months. The named list widens: six firms in May, nine in July, eleven in September, with Futures Elite, Bulenox, FTMO, Goat, Funded Seed, WRM, Blue Sky, Fidius/"Fidious" (PN1UKQMPb5M L941), The Trading Pit ("one from the trading pit" — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~0:38, L50) and "104 new prop firm" (PN1UKQMPb5M L233) appearing only as single payouts. The latest breakdown (dJdBnSBJlgQ, 2026-09-22) is the current one.

**4. Contradictions**

1. The listed firms never sum to the headline. The gap is ~$290k-$560k at every breakdown (table). His own explanations: smaller firms "not really worth showing" (8lbkj0PF1uM L50), live-account payouts paid to a bank (7UXiI2arAlQ L34), and the 1099 split (3L8xdh3oPm4 L80; Rise handles several firms). None is itemised, so the headline is not reconstructible from the per-firm figures in the corpus.
2. FundedNext: "not 250k. Uh just 57k total on my five bolts" (5RzMu2B2E_0 L138) corrects an earlier video not in the corpus.
3. Apex: 60 (03-28, 04-12, 07-25, 07-30, 09-22) vs "about 70,000" (08-27) for a firm that banned him.
4. Lucid: "$60,000 from $5,000" (5RzMu2B2E_0 L407, 04-12) vs "6 oz $430 into $100,700" (C-_0QhKZmA4 L235, 06-06, medium, garbled) vs "I spent 6K and I got 120,000. No, 105K." (dJdBnSBJlgQ L644, 09-22) vs "I spent 20 and I've gotten about 140 150 or this might be 105" (dJdBnSBJlgQ L652, 09-22, medium).
5. Topstep: "$320,000" (06-06) vs "$292,000 approved" (07-25) vs "300K" (09-22).
6. Dossier Section 1 and Section 5 name "Funded Engineer" and "Bulwark"; the corpus captions are "TradiFi" and "Oola Prime"/"Hola Prime", i.e. Tradeify and Hola Prime. Dossier Section 10 items 8, 10 and 18 are resolved by this.
7. The dossier's Section 5 firm list (which ends with Bulwark Prime) and its Section 9 phrase "Bulwark Prime ($55,000 of his payouts)" carry the same mishearing.

### From the primary corpus: evaluation spend versus payouts, return multiples and the monthly run-rate

**1. The economics in his own words**

*1.1 The lifetime ratio.* ("If I'm able to spend $550,000 on evals, but take more than $1.8 million worth of prop firm payouts, well, that's $1.3 million worth of profit" — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~7:44, L359); ("I have received $1.8 million in payments from funding firms and have spent $550,000 on appraisals, which gives me a profit of approximately $1.3 million" — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~9:07, L365); ("in total, I have done 1.8 18, I think it's 56. It might be a little bit more at the time of recording. But, how much have I spent on evaluations? About $557,000." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~12:04, L439; medium); ("So, when people cringe originally, I tell them I spent $550,000 on evals." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~13:04, L473); ("I started with $5,000. Now, I'm at 1.3 million in profit with more in my accounts. Like I could withdraw 200k from my accounts and never spend another dime." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~13:11, L477); ("If I had a $550,000 live account and I grew that live account into $1.9 million in just a year and a half, that would be incredible." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~0:17, L38). The multiple: ("I started with 5,000. So, if you look at this return, it's a 3 .5x return." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~12:16, L446); ("So, my return would probably go up to like 3.8 or 4x maybe." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~12:20, L448); ("Here was the original investment, 517, so 3.5x, and it's still about 3.5x because pretty much nothing has changed with the prop firm environment." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~13:29, L487; medium); ("on average I'm getting 3.5x, but on lucid I ran really well." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~21:55, L652; medium); ("I run my simulations and I prove that my strategy is going to execute properly and generate me that positive expected value of about a 3.5 times return on my investment within a month." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~7:53, L365). The one outlier: ("So then it's like 400k spent, 1.75 million in profit, which is a really good amount to start your own live account" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~51:09, L1636), recorded 2026-08-25, two weeks before "$557,000". $1,931,000 / $550,000 = 3.51, so the 09-22 pair is internally consistent with "3.5x"; $1.8M / $550,000 = 3.27 and $1.75M / $400k = 4.4 are not.

*1.2 The monthly cycle.* Spend: ("I've been able to scale up to spending about 40k a month and receiving back, on average, about 140k. Some months less, some months more, but consistently around six figures a month." — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~9:21, L374); ("on average, I'd probably spend like 40k a month, maybe more, maybe less. Obviously, depends on the month. And the payouts I'm just doing anywhere from 120 to 170 just obviously depending on the month cuz you know how payouts stack." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~19:41, L731); ("and to do about 130 150k a month in payouts. I'm usually spending 40k, sometimes more, sometimes even 50k a month if possible" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~26:03, L769); ("but 3x return is totally doable, understandable, easy. Uh, so you can make 100k a month spending 50k" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~26:36, L785); earlier and smaller: ("Like you spent 30k on evals and you withdraw 130k." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~10:07, L363) with ("This is sort of what what mine looks like per month." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~10:25, L373; medium). Bounds: ("and myself personally, are getting a 4X return on our investment in the best-case scenario, okay? So, the best thing we can do is spend 40K and withdraw 160K." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~25:11, L861); ("in the best-case scenario, I'm aiming for a 4x return. At worst, I'm aiming for a 3x return, okay? Most of the time it will be between these values. And that's 80,000 a month. That's 120,000 a month. The midpoint, 3.5 thousand per month, would be 100 thousand per month, which is exactly my personal goal." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~25:38, L876). Rounds: ("I refer to it in my head as a round of spending. Like, I'll drop 5K and I'll turn it into more than 5K." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~20:56, L624). The rates that drive the arithmetic: ("If I trade 50K accounts, which I don't. I do about 0.75, so it's about 3 weeks from eval to getting a payout. Uh I can buy 100 per month, 33% pass rate, 50% payout rate." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~12:05, L447); ("I try to pass about 30% of the evaluations, and then payout rate I also aim for it to be about 30%." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~40:45, L1445); ("I'm passing like 10 evals in a day. Just by taking so many trades of smaller risk to reward." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~9:53, L405). The trade-level inputs he gives for himself: ("I win at 41% on 1.5R setups when I am trading with max aggression targeting speed to pass." — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~0:00, L20; description); ("So, my actual success rate is approximately 42%, and that's on a trade with a risk-reward ratio of 1.5." — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~0:52, L65); ("definitely more aggressive on my own accounts because I'm taking a bunch of displacements. So that lowers my win rates, but it increases the amount of trades I can take and then I make more money because I'm taking profitable trades on props." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~48:09, L1302); volume ("I have performed 20 trades per day for the past 16 months." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~1:53, L106; "20 operations a day for 16 months, that 's more than 7,000 operations." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~14:53, L633); losing streaks ("Once, I even lost 11 times in a row." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~2:08, L113); account attrition ("I still have many accounts that reach the maximum loss limit, but as long as I optimize for that specific constraint, most of the time they won't reach that limit" — JJ, 4IGbxmKJ4BU ~4:30, L221; "I make mistakes constantly. I've burned through a lot of accounts." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~0:31, L50). A losing week is still a paying week: ("how it was a losing week strategy-wise, but I still made money because of the profit specific environment. Obviously, your eval fees versus your payouts are what really matters." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~0:13, L37); ("but still got [clears throat] more in pays than I spent on evals, which is the entire goal really." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~11:05, L382).

*1.3 Profit versus payouts, and the ceiling.* ("Like I was at 75k for a few months. I was wondering, can I ever hit 100k in profit? Cuz I can hit 100k in payouts, but can I ever hit 100k in profit? And then eventually I did and then I stuck with it." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:03:16, L2204); ("Right now, I'm trying to make about $100,000 a month." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:03:09, L2200); ("But I earn 100,000 a month on average. Some months less, some months more. It depends on when my payments accumulate, when I transfer to real accounts" — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~25:58, L887); ("currently I'm doing about 130 140k a month in payouts which is absolutely amazing." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~20:11, L749); ("right now profit trading sitting about 90 100 110 120k profit per month, which is great." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~20:32, L761); ("You can't make more than 120k a month realistically." — JJ, 1CEAgRUNZ4c ~21:58, L812); ("$100,000 plus every single month in payouts. Try to get my profit north of 75K. Usually we can do about 100 110." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~17:05, L539); ("now I'm doing $100,000 a month in profit trading through this approach that was mostly molded by poker." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~24:32, L702). Money left in accounts: ("So if I was to stop spending and only go for winning days and just withdraw all of that, I'd probably get about 250k back." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~50:53, L1628); ("Well, technically if I stop spending money today, I could probably withdraw 150 grand next month and have a 150k profit month, which would be great, right? But then the month after that, what am I going to get? Nothing." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~9:13, L374). The approach was only "right" for part of the period: ("I followed the right path for about the last 6 months before was in terms of my prop firm approach, it wasn't the best." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~29:03, L1051). Student economics he states as their mentor: ("this is Henry. Henry did $55,000 worth of payouts in September, but he spent 31k on evals." — JJ, r5AhmGaFujU ~0:02, L33); ("So, you got 42K in in funded balance across across the board." — JJ, r5AhmGaFujU ~20:07, L628); ("Quinn is making $50,000 a month in proper payouts and I'm going to scale him to $100,000 a month in proper payouts in this video." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~0:03, L28), with the caveat ("Okay, well, 50k last month's impressive, but I guess it was just a big month because everyone just moved you live, unfortunately." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~1:23, L78); ("how one of my students turned $0 into $780,000 trading in Prop Firms in just under a year" — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~0:08, L27), the student himself: ("I think my best month was December, when I had 200,000 while only spending 15,000." — other, xNeBEqv06k0 ~2:48, L127). Teaching models, not his own figures: ("you will have successfully converted 20,000 into 51 or 52,000 in your bank account. So you just earned $30,000 in one month with a 33% approval rate." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~12:54, L492); ("this exact method will be used to make up to $27,000 a month if you're using five prop firms at once." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~6:51, L235).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The monthly spend rises in his telling from ~$30k (internship month 2025, 1CEAgRUNZ4c L679; "30k on evals" in May 2026, vJuwbKibN1E L363) to $40k (July-September 2026, AxP-cg50TdM L861; 2BtwoTCxcbc L374) to "sometimes even 50k" (September 2026, dJdBnSBJlgQ L769), while stated payouts move from ~$130k to a 120-170k range; the ratio he quotes stays 3-4x throughout, and the lifetime pair ($550k, $1.931M) matches 3.5x exactly on 09-22. The pass and payout rates are stated once each in June 2026 and differ between the two videos (33%/50% vs 30%/30%); nothing later updates them. The $100,000-a-month headline is used from May 2026 ("I make a $100,000 every month following just one simple candle." — JJ, 7UXiI2arAlQ ~0:02, L24) to September 2026 (sWJa8vRfPb8 L230; nvOaN6mRu-o L702; 1CEAgRUNZ4c L37) and is a profit figure in the later videos ("100k in payouts, but can I ever hit 100k in profit" — aCOgfvL6lK8 L2204; "$100,000 a month in profit" — nvOaN6mRu-o L702) and a payout figure in the earlier ones (7UXiI2arAlQ L26, L36). The latest statements (1CEAgRUNZ4c 09-27, PN1UKQMPb5M 10-04) are: payouts 130-150k, profit 90-120k, spend 40-50k.

**4. Contradictions**

1. Lifetime spend: "400k" (rec. 08-25) vs "517" (earlier video, restated 09-08) vs "$550,000" (09-03 onward) vs "$557,000" (09-08). The 09-22 pair is the only one where payouts / spend equals the stated 3.5x.
2. Monthly spend: "40k, sometimes more, sometimes even 50k" (dJdBnSBJlgQ L769) vs "I don't see anyone else dropping 100 grand a month every single month and documenting it." (JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~27:00, L796; medium) one minute later.
3. Pass/payout rates: 33% / 50% (XhsbfEdJBAc) vs 30% / 30% (aCOgfvL6lK8), both June 2026.
4. Own win rate: 41-42% at 1.5R in his own figures vs the dossier's 57.5% (80-trade sample) and `PortfolioConfig.p_win = 0.54`; he says explicitly that his own accounts run below his demonstration win rate (MVP7X-3v8xk L1302).
5. "earns me $100,000 a month" from a strategy that "wins close to 40% of the time and generates an expected value of 41%" (2BtwoTCxcbc L436, medium) is garbled arithmetic; the 41% is the win rate.
6. "You can't make more than 120k a month realistically." (1CEAgRUNZ4c L812, profit) vs "130 140k a month in payouts" (1CEAgRUNZ4c L749) in the same video; consistent only if read as profit vs gross.
7. Dossier Section 10 item 14 treats "16 months" and "18 months" as a conflict; by his dating (Feb 2025 start) they are June 2026 and August 2026.

Mechanized versions of the rules fire about 1.2-1.3 trades a day (his own FX Replay session averaged 2.67, MVP7X-3v8xk L1296, and NEXOR's challenge test 5.1, JcW8Wjnw8ck L176; 314
trades in the one-year test), an order of magnitude below the 10-30 a day he
describes. Critiques to weigh: these are small samples (150-314 trades),
sensitive to the continuation window and to fills on 16.5-point stops with 3
contracts; fxreplay found the choppy April 2023 regime performed "not as
good"; ZenomTrader (X) calls the technique automatable and hard to lose with
enough accounts but with tiny ROI, "5-10 accounts pay almost monthly while
buying challenges constantly"; faketrades.in rates the strategy 1.0 star
with one "bust", on Indian market data, so not a valid NQ test; prop-firm
payouts are gross of evaluation and reset spend; and the 10-30 trades-a-day
cadence in his videos is not what the codified rules generate.

<!-- target: Section 8 -->
### From the primary corpus: his backtests, sample sizes and statistics

**What a backtest is for, in his words.** The most repeated instruction in the corpus is that the statistic to backtest is the evaluation pass rate, not an equity curve: ("You need to find your pass rate. You do not need your average profit, your sharp ratio, your expected profit per trade, your win rate. That does not matter. The only thing that matters is this." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~35:12, L1195); ("Do not bother backtesting your live equity curve. That's not going to matter because we're trading on prop firms. All we need to know is the hit rate of the profit target before the max drawdown. So, how many times you can win before lose on the eval. That's all you need to backtest for." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~2:00, L85); repeated in June (XhsbfEdJBAc ~14:06, L517), in the July recording (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~23:31, L791) and in September (sWJa8vRfPb8 ~1:33, L80). The test must run under the firm's rules, per firm: ("Run retrospective tests, but pretend you're in a funded account environment. Make your trades, follow the drawdown limit, have a profit target and consistency rules. Just treat it that way. You will automatically discover if you will be successful in funding ventures." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~16:00, L558); ("it is best to simulate it specifically for each different firm before you even try because remember, you can determine if you're going to make money on a prop firm before you buy a single eval." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~8:09, L376). He has never tested the method on a live curve and expects it to break even there: ("If you back test it on live, I assume it's going to break even. I haven't even done it. Like there's literally no point for me to back test this thing on live because I only trade prop firms. I only need to optimize it for the pass rate." — JJ, 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~34:43, L1179); ("Don't be surprised when you use a static minus 500 plus 750 when it doesn't work on a live account because it's optimized for a prop firm." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~32:07, L1054). The pass rate is then used to infer the payout rate (XhsbfEdJBAc ~13:37, L500); the funded statistics he tracks are pass rate, average payout and average payout chance (aBVn6IUAjnM ~13:58, L469).

**The simulation recipe.** The account he simulates is a 50K evaluation with a $2,000 loss limit and a $3,000 target: ("it simulates that you have an account with a funding firm. It starts with a balance of 50,000 dollars, and if you ever lose 2,000 dollars, it means you've reached the maximum loss and your account is lost. See how many times you can reach 53,000 before dropping 2,000." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~2:30, L121); the same 3k/2k account in nvOaN6mRu-o (~1:58, L83). The July recording uses 10 accounts as the worked example (KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~23:17, L784, with the same risk-to-reward on every account and trade) and 50 evaluations as the sample: ("Back test 50 evaluations. At that point, you'll have a good estimate of your pass rate. If you only try three and you lose all of them, it's not because the strategy doesn't work." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~26:14, L874); 50 again in September (0Ua-ffltO0I ~3:34, L189). The funded side gives a dollar value per funded account, which caps what he will pay to reach one: ("then pretend you have 30 funded accounts. See how many total payouts you can get, divide that by 30, and now you know, if I got 30k in payouts over 30 accounts, I'm expected to make a thousand dollars in payouts if I'm given one funded account." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~3:50, L200); sWJa8vRfPb8 (~8:23, L318) runs 50 funded accounts over a year in replay and asks whether a funded account can be reached for less than that value; the reward ratio is chosen by the same count: ("Basically, if you were to have a 100 funded accounts over the course of a year, how many payouts in total would you get with what specific risk-to-reward? One of them will be optimal and that's the one you should choose." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~25:17, L845). He says he grids stop, target and size over every account state (eval new and in profit, funded new, in profit and after payout, live) (sWJa8vRfPb8 ~17:20, L630), and his point targets are outputs of that code: ("my take profit is not a specific location. It's a specific amount of points that I've backtested, well, not backtested, coded to simulate a backtest uh for my exact strategy. So, I'm able to see exactly how many points I want to target for each trade." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~6:13, L249). The May 2026 worked model: ("The only required edge is a 33% pass rate on the evals, meaning a 33% chance to reach plus 3K before minus 2K. This is the only stat to backtest as well." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~1:54, L82), a 30% funded hit rate to a $3,000 target (~5:53, L205), about 29-30 evaluations and $3,000 of fees per 10 funded accounts (~5:35, L196; medium), net about $5.4K per 10-account cycle (~6:03, L210), summarised as $3,000 expected fee, $5,000 expected profit, $8,000 withdrawn after split (~6:16, L217). His firm selector takes trade count, wins, average win and average loss, with at least 50 trades (vJuwbKibN1E ~16:03, L562), and ranks firms: ("based on these statistics that I just entered, if I buy the Apex end of day 150K account, I'm making $244 per eval." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~16:34, L579); Lucid Direct -$166 and "Trade if I Lightning" (probably TradeDay Lightning) -$192 per eval (~16:41, L583; medium). The inputs he typed are not in the transcript. His replay tool is FX Replay, which carries red-folder news but not tweets (MVP7X-3v8xk ~11:49, L339).

**Sample sizes he asks for.** The thresholds rose through the year. May 2026: 50 trades (vJuwbKibN1E L562). June 2026, for evaluations: ("Personally, it's all of the evals that I've done. Five is probably not enough. I would recommend at least 50" — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~39:39, L1408), with 20 giving a good approximation (~39:46, L1412). August 2026: ("You need a minimum of 250 trades before judging a strategy." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~1:02, L69), a year for a one-entry-a-day method (~1:08, L73) and ("That allows me to determine statistical significance in just 1 month." — JJ, MtBZeAkYH8Q ~1:17, L79) at his cadence. The 7,000 trades of that title is arithmetic stated a week later: ("20 operations a day for 16 months, that 's more than 7,000 operations. Someone who operates one configuration per day will only have 252 configurations in a year." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~14:53, L633; `results.json`). September 2026: over 500 trades (2BtwoTCxcbc ~8:03, L326); 50 real accounts and 100 to 200 trades (dJdBnSBJlgQ ~18:49, L564); ("So check your full sample. Ideally more than 50 evals and 50 fundeds or 200 trades. Check your results after cost." — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~33:14, L973); 200 trades again in nvOaN6mRu-o (~18:07, L526). One month is never enough: ("A month is definitely not enough sample size to determine if a strategy is good or bad." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~12:53, L429; also ~29:49, L951; 2hwd27aqD30 ~1:41, L91). The quant framing of August 2026 is stricter than any trade count: ("The T statistic is roughly calculated by multiplying the Sharpe ratio by the square root of the number of years something has been operating. And you can see that trading it for 6 weeks gives you a T-statistic of 0.34, 6 months 0.71, 1 year 1, and four years 2." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~4:55, L229); ("it can take almost four years to prove that your advantage is non-zero. 6 weeks is literally a coin toss." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~6:16, L284); and he concedes the selection bias in his own process: ("Personally, I never ran 100 backtests. I probably made thousands and didn't count a single one. Each pattern I noticed was chosen because it had already worked." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~4:37, L217).

**His own statistics.** Five win rates appear and the dates matter. June 2026, the FX Replay session (the extractor's claims date it to May; no quote names the month): ("So I took 56 trades and I won 37 of them. Average win was just I'll just do 750 and then average loss 500 because that's what I pretty much what I follow for all of them" — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~49:03, L1326); ("67% win rate. Uh, definitely not enough trades to consider it like a solid uh win rate, but obviously the strategy works." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~47:28, L1284); 2.67 trades a day with six red days (~47:55, L1296); no $2,000 day and a max drawdown read as "120", probably $1,200 (~47:17, L1279; medium). July 2026, the 80-trade sample: ("I have 2 weeks of data, 80 trades, 8 days, 10 trades per day, showing you exactly what I did, and how I achieved a 57.5% win rate with a risk-reward ratio of 1.5" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~0:15, L47); ("That's 80 operations being extremely aggressive. Remember, you can improve this if you are much more conservative and only take structure breakout trades." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~26:49, L1003); inside it one day was six wins and six losses (~12:05, L473), one 13 trades all long (~20:17, L768) and the news day 13 trades with 10 wins (~21:09, L799). July 2026, the Monte Carlo input: ("This was also simulated with a 40% success rate." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~8:19, L353). August and September 2026, the long-run edge: ("Remember that it's a 1.5. On average, you'll win about 41% of the time, realistically speaking." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~18:22, L687); ("my specific advantage is a 41% success rate. 41% with a risk-benefit ratio of 1.5. The good thing about this is that it allows me to trade 10 times a day. Basically, I'm trading with a reversal bias." — JJ, 2BtwoTCxcbc ~9:30, L379); ("My win rate is much lower than this in reality when I'm doing 1.5R trades. You could take the better setups for a slightly higher win rate." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~25:07, L806); ("obviously you lose about half the trades you take" — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~1:03, L58; medium). The latest number he attaches to his own edge is 41% at 1.5R (2026-09-03), one point above the 40% break-even he uses in the Monte Carlo; the 57.5% is an eight-day sample he calls extremely aggressive, and the 67% a 56-trade replay he calls not solid. He says the full curve is known but gives no other number: ("I assume with a 1R trade, a 2R, a 3R, I know my win rate for these. I know my strategies win rate for these. I've tested it over a year of like actual trading and then eight years of back testing. I even know my win rate for a 0.5R if I ever wanted to try that." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~14:35, L429). Day-level frequency: ("15% of days don't revert. So, it's just going to be like that sometimes." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~25:13, L831; also BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~18:08, L591), with two or three reversion chances in the first 90 minutes even on a bad day (7UXiI2arAlQ ~5:48, L197). The round claims of 95% (7UXiI2arAlQ ~3:50, L138) and 99.9% (~11:01, L353) come from a seven-day chart walkthrough (~1:00, L53), not a count. One candle statistic: ("if you see a green candle, then there's a 50.5% chance the next candle is green. So, do with that what you will." — JJ, DEkzU8gqX-o ~10:14, L377).

**Monte Carlo and streak arithmetic.** ("This is a simulation that gives me a stop loss of $500 and assumes a loss limit of $4,000. It takes eight consecutive losses to blow up the account. And when you simulate this 6,000 times in 100 trades, you have virtually a 50/50 chance of blowing an account." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~7:51, L335). ("Imagine you have a 50% success rate, basically flipping a coin, and you're going to perform 100 trades. Your probability of losing four in a row is greater than 97%. Your probability of losing five in a row is 81%. And your probability of losing six in a row is 55%." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~6:34, L296). The September demonstration is edgeless by construction, a break-even win rate on 1:1 trades of plus or minus $500 with no drift, cost or slippage (sWJa8vRfPb8 ~12:45, L470), 100 random trades giving 47 wins and 53 losses with 10 trades per account (~13:09, L484), repeated at 1:1, 1:1.5 and 1:2 (~13:57, L512). The only stop-type comparison he reports: ("No. No. I found infinitely better to have a static stop loss." — JJ, -lxNWJGWtbg ~8:23, L343; medium).

**Third-party tests inside the corpus (host speech).** FX Replay (SNO1wqJTq5A, 2026-04-29): ("over all the period we tested, 54% win rates, 1.76 profit factor." — host, SNO1wqJTq5A ~41:41, L1411) on 158 trades; the earlier pass was 150 trades, 46R, 52%, PF 1.66 (~49:28, L1662); the filtered pass ("I removed those first three minutes. I removed the 10 and 11 hour versions. I removed the 3 to 4 p.m. hour versions. Now we have a 2.46 profit factor with a 62% win rate." — host, ~49:34, L1665); continuations had the higher win rate and a PF over 2 (~41:58, L1420); mean reversions ("59 or 49% win rate. Still 15R over the period, 1.46 profit factor" — host, ~43:53, L1482; medium) on 67 trades (~45:19, L1528); ("1.5 is optimal um for this strategy the the way we traded it." — host, ~42:57, L1452) with 1.6R slightly better (~43:12, L1460); sub-periods and streaks are in the table. The host says his testing focused mostly on the first leg away and back (~12:17, L464) and guesses his data depth at two years (~39:36, L1344). Backtesting with Vai (c61c4CxTpYI, 2026-06-09): ("I recently back tested JJ Simmons fair value strategy for two full months across 40 trades. The final result sounds like a dream. 37% return with a 54% win rate." — host, c61c4CxTpYI ~0:03, L45); April -4% on 20 trades, May +42% on 20 (~38:48, L877); ("40 trades is definitely not a adequate sample set to make a hard decision." — host, ~40:18, L909). NEXOR (JcW8Wjnw8ck, 2026-08-27), a 14-day simulated challenge using the 25/50-point rule at 1.5R (~2:20, L86): ("14 trading days, that's about 3 weeks of the market. 71 positions, 38 loss and 33 wins. That's 46.48% win rate." — host, ~6:01, L187); 5.1 positions a day (~5:37, L176); five returns to zero (~5:26, L171); passed on one strong day (~5:52, L183); ("B tier. Cuz, you know, it did the job. It survived, this is so important. And it actually passed the prop firm test, but it was such a pain, bro." — host, ~6:23, L197). Nothing the NEXOR host says supports the dossier's "forward test" label; the transcript reads as a replay.
