**Evolution and what is current.** The objective never changed: pass rate on evaluations, payouts per funded account per year on funded accounts (March 2026 3L8xdh3oPm4 through September 2026 sWJa8vRfPb8). The simulation count is 50 evals already in the July recording (KHEQ5g55dQ4 L874, after a 10-account worked example at L784) and 50 evals plus 50 funded in September (0Ua-ffltO0I, dJdBnSBJlgQ); the latest is 50/50. The minimum trade count went 50 (May) to 250 (August) to 500+ (3 September) and back to 200 (22 and 29 September); the latest is 200 trades plus 50 evals and 50 funded. His reported win rate went 67% (June replay, 56 trades) to 57.5% (July, 80 trades) to 41% (16 August and 3 September) and "about half" (29 September); the latest explicit figure is 41% at 1.5R. The 15% no-reversion-day figure is stable (17 and 20 September).

**Contradictions.** (1) 57.5% (KN7j6NXXAio L47) versus 41% (2BtwoTCxcbc L379, 0uAQUHEB_L8 L687) versus the 40% Monte Carlo input (J8qXxtFFpGg L353) versus "lose about half" (nvOaN6mRu-o L58): the dossier (Sections 4 and 6) and `fpt.cli edge --p 0.575` use the eight-day figure; his later statements put the edge barely above break-even. (2) "I only need to optimize it for the pass rate" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L1179) versus the funded-side objective he describes everywhere else (payouts per funded, KHEQ5g55dQ4 L845); the former is the evaluation half only. (3) In MVP7X-3v8xk he reads the drawdown table both ways within a minute, "you would have lost it like backtoback days here" (L1274, medium) then "no instant $2,000 draw down streak" (L1279, medium). (4) The quant video says four years are needed to prove a non-zero edge (PMGhHCjYG5c L284); the week before, 250 trades, one month at his cadence, suffice (MtBZeAkYH8Q L69, L79). (5) The dossier's Section 7 attributes "80 trades, 57.5%" to MVP7X-3v8xk; that video reports 56 trades and 37 wins; the 80-trade sample is KN7j6NXXAio (2026-07-25). (6) The dossier's Section 8 calls the NEXOR test a forward test; the corpus does not say so. (7) The dossier says mechanised versions fire about 1.2-1.3 trades a day; his own replay fired 2.67 and NEXOR's 5.1, both still far below the 10 a day of the 80-trade sample. (8) 10 trades a day (2BtwoTCxcbc L379, KN7j6NXXAio L47) versus 20 a day in the 7,000-trade arithmetic (PMGhHCjYG5c L633).

## 9. Prop-firm rule parameters (as of 2026-10-05)

Researched in `docs/research/prop-firm-rules.md` (58 queries, official help
centers where marked) and encoded in `fpt/propfirm.py` (`FIRM_PRESETS`,
each with `verified` and `source`). Presets cover 8 of the 17 firms he
names; Bulwark Prime ($55,000 of his payouts), TickTick Trader, FFF, Bulenox,
Futures Elite, Take Profit Trader, Funding Futures, BGF, Phidias, FXIFY and
TopOneFutures have no preset or research row yet; his own statements supply partial rows for Bulenox ($1,500 cap on the 50K, three funded accounts, 10 trading days, 40% consistency: 5RzMu2B2E_0 L458, L473), Take Profit Trader (80% split, intraday trailing: L534; live accounts withdrawable the same night: q8PXXvY9lS0 L271), FundedNext (80% split, $1.2K daily per account, Bolt pays from day one above a $2,000 profit buffer: L146, L153), Elite Trader Funding (about $1,000 caps: L346), GoFunded Futures (80%, payout windows about the 8th-10th and 20th-23rd: L384, medium) and The Trading Pit ($2,000 buffer, about 80%: L704, medium); see docs/research/synthesis/firms_payouts.md. account:

And you can have 20 of them", 5RzMu2B2E_0 L118; of intraday trailing he says "Do not buy accounts with dynamic scaling", PMGhHCjYG5c L570) | $790 one-time + $69 (third-party) | n/a / $3,000 trailing, safety net +$100 | none on the intraday plan; present on his EOD accounts | 50% in PA | 5 qualifying days, $500 min, weekly | 100% of first $25k then 90/10 | 20 per household |

Two facts matter most for replication. Every firm allows copying between
accounts you own (Alpha only from an external master). And the official
funded-account caps (Topstep 5, MyFundedFutures 3-5, Tradeify 5, Lucid 5,
Alpha 5, Apex 20) sum to about 45, matching the "45+ accounts" in the
Chart Fanatics blurb. That match is this dossier's inference, not his
statement: in the episode he says 45 accounts twice but never splits them by
firm, says the firm does not matter as long as risk is tuned to its rules,
and names only Lucid, Tradeify and (probably) Topstep himself; every Apex
mention there is the host's sponsor read. "Funded Engineer", credited with ~$180,000 in
his July 2026 video, was an FX prop firm that filed for bankruptcy on 15
July 2024, before his trading career began; the transcript almost
certainly mishears another firm's name (candidates on his own list: FFF,
Funding Futures; outside it, Funded Futures Network). The caps of these six
firms sum to 43-45 (Alpha's cap is per plan type, MFFU's is 3 with any 100K
account); E8, FundedNext, Bulwark, Fundedseat and the other firms he lists
are not in the sum. The simulator's cap-feasible 45-account mix:
`--account topstep_100k:5 --account tradeify_100k_growth:5 --account
mffu_100k_pro:3 --account lucid_100k_flex:5 --account alpha_100k_standard:5
--account apex_100k_intraday:20 --account e8_100k_signature:2`.

**News and operating constraints that bear on the method** (official help
centers unless noted; `fpt/propfirm.py` models none of them): MyFundedFutures
funded accounts may hold no positions or orders two minutes either side of
FOMC, FOMC minutes, the employment report and CPI, so the 08:30 news trade is
unavailable there; Topstep allows news but bars new opening transactions on
the index minis for five minutes either side of CPI (from 2026-09-11);
Tradeify has no news restriction; Apex allows news trading with brackets
(third-party comparison). Topstep sells at most 20 Combines a month per
trader, switches the copier off while a payout is processed, and sets the
loss limit to $0 (the start balance) after the first payout; Apex requires
copied accounts to trade the same direction with no hedging; Lucid caps a
household at 10 evaluations and 5 funded accounts; Alpha allows copying only
from an external master into Alpha.

<!-- target: Section 9 -->
### From the primary corpus: winning days, payout cadence and timing

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 The payout rules are the floor; the balance is the ceiling.* He describes the funded account as a container whose contents can only be withdrawn in the slices the payout rules allow: ("Your funded account balance is the ceiling, the payout rules are the floor. The problem is most traders get caught in between those." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~4:33, L229); ("which is the entire reason why the money that you see in your sim funded account can never fully be withdrawn as a payout, and that is why rules are what actually matter on prop firms more than the strategy." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:56, L123). The gates he lists: ("Once you get to the funded account, well, now you have to make another X amount of dollars, you have to achieve a buffer, you have to meet consistency, you have to meet their winning day rule, and then you can only take out 50% of what you make." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~2:11, L133); ("Some have minimum balance requirements, minimum days of earnings, withdrawal limits, and consistency rules that reset your cycle if you don't meet them." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~8:54, L374). The motive he attributes to the firms: ("That is why they put so many rules on the eval, so many rules on the funded account, is because they want to reduce your expected value as much as possible, so they can keep making as much money as possible" — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~1:49, L118). The funded account, not the evaluation, is where repeat payouts come from: ("The eval ends completely the instant you pass it. On a funded account though, it doesn't end until you breach a rule. You can take multiple payouts from a funded account. That should be your goal." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~4:14, L216).

*1.2 The standard cycle: five winning days of $150, then half the balance up to a cap.* He states this for Topstep, for Lucid and for "the top firms" generally: ("And their payout rules are literally just winning days. So, five winning days $150 and you can take out half of your balance up to 5k." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~18:05, L632) (Topstep 150K); ("On the funded account, to get the payout, you need 5 days of $150, and you can withdraw 50% of your profit up to $2,000." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~1:21, L65) (Lucid 50K Flex); ("Withdrawal requirements at most funded accounts, such as Topstep, Lucid, Fritify, any of the top firms, require five winning days of $150." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~6:17, L256; medium confidence). The $150 is a threshold: ("You have to realize that you need $150 to get a winning day. If you get less, it doesn't count. If you get more, it does count. But, remember that you need five of them to get to the payout." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~8:43, L293). Winning days accumulate and are never lost on down days: ("At this point, you'll have five winning days, and then you can withdraw half of the balance, which is $1,500." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~8:11, L324); for a coin-flip trader ("it will take you 10 days on average to get five winning days" — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~8:47, L345). The two phases have different objectives: ("Evaluation, only earn $3,000. Funded account to win any amount of money, five winning days and then withdraw the payment" — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~6:06, L266). Cadence at most firms: ("most of the time you can't withdraw more than once per week, obviously depending on the firm rules, sometimes you can, but most firms are going to let you get one payout every five winning days." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~5:29, L267); In the weekly videos the rule produces deliberately tiny trades taken only to log a day: ("I was just trying to qualify my account towards payout, so I needed like a $200 win, a $400 win, and a $150 win, so I was just like tiny little scalp." — JJ, EyR3i2GxwyA ~15:31, L541); and targets set by what the account still needs: ("a top tick, take profit just cuz my account needed to pay. So that's just how much it went for." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~32:12, L989); ("I needed, I don't know, 5,100, that amount of profit to get the maximum payout." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~6:45, L251)..

*1.3 Daily payouts, windows and timing.* Daily-payout accounts change the funded routine to taking the maximum as soon as the account is eligible: ("Um, but the funds, I'm trading these daily payout accounts. So, we'll just take the max really quickly here for the first account. Okay. Well, it's not eligible." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~6:19, L230); ("And they're apparently going to let me take a daily payout of 1.2k from each account, I think, forever." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~3:35, L146) (FundedNext); ("And then, I have one scheduled for tomorrow as soon as I have another winning day." — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~8:34, L310; medium); ("And then vest markets. I started trying out this firm because I saw it online and it's paying daily payouts." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~31:54, L980); ("They also have the select plan, so you can choose daily payouts, or you can choose weekly payouts that are much larger." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~0:41, L49) (Tradeify). Live accounts at Take Profit Trader: ("The great thing about these real accounts is that you can withdraw your earnings that same night." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~7:16, L271). Processing: ("2,500 more are coming from Hola Prime. They usually ship it within an hour" — JJ, pwO9IAu7FUQ ~13:16, L464). Windows: ("they have payout windows, so you can only request a payout on a specific set of days. I think it's the 8th to the 10th and the 20th to 23rd, something like that." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~10:41, L384; medium) (GoFunded). Frictions he records: ("I don't think they're going to update the dashboard because it is Friday. So, I can't really request after the market close unfortunately." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~16:37, L556); The lumpiness is structural: ("like how close you are to the next payout varies pretty much. So, you get a large chunk of payouts one week and then none the next week. It's totally fine. Just be consistent month to month, not week to week." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~28:57, L950).

**2. Concrete numbers**

| What | Value | Applies to | Citation |
|---|---|---|---|

**3. How the statements evolved**

The mechanics (five winning days of $150, half the balance per request, at most about weekly) are stated the same way from 5RzMu2B2E_0 (2026-04-12) through wsKx7L9EJys (2026-05-10), S8fSNbPFuLA (2026-08-06), 0uAQUHEB_L8 (2026-08-16) and 0Ua-ffltO0I (2026-09-10); nothing later retracts them. Daily-payout accounts enter the routine over the summer: FundedNext's $1.2K daily is announced in April (5RzMu2B2E_0), its next-day scheduling appears in August (pwO9IAu7FUQ), "these daily payout accounts" in September (HlWSP7ajgpQ) and Vest Markets in October (PN1UKQMPb5M, 2026-10-04, latest). The weekly videos from June to October 2026 show the rule in operation: tiny qualifying scalps on a holiday (EyR3i2GxwyA, July), targets set to the amount an account still needs (pwO9IAu7FUQ, August; PN1UKQMPb5M, October), a Friday request blocked by a stale dashboard and a Topstep payout reversed (HlWSP7ajgpQ, September; MIWsdldzJgQ, July). The $30K-a-week goal is from September 2026 (HlWSP7ajgpQ).

**4. Contradictions**

Within his statements: (a) he says of the Lucid 50K Flex ("you can withdraw 50% of your profit up to $2,000." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~1:21, L65) and of Topstep ("you can take out half of your balance up to 5k." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~18:05, L632); on a sim account whose balance is start plus profit these are different bases, and the 22-account model uses "50% of your balance" (0uAQUHEB_L8 L369) while the $3,000-to-$1,500 step in wsKx7L9EJys uses half the profit; the corpus does not settle which base a given firm uses. (b) ("most firms are going to let you get one payout every five winning days." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~5:29, L267) (Sept 2026) sits beside firms he says pay daily (FundedNext, Vest, Tradeify Select Daily, Take Profit Trader live); the generic statement is about "most", so this is scope, not contradiction. With the dossier: Section 9's Topstep row gives "5 winning days of $150+, $125 min, $3,000-$4,000 per request" without the half-balance limit he states and without the $5,000 he gives for the 150K. `PropAccount.month_end` pays once a month and counts every traded day toward `payout_min_days`; he describes one payout per five winning days of at least $150, roughly every 10 trading days for a coin-flip trader, at most weekly at most firms and daily at some. Section 5 describes the simulator's "monthly cadence" as if it were a firm rule; no firm rule he states is monthly except GoFunded's two windows.

### From the primary corpus: payout caps, profit splits, buffers and direct accounts

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 Caps per request.* Topstep's cap is the one he builds the 150K cycle around: ("They have 5k max payout, which is insane. Not many other firms will do that. They'll cap usually at 3k. I know Alpha does 15k, but this thing costs like three times the amount of as a Topstep and it gives you I don't know, just too many rules." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~17:40, L618); ("You can get like three accounts up to 10k profit and take a 15k payout, which is really, really a good amount of money. And it's honestly not incredibly hard to make 10k if you're on the 150k's because you're given $4,500 worth of drawdown." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~17:53, L625); ("Topstep is a good firm for you because they have a 5K max payout. And that's 50% of your balance." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~16:31, L551). Caps scale with account size, not linearly: ("Payment limits increase from 2K to 5K. Sometimes they go up from two to three. In that account it's fine to operate exactly the same, but most will go up from two to five or maybe four." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~8:07, L308). Small caps disqualify a firm: ("The payout caps are really really small like $1,000 and there's no real reason to be trading when you have 1K payout caps, or you can go to Topstep and get a 5K payout cap," — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~9:33, L346) (Elite Trader Funding); ("the payout cap is 1.5k on the 50k. Anything larger than the 50k, it's not really worth your money because the activation fee is so large." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~12:54, L458) (Bulenox). E8 is his clearest case of a cap moving under him: ("Back when I was trading them, they had uncapped payouts after the fifth one. Now it's it's capped" — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~16:48, L589); ("Look, this one's from E8. Of course, they've changed their rules since their maximum payout is now $3,000" — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~3:55, L165). The uncapped era produced his largest single payout: ("$15,000 was the optimal profit target for this account. The reason? They had unlimited payments. So, there is a payment of $45,000." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~12:36, L446). Tradeify: ("Tradeify was going to get a maximum payout of $25,000" — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:45, L54) (the plan is not named). Lucid, itemised: ("16 for 3K, 5 for 3,500, and then the live account payout for 46K. This much in payouts, 90% split, gave me this much in profit." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~6:45, L249).

*1.2 Profit splits.* ("They also have, I believe it's an 80% split, whereas Lucid and Topstep have 90." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~19:53, L692) (MyFundedFutures); ("funding firms usually pay a 90% split. So, 55*90% will give you 49.5, so basically 50k." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~6:00, L246); ("If you have $3,000 in profits, that account is technically only worth $2,700 because funding companies give you a 90% share." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~3:08, L151). An 80% split is what keeps a firm out of his top tier: ("So, uh 80% split is why it's not the S tier" — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~3:35, L146) (FundedNext); ("The only problem is 80% profit split and the intraday trailing drawdown." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~15:10, L534) (Take Profit Trader); ("The only problem is 80% split, and they have payout windows" — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~10:41, L384; medium) (GoFunded Futures); ("I believe it's 80% split, but don't quote me on that." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~20:14, L704; medium) (The Trading Pit). On his record day he expected the lower figure for the accounts involved: ("these are Funded accounts, so I will probably get 80% of this in a payout." — JJ, RnwOqmEF6Rk ~1:00, L77).

*1.3 Buffers, profit thresholds and direct (instant-funded) accounts.* ("I would recommend just going with the bolt just because you're able to get payouts on your first day. So, if you make $3,000 in your first day, then you can withdraw anything above $2,000 of profit. So, you can withdraw 1,000, which is amazing." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~3:47, L153) (FundedNext Bolt); ("if you make $5,000 on your funded account and get some winning days, you can withdraw anything over $2,000 buffer balance." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~20:14, L704; medium) (The Trading Pit). Lucid's direct accounts replace winning days with a profit threshold plus a 20% consistency rule: ("I was buying the direct accounts because I loved the instant funded option. Just saved me a ton of time, and I needed 9K to get the payouts. Just the requirement that they have, and they have a 20% consistency rule." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~7:02, L258); ("Now, basically just take 20% of 9K, and you get 1,800. So, 1,800, I need to win that five times or more in order to get a payout." — JJ, C-_0QhKZmA4 ~7:12, L264). The threshold steps down after each payout: ("It's a direct account. The new rule they gave him was that he needs to earn $3,000 or something like that for his next payment. Initially, you need to earn 9,000 for your first payment. Now you need to earn 4,500 or now you need to earn 3,000." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~9:55, L357), so the optimal target moves with it: ("Now, the optimal profit target is any value between 1,000 and 1,200, which should have been followed here." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~9:26, L341).

**3. How the statements evolved**

E8's cap: uncapped after the fifth payout when he earned the $45,000 (it "arrived in July", said in March 2026, so July 2025), "capped" by April 2026 (5RzMu2B2E_0), "$3,000" by August 2026 (4BXpI-hYqe0, latest). Topstep's $5K cap is stated in March 2026 (aBVn6IUAjnM) and April 2026 and is the basis of the June 2026 10K/half cycle (aCOgfvL6lK8); not revised. Lucid's direct accounts ($9K threshold, 20% consistency) are described in June 2026 (C-_0QhKZmA4) and the stepping threshold (9,000, 4,500, 3,000) in July (AxP-cg50TdM); no later video revises them. Splits: the firm-specific statement (90 at Lucid and Topstep, 80 at MFF, FundedNext, Take Profit Trader) is from April 2026 and is not revised; in his own arithmetic he uses "80% of this" on the record day (RnwOqmEF6Rk, 2026-04-09), "usually pay a 90% split" in the August model (0uAQUHEB_L8) and "0.9x" lifetime in October (r5AhmGaFujU, 2026-10-01, latest). FundedNext Bolt's day-one payout above a $2,000 buffer is from April 2026 only.

**4. Contradictions**

Within his statements: (a) caps are "usually at 3k" (Apr 2026) and, in the generic 50K-to-150K case, $2K rising to $5K "or maybe four" (Aug 2026); compatible only as a range. (b) The 80% expectation for his record day (April) against the 90% "usually" (August) are different firms, not a revision. (c) Alpha "does 15k" (April) while he also says it has "too many rules" and is among his lowest-EV firms; the cap is not what ranks it. With the dossier: Section 9's E8 row carries a ladder "then up to $25,000" against his "maximum payout is now $3,000"; its Alpha row's "$4,000 max" against his "Alpha does 15k" (plan unstated); the FundedNext preset in `fpt/propfirm.py` uses a 0.95 split against his 80%; `lucid_100k_flex.payout_max` is 2,500 for the 100K where he gives $2,000 for the 50K (consistent if the cap scales). `PropAccount.month_end` withdraws the whole profit above the buffer up to the cap, where he withdraws half the balance; the buffer field exists but no preset carries FundedNext Bolt's $2,000 or Lucid direct's stepping threshold.

### From the primary corpus: consistency rules and payout sequencing

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 Consistency rules and the maximum day they imply.* The evaluation standard: ("Uh the evals are pretty standard, just 50% consistency, same as I guess the majority of evaluation sites." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~18:05, L632); ("For the evaluation on the left side, you have a 50% consistency rule, $2,000 max loss, $3,000 profit target." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~0:56, L52) (Lucid 50K Flex); ("The most common evaluation rule set is a $3,000 profit target and a $2,000 max loss limit." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~21:19, L614). With a $3,000 target the rule caps the best day at $1,500: ("The evals have a max win of 1.5K cuz they have 50% consistency rule." — JJ, BLvsYJ4sqn8 ~19:01, L618); ("So, uh, I'm just going to go for 1500 because you don't want to win more than half of your thing in one day because consistency rule." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~38:41, L1051); ("if you make more than $1.5,000 on an eval, then you're breaking the consistency rule. You're going to have to make even more." — JJ, wsKx7L9EJys ~9:36, L320; medium). Why he limits winners: ("So automatically when I'm assigned a consistency rule, I'm trying to limit my winners. If you win too much, then you have to win even more and more and more and your chance of getting that payout decreases because your profit target increases by way too much." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~11:03, L410); ("since the evaluations have consistency rules. You don't want to earn too much on an assessment, but of course you could" — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~5:03, L197). One video words the rule per trade rather than per day: ("some with winning days, and of course, the rule of not making more than half in a single trade" — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~3:13, L158). Tighter rules: the 20% on instant-funded consistency accounts (caps block above, 1.3; ("If you're someone who likes small wins, maybe an instant funded consistency account is best for you because they have 20% consistency. And if you're winning like massive wins at a time, consistency is not something in your favor." — JJ, aBVn6IUAjnM ~16:40, L556)), with its R arithmetic ("The reason why is in order to pass, if it's 20% consistency, then you need 20 RR. Basically, 20%* 4" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~44:13, L1424; medium), ("gives you 20 because it's five trades of four riskreward to be up 20." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~44:19, L1427), ("It is going to take about 3 to 6 months to get your 20 RR if you're doing minus 1R plus 4R once a day. And the reason for that is because of oscillation." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~44:29, L1432); 40% at Bulenox with a minimum-day count ("the only problem is you need 10 trading days and 40% consistency. So, it does take a really long time and the payout is only 1,500 on the 50K and you can only have three at once, so with 22 trading days in a month, you're getting a 9K payout max per month," — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~13:20, L473). A student on the 30-40% variants: ("I've noticed that typically I'll struggle a lot more with passing evals on 30% or the 40 sorry, 40% consistency. I think it's just something to do with that you have to hit the trade three times in a row." — other, VY-bO35-tjE ~3:45, L168). The rule decides where large targets can be used at all: ("the larger win, I'm able to realize all of that expected value if the prop firm does not have a consistency rule. Same if the prop firm does not have a strict policy where they move you to a live account." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~8:50, L342); ("Some of these sites are best for people who like to do large winning days. Some of them are best for people who like to do plus 500, 500, 500, very consistent winning days." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~6:20, L238); ("Maybe an account's 100% consistency, but it's got a 2K payout cap. This one's maybe it's got a 5k payout cap. The same strategy is going to perform extremely different on these two." — JJ, nvOaN6mRu-o ~12:28, L371).

*1.2 Sequencing: the 10k / half cycle and its recursion.* ("I try to get 5k per payout. So, I'll bring my account to 10k, take out half, back to 10k, take out half, and just keep doing that. So, 150k divided by five is going to give you 30 payouts." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:06:11, L2302); ("Technically, like it's basically like one payout a day for the month for $5,000. And it's definitely not the hardest thing to do if you're running 40 accounts at a time. So, you could even run 30 accounts. You would just have to keep refreshing them if they lose." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:06:22, L2308); ("If I'm starting from eval, it's pass rate multiplied by chance of reaching $10,000 balance because I want to get to a $10,000 balance on my account and then take out 5,000, which would be the max." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~39:23, L1399). On a 50K with a 2K cap: ("you could either grow it back up to 4k to take the max payout of 2k, or you could just do some winning days and then take out a thousand because you had 2,000. So, both of both approaches work. It's the the same expected value for both approaches just because of how recursion works for funded account values." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~1:07:40, L2352); ("if you make $3,000, you can withdraw half, more winning days, withdraw half, that sort of thing. So, it should be pretty easy to get at least $2,000 back from an account at $3,000, that is a funded account." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~4:09, L173); ("if you're able to take a payout every 5 winning days and you are a break-even trader, then on average in the long run, you take out half of it, you take out another half of it." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~4:21, L170; medium). His calculator's base rates: ("Payout rate is 33%. Basically, how many of your funded accounts get to payout and then average payout size is obviously it's just the average payout size. Right now, it's at 4% at 50k. So, it's just $2,000." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~0:49, L58); ("Payout size of $2,000 on a 50k account is a pretty good amount just because you have $2,000 worth of drawdown. So, over the lifetime of your account, you can expect to get about $2,000 back from it." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~1:04, L67); the half-withdrawal haircut ("You'll get up to plus 2,000 50% of the time if you're a break-even trader. So, but from there, you can only withdraw $1,000." — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~1:18, L75); his preferred direction of optimisation ("try to increase the payout rate more towards 50%. Even if that decreases your payout down to 3%, you're still gaining a monthly increase on your statistics, right?" — JJ, XhsbfEdJBAc ~8:29, L323). Lifetime extraction ratio: ("the usual metric that I use is 0.9x of if I have 42K in my funded accounts over the lifetime of those accounts, I'm going to receive 0.9x times that in payouts. So, about 36K in in this example, maybe a bit more." — JJ, r5AhmGaFujU ~20:11, L630). Realised average: ("It is going to be about 483 payouts if we assume the average $4,000 payout per payout. Yes, cuz the average on the 150ks is probably 4.5K after the split" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~5:38, L190). The 22-account month model: ("Remember that we have 22 of these accounts. So, that would be a payment of 22*1,500, which should be 33,000" — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~8:20, L329); ("Now, you can withdraw another payment for 50% of your balance across the 22 accounts, which would be a payment of 22*750. This gives a payment of 16,500." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~9:27, L369); ("A month has 22 trading days and three payout cycles require five winning days, okay? So, with 22 trading days, we need to dedicate at least 15 to winning days, and that leaves us seven for evaluation." — JJ, 0uAQUHEB_L8 ~13:50, L525). Proximity to the next payout is itself value: ("So $4,000 worth of profit and you're qualifying for a $2,000 payout pretty soon. Well, your expected value is probably going to be a lot higher" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~10:15, L321). Journal fields: ("Keep track of how much you spend on assessments. Record how much you receive in payments. Track your chances of getting paid into funded accounts. And record the size of the payments in your funded accounts." — JJ, J8qXxtFFpGg ~13:32, L557).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The 50% evaluation consistency rule and the $1,500 day cap are stated identically in wsKx7L9EJys (2026-05-10), MVP7X-3v8xk (2026-06-10), BLvsYJ4sqn8 (2026-09-17) and KHEQ5g55dQ4 (2026-10-04, latest). The 20% consistency account appears in March 2026 (aBVn6IUAjnM) as a style match for small-win traders, in June as his own Lucid direct accounts (C-_0QhKZmA4) and in October as the 20R arithmetic (KHEQ5g55dQ4). Payout-size goal: $2,000 average at a 33% payout rate (XhsbfEdJBAc, 2026-06-17); $5,000 max per payout via the 10K cycle five days later (aCOgfvL6lK8, 2026-06-22); $4,000 realised average over ~483 payouts (dJdBnSBJlgQ, 2026-09-22); 0.9x lifetime extraction (r5AhmGaFujU, 2026-10-01, latest); $30K a week toward $120K a month (HlWSP7ajgpQ, 2026-09-13). The 22-account month model (0uAQUHEB_L8, 2026-08-16) and the 10-funded-account model (wsKx7L9EJys, 2026-05-10) are teaching models, not his own account counts.

**4. Contradictions**

Within his statements: (a) the consistency rule is ("you don't want to win more than half of your thing in one day because consistency rule." — JJ, MVP7X-3v8xk ~38:41, L1051) and ("the rule of not making more than half in a single trade" — JJ, KhooqEQK9bA ~3:13, L158); the per-day reading is the one his $1,500 cap implements. (b) The June calculator uses a $2,000 average payout on 50K accounts while the September average is $4,000; the latter is dominated by 150K accounts ("the average on the 150ks is probably 4.5K after the split" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~5:38, L190), so the two are different account mixes. (c) He says both approaches after a half-withdrawal have "the same expected value" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L2352) and, in XhsbfEdJBAc, that he would push the payout rate toward 50% even at a smaller payout; the first is a statement about one account's recursion, the second about monthly cash across accounts. With the dossier: Section 9's Lucid Flex row shows no evaluation consistency and `lucid_100k_flex.consistency_pct` is None, while he states 50% for the 50K Flex evaluation; the Topstep preset uses 0.55 where he says "just 50% consistency" (the help center may be right for the Combine; he may be rounding). `PropAccount.month_end` withdraws the full profit above the buffer, where he withdraws half and treats the remaining half as the seed of the next cycle; and `PortfolioConfig.trading_days_per_month` is 21 where he counts 22.

### From the primary corpus: drawdown types, daily loss limits and account caps

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 The drawdown is the account.* ("Account size is marketing. The drawdown is the real account that is assigned to you. If you buy a $100,000 valuation from a firm, they don't give you $100,000 in operating capital. You are generally given a maximum loss limit of $3,000." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~3:36, L169); ("Obviously, the account has a headline size like 50k, 150k. That doesn't mean anything. You're given 2k of draw down or maybe you're given 4.5k of draw down on the 150k." — JJ, sWJa8vRfPb8 ~4:34, L185); ("when you're only allowed one more contract but your loss limit goes from 2K to 4,500, that's a 1.5x increase. This is more than a 1.5x increase, so you're not linearly scaling your maximum loss with respect to the number of contracts you hold." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~7:51, L299). On the funded side the drawdown is the only hard constraint: ("if you're on that funded account, you're given $2,000 of max loss. You don't have a set profit target. You just have to make some money, and then you can take a payout once you meet the payout rules." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~3:47, L151); ("On your funded account, you do have a different target. Now, your target is to get as much as you possibly can in the form of payouts before hitting your minus 2,000." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~6:23, L238).

*1.2 End-of-day versus intraday trailing.* The trailing mechanism: ("That maximum loss limit is also dynamic. So, as you earn money, it follows you upwards. If you win $3,000, then your new maximum loss limit would be $100,000." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~3:47, L176); ("You could win 3,000 one day and lose 3,000 the next, and you'd still lose track." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~4:02, L186; medium; "lose track" is the caption for losing the account); ("every time it goes up, you lose a little expected value. Because maybe you earn $2,000, but the reduction keeps going up and now you're back to having only $2,000, even though you made money." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~4:38, L209); against small targets, ("So if you win 2,000 in 10 trades, the drawdown will follow you 10 times, got it" — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~28:57, L985). The two types and when the end-of-day one locks: ("The way intraday trailing drawdown works is instead of at the end of the day, like the end of day trailing drawdown, at 3:00 p.m. or sorry, 4:00 p.m. EST on an end of day, your drawdown will trail up and lock." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~15:10, L534). His rule: ("trailing drawdown is much worse. Do not buy accounts with dynamic scaling. They have significantly less EV than accounts with end-of-day reduction." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~13:20, L570) (the captions' "dynamic scaling" and "end-of-day reduction" mean intraday trailing and end-of-day drawdown). The strategy is built around the end-of-day type: ("The just since the trailing drawdown moves up at the end of the day on the futures accounts, then you can obviously you obviously need to build a strategy around that. Basically, like I'm solving for max drawdown in my equity curve." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~7:07, L312). The market-derived stop is not what the drawdown respects: ("I hope the profit respects it. Obviously they don't, because prop firms have to make money. That's why they add all these rules." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~17:29, L738; medium).

*1.3 Daily loss limits.* Apex's end-of-day accounts carry one: ("the end of day accounts are a bit hard to get the payout on just because there's a daily loss limit, and there's a buffer, and there's consistency, but they're super cheap. And you can have 20 of them, which is why it's in the A tier." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~2:45, L118). FTMO's: ("Yeah, don't worry about the FTMO. Just with the DLL, it takes absolutely forever to pass those. So, it's it's standard. There's lots of oscillating between winning, losing. That's how the DLL accounts work." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~6:07, L257); the NEXOR host's test used ("Maximum loss limit is going to be 10%. The maximum daily loss is going to be 5%. That's FTMO rules, right?" — host, JcW8Wjnw8ck ~1:45, L70). The full list he optimises against: ("Funding firms impose a great many rules. They give you consistency rules, minimum winning days rules, profit targets, maximum drawdowns, and daily loss limits." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~7:13, L268). A smaller stop at one firm makes losses there expected when the other firm's accounts win: ("171 on one site and then 21 loss on the other. um just because they have a much much smaller stop loss. So it's obviously normal to lose those." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~4:42, L179; medium).

*1.4 Account caps.* Apex, 20 (1.3 above); ("And then I lost all of my E8. Two of these were fundeds and then the rest were eval because you can only have three funded." — JJ, l6iq0ljhxIo ~25:43, L847); ("Bulanox Bulanox is going to go B tier just because you can only have three funded accounts or something" — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~12:54, L458); the Lucid and TradeDay live programs ("they do cap it at five, which is fine." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~14:52, L522); his ramp at a new firm ("I'll start with one, I'll get the payout, reinvest that payout. Now, I'm going to run five funded accounts and go for max payouts." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~49:03, L1572).

**3. How the statements evolved**

The drawdown description is constant (S8fSNbPFuLA, 2026-08-06; sWJa8vRfPb8, 2026-09-15; 4BXpI-hYqe0, 2026-08-27). The end-of-day lock time and the Apex end-of-day description are from the April 2026 ranking (5RzMu2B2E_0). The intraday-trailing warning is from 2026-08-20 (PMGhHCjYG5c), after he had taken Take Profit Trader payouts in June and July 2026 (xTxrtFKlQfc, q8PXXvY9lS0); April's ranking already named its intraday trailing as "the only problem". FTMO's daily loss limit is discussed in September 2026 (VY-bO35-tjE). Account caps are stated once each (Apex and Bulenox April 2026, Lucid/TradeDay live May 2026, E8 September 2026) and not revised; the new-firm ramp to five funded accounts is from October 2026 (KHEQ5g55dQ4, latest).

**4. Contradictions**

Within his statements: (a) ("Do not buy accounts with dynamic scaling." — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~13:20, L570) (Aug 2026) against continued Take Profit Trader payouts in June and July 2026, a firm whose intraday trailing he had named in April; (b) the same live account is ("$4,500 dynamic drawdown at the close of the day." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~12:47, L452) and ("They have a daily loss limit of $4,500" — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~31:06, L1056) in one video, so one of the two labels is loose. With the dossier: Section 9's Apex row and the 45-account mix use `apex_100k_intraday` ("trailing_intraday", daily limit "none"), while he describes his Apex accounts as end-of-day accounts with a daily loss limit, buffer and consistency rule and tells viewers not to buy intraday-trailing accounts; `mffu_50k_rapid` is also intraday-trailing. The E8 allocation of 2 in the mix is inside his "you can only have three funded"; Bulenox (3) and the Lucid/TradeDay live cap (5) have no preset. The approximation note in `fpt/propfirm.py` (intraday trailing applied after each closed trade) is more lenient than the continuous trailing he describes.

### From the primary corpus: live transfer, bans and restrictions

**1. Rules in his own words**

*1.1 Live transfer: the generic rule and the $15,000 ceiling.* ("a large simulated balance would be great because you could get multiple payouts from that. On others, it would be horrible because you'd be moved to a live account very quickly and never realize any of that expected value." — JJ, 0Ua-ffltO0I ~4:24, L223); ("if you earn $15,000, they'll most likely move you to a live account." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~8:22, L353); ("The way real accounts work is that they remove your simulated capital and give you a real account. Generally, it follows the same rules as a newly funded account, but with a small bonus if you perform well." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~8:25, L355); ("In reality, you don't receive payments from those earnings you made in the simulation account." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~8:40, L365). His operating response: ("I know if I go live with this firm, I'm getting a $15,000 expected value account. So, what I'm going to do is I'm going to keep my SIM funded balance below $15,000." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~47:55, L1537); ("I'll I'll trade in between 13 and 14K. If I go over 15K, like I run it up to 20K and I get moved live, then I lost 5K in expected value." — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~48:05, L1542); ("different return on different sites because I structure my balances differently if I'm trying to avoid the live accounts" — JJ, dJdBnSBJlgQ ~26:19, L777). The $45,000 counter-example: ("In this account here, if I earned $45,000, great, but the firm would send me to a real account." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~12:36, L446); ("And that real account, $0. $4,500 dynamic drawdown at the close of the day. So, if I had this profit goal here and applied it to this firm, that $45,000 would have disappeared and I would have been left with a real account of $4,500. So I would have lost $40,000 just by making that simple change." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~12:47, L452); ("They give you five accounts with a zero dollar balance. They have a daily loss limit of $4,500, the same as the funded account you just boosted to $10,000 profit." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~31:06, L1056). The check he runs before sizing any account: ("First, I would look at the specific profit rules and see if, when I earn X amount in my account, how much will I receive in payouts over the life of this account? Will I be transferred to a live account? What is my risk of bankruptcy?" — JJ, PMGhHCjYG5c ~16:33, L700).

*1.2 Topstep's live program.* ("If Topstep saw $50,000 in your sim accounts and you went live, you would get a $10,000 live account." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~12:32, L444); ("Where did the other 40k go? Well, you have to unlock it. You have to unlock it through making bonuses. Every time you make 9k, then you get a bonus of 10k added to your account." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~12:37, L447); ("You have to do this four times in order to unlock the 40k that you had. So, automatically, your expected value of your account diminishes greatly, probably at least 50%." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~12:54, L456); ("I think Topstep is one of the worst live account programs in general now that they've kind of done this uh bonus unlock program where they just delete 80% of your profit and you earn it back." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~13:56, L491). Trigger and timing: ("if you're a very good trader and you're making a lot of money trading on prop firms, uh more than say 20k a month, then uh you need to be careful because Topstep live account, they will send you live uh pretty much at a random time." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~18:36, L649); ("So, if you make 50k in an Express Funded account or 10k on five, you only get a 10k live account and you have to unlock the rest in a bonus by trading." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~18:54, L659); in practice ("I was eligible for a payout, and then of course I was sent to a live account an hour before I could withdraw, so unfortunately there's no 5K Topstep payout this week." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~10:18, L372).

*1.3 Lucid, TradeDay and Tradeify live programs.* ("What they'll do is they'll give you a $0 live account. You get one $0 live account per funded account that has taken a payout on both of these firms." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~14:16, L502); ("For 150k accounts, you have a $4,500 max loss, and if you make $4,500, then you get a bonus of $4,500 out of your account." — JJ, vJuwbKibN1E ~14:43, L517); ("A quick little example with Lucid or Tradeify. If you make $4,500 on a 150K live, then you get a $4,500 bonus" — JJ, KHEQ5g55dQ4 ~52:00, L1662). Tradeify's live program: ("they have a very, very good live program that literally will pay you monthly salary to trade live for them, which is amazing. Not many other firms do that. The rules are very simple." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~0:41, L49), yet two months later ("they sent me to a live account even though I didn't meet any of the requirements for a live account." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:45, L54). A "funded seat" live product: ("I have 18K that I made on my funded seat live account. So, um I qualified for that final day today." — JJ, HlWSP7ajgpQ ~16:28, L551). The unnamed firm in the student roadmap: ("once you significantly increase your balance in this account with this specific site, you will be reviewed to be upgraded to the live version." — JJ, xNeBEqv06k0 ~6:47, L277).

*1.4 Bans, restrictions and deletions.* ("if you're trading with them, please do not make too much money on your funded account because they will send you to a live account and they will delete 75% of that of your profit." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~1:47, L86) (DayTraders); ("they of course didn't give a reason like most of firms that I trade with once I make 50 to 100k profit on they'll just send me a message saying hey you're not allowed to trade with us anymore." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~8:48, L321) (said of Funded Futures Family); ("I got let back on Alpha Futures. I was on their live account for 15 months, something like that. Just got back onto the funded accounts. Got the first payout for 1350 which is cool." — JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~7:03, L240); ("Apex, eventually you do get sent live, nothing you can do about that. Funded Next, we can avoid it as much as possible. Blue Guardian, that one's a little bit weird, but we'll try our best." — JJ, VY-bO35-tjE ~23:05, L898). In August 2026 he put it the other way: ("So there weren't too many problems with being banned, moved to real, restricted, all that kind of stuff. Only the two main firms that everyone knows do that" — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~16:36, L596) (the two are not named) and drew the portfolio rule from it: ("In short, you should diversify your risk across different firms as soon as you start making money, because you don't want to get blocked or switched to a real account." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~18:02, L645); ("Changes in rules, payment delays, or a firm going bankrupt only affect a portion of your capital. Mmm, basically this means diversifying across firms." — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~12:36, L460). Expected direction: ("The only problem is the live accounts on the prop firms. They're obviously getting more and more strict because they want to try their best to limit the profitable traders." — JJ, aCOgfvL6lK8 ~45:32, L1606). On live trading as such: ("It is much more valuable to operate in a funding account than in a real account right now because they are very profitable." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:27, L90); ("Please do not trade real accounts. In a live account, you need to be the best trader in the world because your competition in live accounts is the institutions" — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~27:56, L1043); but a firm's live account can have better terms: ("It's exactly the same, except you have better payout rules on a real account." — JJ, S8fSNbPFuLA ~3:19, L158); ("The great thing about these real accounts is that you can withdraw your earnings that same night." — JJ, q8PXXvY9lS0 ~7:16, L271) (Take Profit Trader).

| Alpha restriction | 15 months on the live account; first payout back $1,350 | Alpha | JJ, PN1UKQMPb5M ~7:03 L240 |
| Tradeify | $25,000 max payout forfeited to a live transfer | Tradeify | JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~0:45 L54 |

**3. How the statements evolved**

April 2026: Topstep sends high earners live at random, DayTraders deletes 75%, "most of firms" ban him at 50-100K (5RzMu2B2E_0). May 2026: the Topstep bonus-unlock arithmetic and the Lucid/TradeDay $0-live model (vJuwbKibN1E). June 2026: Tradeify moved him live without, he says, his meeting the requirements (CzxSgYujDxs). July 2026: the $45K / $40K lost-EV example and five $0 live accounts (AxP-cg50TdM); Topstep's transfer an hour before a payout (q8PXXvY9lS0); "Please do not trade real accounts" (KN7j6NXXAio). August 2026: the generic $15,000 threshold (S8fSNbPFuLA) and "there weren't too many problems" with bans (4BXpI-hYqe0). September 2026: Apex live inevitable, FundedNext avoidable, Blue Guardian unpredictable (VY-bO35-tjE); the $18K funded-seat live account (HlWSP7ajgpQ). October 2026: keep the sim balance under $15,000, the Lucid/Tradeify $4,500 bonus (KHEQ5g55dQ4) and the Alpha reinstatement after 15 months (PN1UKQMPb5M, latest). The latest position is therefore: expect transfer near $15,000 of sim profit unless the firm is one whose live program pays (Lucid, TradeDay, Tradeify, Take Profit Trader), and treat Topstep's as a loss of most of the sim balance.

**4. Contradictions**

Within his statements: (a) ("most of firms that I trade with once I make 50 to 100k profit on they'll just send me a message saying hey you're not allowed to trade with us anymore." — JJ, 5RzMu2B2E_0 ~8:48, L321) (Apr 2026) against ("there weren't too many problems with being banned, moved to real, restricted, all that kind of stuff. Only the two main firms that everyone knows do that" — JJ, 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~16:36, L596) (Aug 2026); the two firms are never named. (b) ("Please do not trade real accounts." — JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~27:56, L1043) (July 2026) against the Lucid live $46K, the Take Profit Trader live $5,000 and the funded-seat $18K, all firm-issued live accounts; the consistent reading is that he avoids self-funded live trading, not firm live programs with bonuses. (c) Tradeify is S tier for its live program in April and the firm that moved him live against its own requirements in June; by October he again uses "Lucid or Tradeify" as the live-bonus example. With the dossier: Section 9 states Topstep "sets the loss limit to $0 (the start balance) after the first payout"; the corpus neither confirms nor contradicts it. No preset models a live transfer, a sim-balance ceiling, a ban, a payout reversal or a bonus-unlock schedule, so `fpt/portfolio.py` lets a funded account grow without bound where he caps it near $15,000.

## 10. Contradictions, unknowns and verification status

Statements that conflict or evolved over time (each is a rule the
replication must choose a version of; the "corpus:" items come from his own
transcripts, dated, and the latest dated version is the default choice):
1. Continuation phase length: his own figures move from 0-10 minutes (March 2026, 3L8xdh3oPm4 L117) through 10-15 and 15 minutes (June, UVKVSWKFlvo L310; gjWiGBiVis0 L460) to 5 minutes (July and October, KN7j6NXXAio L95; KHEQ5g55dQ4 L2766, the latest); fxreplay and AndrewFXTD codify 10-15.
2. Reversion window: full 85 minutes to 11:00 (JJ) vs first ~30 minutes only (fxreplay's filtered result).
3. Fair value anchor: 09:30 open (fxreplay, AndrewFXTD) vs the candle right before the open (365-day backtester) vs six selectable sources (joetroyer); in his own usage the 9:29 body and the 9:30 open are one reference ("you can also dial half of 929. It's more or less the same thing." — JJ, CzxSgYujDxs ~22:59, L762), and the real alternates are the pre-news candle, the pre-open consolidation and the most recent consolidation (Section 2, primary-corpus blocks).
4. Trades per day: the $1.6M video's 10 is the New York morning count and the same video says "There is no maximum number of transactions." (JJ, KN7j6NXXAio ~1:45, L101); his day totals are about 20 ("about 20 trades during a whole trading day. Uh, well, I usually do 10 in the morning." — JJ, 74CRg-mID5c ~19:34, L677) or 21 ("640 operations in 30 days, that's about 20, well, I'll say 21 a day." — JJ, AxP-cg50TdM ~29:48, L1013) vs 30 (Titans of Tomorrow clip title) vs ~1.2 qualifying (365-day backtest).
5. Account count: 20-30 vs 40 vs 45+; his own figures in the corpus are "40 accounts at a time" or "30 accounts" (aCOgfvL6lK8 L2308), 20 Apex (5RzMu2B2E_0 L118), "the 10 best" firms (PN1UKQMPb5M L716) and "20 trusted firms" (S8fSNbPFuLA L438), never a per-firm split.
6. Payout totals: the figures rise with time and the corpus pins them to dates: $1.2M (2026-03-28), $1.3M (04-12 to 06-01), $1.5M (06-06 to 07-11), ~$1.65M (07-13), $1.7M (07-27), $1.828M (08-23), $1.85M (09-06), $1.9M (09-13, 09-17), $1,931,000 (09-20, 09-22), $2M in the week of 09-21 (upload 10-04). Batch-recorded educational videos repeat older figures ($1.5M on 07-30 and 08-06, $1.6M on 08-13 to 08-30), and 4IGbxmKJ4BU says $1.6M aloud with $1.8M in its description.
7. fxreplay statistics: 158 / 54% / PF 1.76 vs 150 / 52% / PF 1.66 (different passes of one study).
8. Firm naming: resolved. "Funded Engineer" in the third-party summary is the captions' "TradiFi", i.e. Tradeify ("TradiFi, $180,000 in withdrawals with them", KN7j6NXXAio ~0:43 L64; "I have $180,000 in payments with TradiFi", 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~0:18 L42; "Tradeify with 180K", dJdBnSBJlgQ ~2:22 L97); the $179,938 dashboard on jjwebinar.com matches that line.
9. The per-firm payout figures he lists never sum to the headline of the same video: ~$799k vs $1.2M (2026-03-28, counting the Topstep 1099), ~$889k vs $1.3M (05-22), ~$1.21M vs $1.6M (07-25), ~$1.21M vs $1.5M (07-30), ~$1.24M vs $1.8M (08-27), ~$1.48M vs $1.931M (09-22). His explanations are smaller firms "not really worth showing here" (8lbkj0PF1uM ~0:37 L50) and live-account payouts paid directly to a bank; neither is itemised.
10. "Funded Engineer" was a mishearing of "TradiFi" (Tradeify); see item 8. Nothing in his breakdowns refers to the bankrupt firm.
11. Two of the podcast appearances in the original brief were wrong: the Captain Green podcast guest is a different JJ Simon, and the Titans of Tomorrow video and "The Genius Who Outsmarted The Prop Firm Game" are one episode.
12. Evaluation sizing: $1,000 per trade on a $2,000-drawdown 50k evaluation is half the drawdown, so two losses bust it; his "higher pass rate" claim and the two-trade evaluation model (Section 6) assume this, his stated evaluation risk is "about 500 to $2,000 per trade, profit target about 1,000 to 1,500 per trade" (wsKx7L9EJys L55), demonstrated as "risk 500, go for 750 every single trade" per NQ contract on 25/38 (MVP7X-3v8xk L52; KHEQ5g55dQ4 L2147), with half the remaining drawdown at 1R on the final evaluation day (VY-bO35-tjE L201, L238); so the $1,000-per-trade evaluation figure in the models is above his demonstrated unit.
13. Firm-rule source conflicts (prop-firm-rules angle, twelve items): Topstep's daily loss limit soft vs hard and its minimum days (none / 2 / 5); MFFU Pro consistency 50% (official) vs none (thetraderstack) and Rapid payouts daily vs every 5 winning days; MFFU Pro and Alpha Advanced pricing; E8 eval-to-eval copying allowed (official) vs prohibited (third party), its payout buffer (drawdown-sized vs 4%) and reset fee; Apex drawdown as dollar figures vs "5% EOD trailing"; Tradeify Select 50K target $2,500 vs $3,000. Section 9 cells that depend on an unresolved one: Topstep "$2,000 soft", MFFU Pro "50%", Apex "$3,000 trailing", "every firm allows copying between accounts you own".
14. Experience: not a contradiction. He dates the start to February 2025 ("started prop firm trading full-time in February of 2025. So, we're at exactly 16 months now.", C-_0QhKZmA4 ~0:59 L57, 2026-06-06); "18 months" is August 2026 ("It's been 18 months now", 4BXpI-hYqe0 ~17:54 L640) and "19 months" the $2M week (PN1UKQMPb5M ~16:07 L510). Only 3L8xdh3oPm4 wavers ("I started in March. Um March, I think February or March actually.", ~1:58 L97).
15. Income promise differs by platform: "scale to $10K+/month" (Instagram) vs "$30K+/month" (YouTube, TikTok).
16. An evaluation-vs-funded tweet in the evidence is dated before the account it refers to existed (profile angle).
17. "Verified payouts" is self-asserted: one firm certificate (E8, $222,122) was found; in the transcripts the proof offered is 1099s read aloud ("Topstep paid me 250k last year and then RiseWorks paid me 450k", 3L8xdh3oPm4 ~1:27 L80), refreshed firm dashboards, his Rise, Wise and bank accounts, Instagram posts, and from September 2026 only settled payouts ("I'm only going to show payouts that hit my bank account or hit the rise count", PN1UKQMPb5M ~8:13 L275); a third-party host says "it's not fully verified on our end" (SNO1wqJTq5A ~0:32 L85); every other figure is his own screenshot or restatement.
18. Resolved: Tradeify is in the $1.6M breakdown as the captions' "TradiFi, $180,000 in withdrawals with them" (KN7j6NXXAio ~0:43 L64), and at $180,000 in every later breakdown through 2026-09-22.
19. Routing: "one account at a time, one trade per account per day" (JJ) vs "trade copiers across dozens of smaller funded accounts" (third-party summary); he says "Sometimes I copy two trades together, but you shouldn't do copy trading until you're making around 20k a month" (KN7j6NXXAio L105); the threshold he states moves between $10,000 of bankroll (March, May 2026), 50k a month (June), 30K a month (July 30), 20-30 thousand a month (August 9) and 35k a month for copying two evaluations under $150 (September 24); in March he said "I'm copy trading five at once" (3L8xdh3oPm4 L305) and in September "have to copy trade" for 39 accounts in one reversion (HlWSP7ajgpQ L169), against "This is my copier if I ever use one, which I don't yet" on July 30 (AxP-cg50TdM L1105).
20. Entry quality: not a contradiction. He grades break of structure A+ and displacement A, enters every A (and some B+) because of the account count, and routes displacement entries to evaluations and breaks of structure to funded and live accounts (3L8xdh3oPm4 L784; KHEQ5g55dQ4 L265, L369, L940).

Contradictions found in the primary corpus (dated statements from the 58 verified transcripts, one line per item, merged where two groups report the same conflict; the group files are `docs/research/synthesis/*.md`; items that quote the dossier describe its text before the corrections in this revision were applied):
21. corpus: Scheduled news priced correctly vs incorrectly: in the same June 2026 interview he says news is "priced in pretty fairly" (aCOgfvL6lK8 ~15:40, L600) and a minute later that "on average the news was forecasted and priced incorrectly" (aCOgfvL6lK8 ~16:09, L616); the May news video sides with the first (8lbkj0PF1uM L89).

From the entries group: Continuation window: 5 minutes (KN7j6NXXAio L127, 74CRg-mID5c L1046, BLvsYJ4sqn8 L87, Jul-Sep 2026) vs 15 minutes (gjWiGBiVis0 L236, xTxrtFKlQfc L390, June 2026); the dossier credits 10-15 only to third parties.

What the affiliate relationship ('code JJ') covers and whether any firm sponsors content; no sponsor read by him appears in this group's facts. What 'the strategy does change monthtomonth' means in rules: the regime-specific entry models (ATH drift, dump, correction, stagnation) are never described. Whether the 20-point minimum excursion and the gap-fill filter stated for the 6 PM/8 PM sessions also govern the 9:30 session (one quote applies the 20-point test to 9:30), and how 'gap' is defined (prior session close to 6 PM open?). Whether the '38 point unfair move' is also the premium/discount gate for reversions or only the continuation target; he uses it only as a target in the corpus. Whether 'copy two evals together' (advice to a $50k/month student) or 'five at a time' (his own practice) is the operating rule, and at what bankroll the switch occurs. Which of the student results ($780k in 12 months; two at six figures; 10-15 at $30k; Henry $55k on $31k; Quinn $50k months) have any documentation beyond his and the students' statements; none is itemised in the corpus. Where the Connor '$210,000 in 45 days' and the '$0 to $250,000 in 90 days' testimonials come from (the linked video dInTrEm-utc is not in the transcript corpus). Whether he uses VWAP at all: a host observed it on his charts (SNO1wqJTq5A L407, medium) but he never mentions it in this group's facts. The commission and spread figures behind 'Nasdaq ... has at least the best commissions and and the spread' (no numbers given), needed for StrategyConfig.commission_per_contract_side. What the free playbook ('risk management system I use across 10+ firms') actually contains; it is description text only.

## 11. Sources

**Primary corpus.** 58 verbatim transcripts (296,678 words) of his channel
and the podcast episodes, plus the usable pages, collected on the GB10 and
pushed to the private repository `ThryveBaseline/jj-simon-sources` (commit
f638a1f, `jj-simon-replication/sources/`); the two interview transcripts
were hash-verified against the GB10's values (KHEQ5g55dQ4
17c0041e..., aCOgfvL6lK8 8abdeeb8...). Every transcript was read in full and
its statements extracted with verbatim, line-cited quotes into
`docs/research/extracted/`, machine-verified by `scripts/verify_extracted.py`
and listed in `docs/research/EXTRACTED.md`. Highest
value items for a replicator: KHEQ5g55dQ4 (Chart Fanatics, 21,988 words),
aCOgfvL6lK8 (Titans of Tomorrow, 16,917), MVP7X-3v8xk ("Watch Me Backtest
My $1,500,000 Trading Strategy", 9,091), 74CRg-mID5c ("How I Made $1.8M
Trading Prop Firms (Strategy Breakdown)", 8,864), AxP-cg50TdM ("$100,000
Per Month", 7,463), dJdBnSBJlgQ ("$550,000 on evaluations", 7,628),
KN7j6NXXAio ("$1.6M", 6,577), BLvsYJ4sqn8 (6 & 8PM session, 6,923),
GMDUiamqgig (6PM & 8PM on funded accounts, 2,681), sWJa8vRfPb8 ("Why I
Can't Show You My Risk Management", 5,148), and the Road to $1M / weekly
episodes 1-13. A local Qwen 35B model on that machine can extract the rules
from all of them in one pass once you allow the batch run.

Primary (JJ): his YouTube channel ([@itsjjsimon](https://www.youtube.com/@itsjjsimon)); [jjwebinar.com](https://jjwebinar.com/); [jj.jjsimontrades.com/schedule-call](https://jj.jjsimontrades.com/schedule-call); transcripts of "The Strategy Behind My $1.6M in Prop Firm Payouts", "Here's How You Can Make $100,000 Per Month On Prop Firms", "I Made $105,700 in 3 Weeks Day Trading NQ Futures (Road to $1M Ep. 2)" on [sozai.app](https://sozai.app/).
Podcasts: Chart Fanatics ([KHEQ5g55dQ4](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KHEQ5g55dQ4), 2026-10-04, [chartfanatics.com](https://www.chartfanatics.com/)); Titans of Tomorrow ([aCOgfvL6lK8](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=aCOgfvL6lK8), full episode 2026-06-22, and the clip [-lxNWJGWtbg](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-lxNWJGWtbg), 2026-09-18); Words of Rizdom (episode not indexed). Not him: PCDHJBdj-Z4 (Captain Green podcast, a different JJ Simon; video now unavailable). Rows in `EVIDENCE.md` and `sources.csv` that label -lxNWJGWtbg as Chart Fanatics are mislabelled.
Third-party tests and codifications: [fxreplay strategy page](https://fxreplay.com/strategies/jj-simons-fair-value-theory-nq-strategy) and [PDF](https://cdn.prod.website-files.com/668852f921e36c3365b91d03/69f277ffef6be0e125ad6a90_JJ%20Simon%20Fair%20value%20theory.pdf); [365-day backtest transcript](https://sozai.app/transcript/jj-simons-strategy-backtest-365-days/); [youtubesummary.com](https://youtubesummary.com/summary/SNO1wqJTq5A); TradingView [joetroyer](https://www.tradingview.com/script/j4BFY7JW-Fair-Price-Theory-NQ-Reversion-Continuation/) and [AndrewFXTD](https://www.tradingview.com/script/jfW4Vilk/).
Reviews: [allpros.io](https://allpros.io/course/jjs-mentorship).
